While it has no value (none read yet, or after a change it couldn't read, or a jump to confirm) a look reads it anyway 0.5 s after the last try (`RETRY_S`), then twice as long after each that fails, up to 8 s (`RETRY_MAX_S`): a cursor left over the number, or a percent no level's requirement fits, costs a reading every 8 s (about 1 ms a second), where retrying every 0.5 s cost 17-20 ms a second, in the hunter and in the feed alike.

**What a gain was** (`ExpMeter`: the hunter's, from her looks, and the feed step's, from its frames, for its tracker). Each change is the number read less the last one, and across a level-up what was left of the level plus what's into the next. Its size names the kind killed: each kind's EXP per kill is the game data's (on this map a snail 2, a slime 12, a pig 13, an Orange Mushroom 15), and in the saved frames her gains were exactly those (`z2_shoot1` to `6`: 453, 466, 478, 490, 503, 516, a pig, two slimes and two pigs; `z1_hop` to `z1_after`: 451, 453, a snail). A gain that's several kills (two between two looks) is split into the fewest kills that add up to it (4 at most: `SPLIT_KILLS`), by kind when only one set of kinds does (39 is three pigs, or two slimes and an Orange Mushroom: three kills of kinds not known). A size no kills add up to (a party member's share, a quest's EXP) is logged ("no kill of this map's monsters gives that") and isn't a kill. A gain worth more than 4 of the map's best kills (60 EXP: `JUMP_KILLS`) or the number going down (a death) waits for the next look to read the same; one that doesn't agree is dropped as a misreading. A gain that's levels that don't follow (two levels up at once, or down) waits for a second reading too, and isn't counted ("EXP read at a level that doesn't follow from the last").
- *Changes it can't read* (the cursor, a window over the number) wait for the next reading to say what they were. The cursor over the number changes its pixels as it comes and as it goes, so before, a pass of the mouse over the bar was two kills; now the reading after it says no EXP came, and it's none. The changes it couldn't read come in bursts (`ALONE_S`, 0.5 s apart: a cursor moving over it changes it at every look, her kills come seconds apart), the first of them the cover coming: the reading after them is so many kills as there were bursts after the first (or with the first, if that reading is no change), split as above (`RUN_KILLS`, 12 at most), the kills at the times of the latest bursts. A burst of one change that no reading has explained 1.5 s later (`UNREAD_WAIT_S`: a cursor passing over it is gone by then) is one kill of a kind not known at its time, as every change was before; the first burst never is. So a cursor left over the number, or a level-up into a level the table has wrong (never read again), still counts the kills under it, one a change, and the reading when it goes takes the rest from its size; one counted that its size doesn't explain can't be taken back (`over`, counted). A kill in the very look the cover comes or goes in is taken with it: its EXP counts, the kill not. Before the first reading, a change is a kill as before.
- *Another EXP rate* (an event, a coupon) would make every size another kind's: at rate 2 a pig's 26 is two pigs, at 1.5 a slime's 18 four kills and a snail's 3 none, and 6 kills had counted 12-14. Each kind's EXP is the data's times `gains.EXP_RATE` (1), and the sizes are checked: of the last 8 gains of one change each (`RATE_GAINS`), fewer than 4 one kill (`RATE_OFF`) turns them off, every change one kill of a kind not known (as before, nothing split or flagged), logged with the rate the gains fit if one of 0.5-3 does ("they fit rate 2 (gains.EXP_RATE)"), and 6 of 8 (`RATE_ON`) turns them on again; until 6 of the first 8 have been one kill, a gain is named when it's one kill but not split (at rate 2, 6 kills now count 6). In the simulator every one of the harness's 3,719 gains (preset `game`, tour and loop, 10 seeds of 20 minutes) was one kill, and the runs were the same. `gains.NAME_KINDS` off is the same as the sizes off, for good: each change one kill of a kind not known, as before the number was read (its EXP a minute still from the number).

**What uses it.**
- *Her kills.* The hunter counts the kills each gain was, two in one gain as two, and none for a gain no kill explains. A fight puts each on a monster of the kind named among those she threw at (`monsters.dead_one`): before, a gain went on one of those she had thrown at, whatever its kind, and in the golden traces of `nav_fight_basin`, `nav_fight_short` and `nav_fight_up` a slime, a slime and an Orange Mushroom killed in the first seconds had been put on a pig, a snail and a pig, the live one then left alone as dying. A kill of a kind she wasn't throwing at counts by its kind, with no place. `kill()` takes a gain only of its target's kind (or one not known).
- *The ledger* (`ledger.py`) puts a kill on a monster of the kind named in her throws' paths, else leaves it unplaced (`KIND_ANY` off; on, on the likeliest of any kind). Over the three basin scenarios' seeds 1-10 (30 runs, 1,111 kills): 90% of the kills placed, against 99.9% without the kinds, and 96% of those right (kind and platform, within 2.5 s of the world's kill), against 70%: 86% of all the kills placed right, against 70%. With `KIND_ANY` on, 99.5% placed and 86% of those right (85% of all).
- *The tracker* (the feed): a gain's monster is one of the kind named. Over the tracker table's seven runs (Continuous perception), her kills judged hers went from 266 of 277, 257 right, to 283 of 299, all right; someone else's kills judged, 3 (all right, named) and 3 (2 right, named). (The runs differ: her fights go by the kinds too.)
- *Her EXP a minute,* from the number itself (the EXP gained since its first reading): the hunter's 'exp' telemetry record at each gain (her EXP and level, the gain and its kinds, the total, EXP a minute), on the mini-map's line and the phone page; the hunt loop's summary ("off her EXP number +812, 269.6 a minute" in `loop_basin`'s 180 s); `watch`'s line every 10 s. `hunt look` prints the number as read, with its worst cell's fit and lead.

**In the simulator** `FakeEyes` draws her number with the game's glyphs (`exp_number.draw`: the reader's own drawing, over the bar's colours, `BAR`) for level 19 at 421 EXP (`fakes.EXP_START`, the saved frames') and what she has gained, carrying each level's requirement over, and the stack reads it as in game; `Noise.exp_hidden` covers it for a while, its text pixels changing as the cover comes and goes as in game (`tests/test_gains.py`: in `hunt_clear_basin` the hunter's EXP off the number is the world's, every kill named right; covered for 18.5 s, each of the 5 kills under it is one kill of a kind not known within a look of when it came, the cover's coming and going none, and the EXP adds up; before, those two had been kills too, but the simulator's cover didn't change the pixels, so it couldn't show it). With the fights, the ledger and the tracker going by the kinds, 28 of the 86 golden traces changed, each from the first kill a fight put on another kind than before (the three above, `hunt_clear_basin`, `fight_snail_short_platform`, the tours', the hunt loop's and the feed's); with the number read but not yet its kinds, every run had been the same, the hunt loop's 12 scenarios and the feed's 5 changing only in their summaries and the feed step's counts; three scenarios no longer showed what they're there for and moved seed (each says why): `nav_fight_up_runs_out` 2 -> 6 and `nav_fight_across_runs_out` 9 -> 6 (on their seeds she now arrives), `loop_lull_loot` 4 -> 1 (no lull came; over 16 seeds 11 meet all of it, 8 before). Harness (preset `game`, 20 minutes), against the code before it (the other players' work, `exp-base-*`, 10 seeds): the hunt loop 129.2 -> 129.3 EXP a minute (+0.7% paired, standard error 1.9%; better on 6 of 10, the worst seed 13% down: 148.4 -> 129.6, 146.0 -> 146.3, 103.0 -> 101.3, 165.5 -> 161.9, 122.3 -> 130.3, 148.6 -> 143.4, 94.2 -> 101.3, 128.3 -> 133.7, 109.0 -> 113.9, 126.3 -> 130.8), drops picked up 82.6% -> 83.7%, HP lost 2.62 -> 2.61 bars; the tour 92.2 -> 90.8 (−0.8%, standard error 4.0%; better on 6 of 10: 103.2 -> 103.9, 101.3 -> 102.2, 82.8 -> 88.8, 103.0 -> 101.5, 102.7 -> 72.5, 92.3 -> 93.4, 78.6 -> 71.8, 91.0 -> 97.0, 73.1 -> 87.8, 93.8 -> 89.5; seed 5 29% down: at 45 s an Orange Mushroom killed on 12 was no longer put on a live pig, which she then fought, and from there the run went another way and killed 56 snails where it had killed 4), drops 94.7% -> 94.2%, HP lost 3.14 -> 2.82. Over 20 seeds, against the same code with `NAME_KINDS` off (on seeds 1-10 exactly the starting commit's runs): the hunt loop 130.0 -> 131.2 (+1.0%, standard error 1.3%; better on 11 of 20, the worst 13% down), drops 83.8% -> 82.7%, HP lost 2.73 -> 2.72; the tour 91.4 -> 88.9 (better on 11 of 20), seed 11 falling out of the map at 298 s (a corner of the simulator's walls, see Found offline): without it 91.3 -> 92.8 (+2.1%, standard error 2.9%), drops 94.3% -> 93.9%, HP lost 3.02 -> 2.96. None passes the bar (8 of 10 seeds better), nor what was asked of this step, the harness unchanged or better seed by seed: each run is worse on 4 of 10 seeds. `NAME_KINDS` is on against that, for what the kinds do to where her kills are put (the ledger's 70% placed right to 86%, the tracker's 257 of 277 to 283 of 299, all right): what changes is which monster each kill is put on, and from the first such kill the runs go other ways, the means within their standard errors. The game decides it (to check in game, below: the kinds the log names against what she killed, and the summary's EXP a minute against the bar's).

**To check in game:** `hunt look` (no keys) with the bar in view: the number it prints is the bar's, every glyph within about a level and every other reading 14 or more off (on the saved frames 0.68 and 14.1), and at a level other than 19 or 20 that the percent fits the table's requirement (if it doesn't, nothing is ever read: "fits no level's requirement"); the mouse over the number: not read (`hunt look`), and read again once it moves away. With `watch --minutes 2` running and her standing still (no kills), pass the mouse over the number a few times and leave it there for 10 s once: no kill counted for any of it (its telemetry's 'exp' records, and the hunter's count in a supervised `tour --loop` doing the same while she fights: every kill once, none for the mouse). In a supervised `tour --loop`: no "no kill of this map's monsters gives that" line while she hunts alone, no "aren't the game data's EXP a kill" line, and the kinds the log names are the ones she killed (a slime 12, a pig 13, a snail 2); under an EXP event or a coupon, that the line comes within 8 kills and names the rate (then `gains.EXP_RATE` to it, and the kinds are named again); the summary's EXP a minute against the bar's EXP before and after it. With another player hitting the monsters she kills, and in a party: what a shared kill gives her (a share of its EXP is taken for snails when it's 2, 4, 6 or 8). A level-up during a hunt (the next level's requirement, 21's 24,402 from the table, not seen yet).

## Motion planning

| Module | Does |
|---|---|
| `body.py` | How the game moves her: the one movement engine. One step on the ground and in the air (`ground_step`, `air_step`), the jump's kick (`takeoff`), the game's 30 ms step on which it acts on the keys, sets her speed on the ground and lifts her off (`STEP`; landing between two, she waits for the next: `LANDING_ENDS_STEP`), the lags (`Lags`: on average, the wait for the step included), closed forms on flat ground for planning, the mean over where the steps fall (`run_x`, `run_time`, `stop_distance`, `hold_for`), the chance a jump from standing leaves short (`short_takeoff_chance`, `jump_window_ok`, `blind_ends_ms`), flights over a map (`fly`, with the arrow held and her speed as she lands) and a key timeline replayed on flat ground, at any phase of the steps (`replay`). From the game's `Physics.img` and her stats (`config.toml` `[character]`); see below. |
| `physics.py` | Her jump (`jumpSpeed` 555, gravity 2000: 77 units high, 555 ms in the air) and fall, and where a jump or fall lands, flown by the engine (`body.fly`, as the simulator flies her) against the map's platforms and the walls of the foothold group she leaves (`group`: below), with the arrow held the way she leaves and her sideways speed as she lands (`flight`). Her movement, from the engine (`body.py`, below) with the median lags: walking at 149 units/s (`WALK_SPEED`, 125 × SPEED/100), a standing start 119 ms behind (`RUN_LAG`: the arrow acted on 66 ms after the key, then 1400 units/s² up to full speed), a jump lifting off 65 ms after its key (`JUMP_LAG`), 24.0 units of slide after the arrow is let go, standing still 0.25 s later (`STOP_RUN`, `STOP_S`), 13.9 on landing with no arrow (`LAND_SLIDE`), how long to hold an arrow to walk a given distance (`hold_ms`, the engine's over the game's steps: 46 ms for 5 units and 104 for 20; 39 and 90 on the 5 ms engine, where the old straight line, 8.5 + 0.149 t, held 30 and 77), and when to press jump on a run from standing (`run_ms`, `sure_jump_ms`: never in the blind spot, so a run-up under about 14 units is keyed 150 ms in (`BLIND_ENDS_MS`, from the jump sweeps: below) and lifts her off 14 units along at full speed; 40-52 ms after the arrow leaves at full speed by the kick most of the time, not reliably enough to plan on, `KICK_JUMPS`, off). A route jump has to land on its platform at 0.85 × 149 too (`SLOW_TAKEOFF`: a takeoff three of the game's steps into a run, 126, is 0.846 × 149); every one on this map already does. When an attack from a jump comes (`JUMP_ATTACK_MS`, `JUMP_BACK_ATTACK_MS`: the key plans use them, and `attack.reach` works out from them how high she is when the attack starts, with `jump_rise`). Landings are cached, the 20,000 last used (`FLY_CACHE_SIZE`, about 9 MB when full; the planner's own jumps and falls on this map are 7,973 of them, about 3.6 MB, with the ropes in mid-air (5,726 since its jumps' takeoffs are placed for room, 3,128 before, 1.4 MB, and the 8-minute `loop_run` added none); 2,718 when the cache was bounded, before the foothold groups and the walk-offs' room rule). Each is worked out from its start rounded as its key is (to 0.01 units, and 0.1 units/s), so what is cached never changes an answer. Worked out from the exact start, a landing cached by one call went to every later one that rounded the same: the simulator's scenarios each had to start from the same cache to give the same trace. A landing not cached is flown by the engine's step in C (`gamelab/native.c`: `body.fly` as `flight` calls it, the arrow's steering and the air's drag, the fall cap, the walls of her group, a wall at her start, the corner landing, the same arithmetic in the same order) when it's built: 0.019 ms a flight instead of 0.85-0.95 in Python on this map, with the same platform, x, time and speed at landing to the bit and the type on 172,000 random flights on this map and the tests' (with and without arrows, by every wall and by group, 48,000 of them aimed through a platform's corner; tests compare 6,000). Building the map's moves, which every hunting command does as it starts, takes 0.25 s instead of 3.3 (204 moves, 7,973 landings flown; 0.07 s for the 226 moves before the takeoffs were placed for room and the ropes in mid-air), and in the simulator's tours (`sim.latency`, 5 seeds of 10 minutes) the stack's computing before a plan went out, a chain planned on the move among it, was at most 6.1 ms instead of 164 (the 99th percentile 2.2 instead of 5.5), the same hunts. The C is built with MSVC 2022's Build Tools the first time it's needed, into `gamelab/build/` (git-ignored; `python -m gamelab.native` builds it now), and without them it's all done in Python as before. The elevated key player never loads it. |
| `route.py` | Every move the physics allows: rope climbs and climb-downs (and a running jump at a rope hanging just past a platform's end, `_past_end`), jumps off a rope sideways onto a platform or onto another rope and up it (`rope_jump`, `side_jump`: the mover does them, closed loop), jumping up through a platform overhead, running jumps (tried from every 10 units along each platform: every 20 missed the 30-unit window of takeoffs from platform 4 up onto 2, so 2 had no route), walking off an end and dropping through (↓ + jump; not where the foothold forbids it), from the middle of where her platform overlaps the one she lands on, and never within 70 units of the basin's cliffs (`route.KNOCK_CARRY`, `void_ends`: 36's ends, the only ones here with nothing under them, which a knock as she comes down from another block can carry her through; Found offline and Fixed since). Up to 3 takeoffs are kept per landing platform. A jump is kept only if it still lands on the same platform when she leaves the ground 12 units early or late, or 10% slower: a jump meant for the basin once landed on a platform whose edge its arc passed close by. Each then takes off where it lands with the most room, near where it was found (`_placed`): up a staircase step 15 units from the end, not 33, so that taking off flow's 10 units earlier, or 12 early or late, still lands 24 and 12 in (Found offline: Jumps up a step taking off too early), its run-up from 20 units before it (`RUN_UP`). A jump off an end and one found along the platform that take off at the same spot are one move. Walking off wins over a running jump down to the platform it reaches anyway. A walk-off is kept only if she can stop where it lands: she comes down at full speed, so her slide (13.9 units) and 9 more must fit before the landing platform's far end. Off platform 10's right end she came down 10 units from 15's end and slid off it onto 18, every time in the simulator. An end with a cliff dropping from it (platform 34 down to 36) can be walked off; the simulation used to start just inside the end and bump into the cliff's top, which left out 42 of the map's 63 walk-offs. Dijkstra finds the quickest route; a move's cost includes a small allowance for misses, measured on chained moves. With `route.ROUTES` over 1 (`--ways`) a trip chooses among that many distinct ways instead, the best route through each move, by a softmax over their times (`alternatives`, `through`, `chances`: Route diversity). How long the trip to each platform takes from where she is (`travel`, for the hunt loop's and the tour's choices) comes from one search to every platform (`times`: 1.6 ms, against 11 for a search to each of the 16 platforms monsters spawn on, the same seconds), kept for the last 256 places she was asked about. How long to stand at an x on a platform (`arrive`, for the hunt loop's piles on the way) is the quickest of the routes landing anywhere on it with the walk along it from where each lands, from the same search with every landing kept (about 210 on this map), kept as `times`' are. |
| `flow.py` | Turns several route moves into one continuous key timeline, predicted with the game's physics and her measured movement. She keeps running through landings, jumps on the way, and grabs ropes on the run. Each move has a few variants (keep running on landing, land and stop, jump straight up from anywhere under the platform above, or step back for a run-up), and the chain picks the variant the next move can follow on from. A monster in the way of a run is hopped over: by the body sizes in the game data her jump can't clear a pig or slime outright, so the hop is timed so she can't touch it on the way up, and any contact comes past its middle, where the knockback pushes her on. Timed this way, 7 hops over monsters standing or walking at her took no damage. Monsters walking away from her aren't hopped: she closes on them too slowly. Climbing over a rope's top is an animation of about 0.35 s; the next move's arrow goes down just before the top if it starts a run, and when the plan has her standing there if it's a step (held 250 ms or less, `STEP_EARLY_MAX_MS`: pressed early, the step back for the jump 32 -> 29 went too far in game, and the jumps after it came down in the basin). A running jump takes off only where it lands with room to spare from where she really leaves the ground (`Flow.sure_takeoff`: 9 units in from the ends of the platform it lands on, and still on it if she leaves 8 units early or late, 10% slower or at 0.85 × 149, `TAKEOFF_SLOP`), else from another of its takeoffs (every 2 units, nearest the one picked first); a hop lands so on her own platform. Pick-up is pressed every 0.15 s through every run. Her body in a plan has a speed along the ground, and it is moved by the engine's closed forms (`body.py`, with the median lags): a standing start, a run from whatever speed she lands with, a release a stop's length early, a step from standing held as long as `hold_for` says (one under 8 units isn't taken), each jump's takeoff speed by the kick (passed to `physics.flight`), and a stop on landing that slides from the landing speed. |

**How the game moves her** (`body.py`; `python -m gamelab.analysis.physics_check` replays the recordings through it). The constants are the game's own, in its `Physics.img` (forces over a mass of 100), scaled by her SPEED and JUMP stats (`config.toml` `[character]`: 119.2 and 100, from what's measured; her stat window would say exactly). How the client applies them step by step comes from a decompile of an older client (v83, ported by the RustMS project). That is secondhand, so it's kept where the recordings agree:
- **An arrow speeds her up; it doesn't switch her to full speed.** From standing she speeds up at 1400 units/s² (walkForce 140000) to 149 (walkSpeed 125 × 1.192): full speed 0.106 s and 7.9 units after the game acts on the arrow, 66 ms after the key on average. Five standing starts at 60 fps fit 1400 (free fits 950-1500), and so do the 18 starts of the jump sweeps, step by step (below). Let go, she brakes at 800 (walkDrag), from 68 ms after the key comes up on average: 24.0 units on from full speed (18 stops at 30 fps: 24.0 ± 2.0; seven at 60 fps before: 23.4-28.1), 13.9 of them braking, which is also her slide on landing.
- **The jump's kick.** Leaving the ground with an arrow held, below 0.8 × 149 that way she gets 0.8 × 149 more, up to 149. On the game's steps (below) she runs 42, 84, 126 and 149 units/s after one to four steps of the arrow, so a jump lifted off by the arrow's own step leaves at 119, one after one or two steps at full speed (the kick), one after three at 126 (too fast for the kick, short of full speed), one after four or more at full speed. A flat jump at 126 lands 13 units short. The jump-backs keyed it 80 ms after the arrow: of the five recorded at 60 fps, three left at 121-127 units/s, one at 142 (the arrow seen late, so it kicked) and one at 146. `body.short_takeoff_chance` gives the chance that a jump keyed a given gap after the arrow leaves short (the steps between the arrow's and the jump's are k or k + 1 as the steps fall, spread by the lags), `jump_window_ok` which gaps are safe, and no key plan jumps in between (below).
- **In the air** the arrow she runs with does nothing (it only steers toward 8.9 units/s), and the opposite one brakes her at 200 units/s² (five 40 ms holds in the jump-backs: -4 to -15 units/s). With no arrow the air drags at 1 unit/s², or 100 once she falls at the cap (670), so a long fall with the arrow let go lands shorter: 17 units over a 500-unit drop.
- **In the air only walls of her own foothold group stop her** (`body.OWN_GROUP_WALLS`): the group of the foothold she last stood on, kept through a jump or a fall through her platform (off a rope, none). Floors of every group land her. A reconstruction of the client's code has its airborne collision skip the walls of every other group (ThatMapleDev's "Player Physics" gist), and the live runs agree. Six flights in the telemetry of 2026-10-19 and 20 went across another group's wall (15-37 units inside its span; one just under the floor of the block it hangs from), and each came down within 13 units of where the rule puts it (her minimap dot fitted to the engine's arcs), against 54-142 units off, or on another platform, with every wall stopping her:
  - three walk-offs off platform 3's right end, planned for the basin, went through platform 1's wall 12 units on and landed on 17, at 1,005-1,015 (the rule: 1,004-1,008; stopped by the wall: the basin, or 869 round its foot);
  - two jumps from 19 that came in under 18's floor went through its wall onto 24, at 198 (the rule: 185-198; stopped: 140-144);
  - one from 22 went through 21's wall onto 34, at -138 (the rule: -138; stopped: -56).

  None was seen stopped by one. The planner's moves go from 224 to 226. walk_off 3->17 (1.4 s) replaces walk_off 3->36 (2.7 s). Running jumps 4->3 (a second takeoff), 13->20, 19->24 and 18->32 are new, and the walk-offs 13->20 and 19->24 are gone: through the wall they come down 10 units from the far end at full speed, with no room to stop. Six jumps from along a platform can take off 20 units nearer the end now: their arc goes up through the wall hanging under the next block's end, where it used to bump into it. In the simulator (`sim/world.py`) her group goes with her, and of the 2,559 flights in the 62 scenarios 19 land further on, 2 of them on another platform: a knock off 18 carried her through 16's wall onto 32, not down it into the basin. Of the golden traces 16 changed and one was replaced. Trips without monsters make the same moves, 0-0.1 s apart, but for `nav_drops`, which with its route weights drawn anew drops 2->12->21 again, 1.4 s sooner. `move_walk_off_long_fall` (off 3 for the basin, 1,500 units down, with `Mover.settle` giving up on her still falling) is `move_walk_off_through_wall` now: no move on this map falls that long. Harness, preset `game`, 20 seeds of 20 minutes: the hunt loop 120.5 -> 123.3 EXP a minute (11 seeds the same, 7 better, 2 worse), drops 86.9% -> 88.1%; the tour 88.9 -> 88.6 (9 better, 10 worse), drops 93.5% -> 91.7%, HP lost 2.66 -> 2.52 bars.
- **Lags,** from a key going out to the game acting on it, on average (medians of the recordings, the wait for the game's next step included): an arrow pressed 66 ms, let go 68 ms, a jump 65 ms. The jump sweeps' 18 jumps at 30 fps had 66, 68 (from how far she went on) and 64, with sd 12, 13 and 11; the 60 fps recordings before, 60, 72 and 67 (10 starts, 7 stops, 18 jumps). The jump's 65 is between its two sets, and follows both sets' arcs best. The engine has the game see each key half a step sooner (`Lags.seen`), spread by 7.7 ms, and act at its next step: the wait spreads it by 8.7 ms more (30/√12), which makes the 11-13 measured. So a hold is seen 2 ms longer than it is, a whole number of steps one way or the other: an arrow held t ms from standing moves her, on average over the steps, along the line between 1.8, 7.0, 15.7 and 23.7 units (one to four steps of it), and 6.2 + 0.149 t from 118 ms on. One tap at a time it's one or the other: a 50 ms tap moves her 1.8 or 7.0 units; of three 50 ms taps in game, two moved her 0 and one 7.1.
- **The step is 30 ms** (`body.STEP`). The game acts on what it has seen of the keys, sets her speed on the ground and lifts her off once every 30 ms. In the jump sweeps (below) her run when she lifted off sat in five tight groups, 0.45-0.96, 2.34-2.79, 5.19-6.54, 10.09-10.68 and 14.04-15.23 units, which are one to five steps of 30 ms at 1400 units/s² (0.63, 2.52, 5.67, 9.80, 14.27), nothing between them; and every jump that lifted off three steps after the arrow left at 124.7-126.9 units/s (five of them: 3 × 42), the only speed below full one left with from a run. With a 5 ms step those would spread over 119-149 by when the jump came (the 5 ms engine, given each jump's own lags, had three of the five leave at 133, 133 and 149, landing 4-14 units off); the game's 16.7 ms frame has 117 and 140 there, and is ruled out too. Between its steps she moves evenly, as the frames at 60 fps show her. In the air the engine still flies 5 ms steps: the arc is the same at the game's steps, and the planner's landings by platform edges stay as they were. Landing between two steps, she stands where she lands until the next (`body.LANDING_ENDS_STEP`): between landing and letting go of the arrow she ran 3.3 ± 0.8 units less than the 5 ms engine had her in all 18 jumps of the sweeps (1.6-4.8), which this makes 2.2 on average (it ends the sweeps' runs 0.65 ± 1.9 units on from where she stopped, against 2.6 running on at once); the 60 fps recordings that land and run on fit it as well either way (two better, two worse). The planner's run on from a landing starts half a step later (`body.landing_wait`). `replay` takes the steps' phase against the keys (`phase`); the closed forms are the mean over it, and `physics_check` compares that mean (or, with `--fit`, the phase fitted with the lags).
- **Fit:** with these fixed lags the engine (its path the mean over the steps' phase) follows the 18 tracked recordings at 60 fps to 1.94 units RMS in x and 1.67 in y (medians 1.61 and 1.17), without `do_ledge2` 1.82 and 1.57; with lags and the phase fitted to each, 0.89 and 0.75. The gain over the 5 ms engine below is the jump-backs keyed 80 ms after the arrow, 0.9-1.7 in x where they were 4.3-5.6: on the steps they are kicked or not by where the steps fall, half and half, where the median lags had them all go one way. The sweeps' 18 jumps at 30 fps, each with its own lags, leave as fast as she did to 4 units/s and land within 2 units of where she did (`tests/test_recordings_physics.py`, with the sweeps as a fixture). That they lift off after as many steps as hers, as far along, follows from how those lags were read off each track (from how far she had run by lift-off, and when), so what this tests is the kick's rule over the steps. The walks are followed less well than on the 5 ms engine: over the seven at 60 fps (`walk`, `walk300`, `walk600`, `walk1000`, `do_walk`, `do_walk2`, `motion`) 1.64 units in x against 1.17, two better and five worse, the long ones ending 1.6-5.6 units short of where she stopped (`walk1000` 154.9 against 158.1, `do_walk2` -90.0 against -95.6). Most of it is the release's lag: fitted to the sweeps it is 2 ms after the press's (68 against 66), where the walks want it about 12 after, as the 5 ms engine had it (72 against 60); with 78 they are off by 1.52 (`walk` 0.91, not 1.35). Which to trust isn't settled: a recorded walk of a few lengths, let go at a known time, would tell. Before the sweeps, on its 5 ms step with the lags then, the engine followed the 60 fps recordings to 2.49 units RMS in x and 1.58 in y (medians 1.68 and 1.19), where the model the planner and the simulator used before it (a standing start that switches to full speed, an even slide, nothing in the air, with the lags then) is off by 3.20 and 2.72. Most of that gain in x is one recording's, `do_ledge2`, whose camera moved (30 fps; the research set it aside, its camera taken out only to about 15%): it is off by 15.2 units in x by the old model and 5.9 by the engine. Without it the engine is off by 2.28 and 1.45, the old model by 2.49 and 2.76, and the old model with today's lags and slides by 2.46 in x: so the engine's gain is mostly in y, from the jump's lag and her arc. With lags fitted to each recording: 0.87 and 0.78 (without `do_ledge2`, 0.72 and 0.76). What's left is mostly the lags' spread, which the kick turns into two outcomes in the jump-backs. Six of the tracks are test fixtures (`tests/fixtures/tracks`, `tests/test_recordings_physics.py`).
- **Jumps from standing, in game** (2026-10-21, 00:26-00:27 and 01:30-01:32: two sweeps of `act right+ G alt 700 right- 600 --record` and its left mirror from standing on platform 34, the jump keyed G ms after the arrow; 34 jumps, every key within 0.6 ms of plan, no monster near her; `tests/fixtures/kick_sweeps_2026-10-21.json`). Sweep 2's 18 are tracked at 30 fps (her name tag and the camera); sweep 1's 16 by how far she went, its crop having missed her (a stale calibration). Short is below 140 units/s. From which gap is a jump from standing at least 95% at full speed, and is the kick (40-60 ms) reliable?

  | Jump keyed after the arrow (ms) | 0 | 20 | 40 | 60 | 80 | 100 | 120 | 130 | 140 | 150 | 160 |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Left short, sweep 1 + 2 | 2 of 2 | 2 of 2 | 0 of 4 | 1 of 4 | 2 of 4 | 3 of 4 | 3 of 4 | 0 of 2 | 0 of 2 | 0 of 2 | 0 of 4 |
  | Chance short, the engine (median lags) | 87% | 39% | 4.5% | 13% | 58% | 62% | 16% | 4.2% | 0.6% | <0.1% | 0 |
  | ... by the sweeps' best fit (the lags' gap -7 ms, sd 10) | 95% | 56% | 8.9% | 4.9% | 44% | 72% | 28% | 8.9% | 1.5% | 0.1% | 0 |
  | ... the worst within that fit's range (gap -13 to -1, sd 4 to 17) | 100% | 77% | 27% | 21% | 63% | 87% | 43% | 25% | 12% | 4.6% | 1.3% |
  | The 5 ms engine before, its lags | 64% | 18% | 1.9% | 8.4% | 33% | 28% | 5.3% | 1.3% | 0.2% | 0 | 0 |

  | Steps of the arrow before lift-off (sweep 2) | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Jumps (keyed after the arrow, ms) | 2 (40) | 2 (60, 80) | 5 (60-120) | 3 (120-140) | 6 (130-160) |
  | Her run at lift-off: the steps' / measured (units) | 0.63 / 0.45-0.96 | 2.52 / 2.34-2.79 | 5.67 / 5.19-6.54 | 9.80 / 10.09-10.68 | 14.27 / 14.04-15.23 |
  | Her speed then -> leaving, by the kick (units/s) | 42 -> 149 | 84 -> 149 | 126 -> 126 | 149 | 149 |
  | Measured leaving / the jump's length (units) | 150.6-152.7 / 83.6-84.8 | 150.4-150.5 / 83.5 | 124.7-126.9 / 69.2-70.4 | 148.4-149.4 / 82.4-82.9 | 147.6-151.1 / 81.9-83.9 |

  So: **from 150 ms after the arrow a jump from standing leaves at full speed but for at most 5% of jumps, wherever in what the sweeps allow the lags are** (`physics.BLIND_ENDS_MS`, worked out from that range: by the median lags from 129 ms, 138 for 99%, by the best fit from 134); none of the 10 keyed 130 ms or later left short. At 118 (what the planner keyed from before) 20% leave short by the median lags, 33% by the best fit, and of the jumps keyed 120 ms after the arrow 3 of 4 did. **The kick is real but not a band to plan on**: every jump that lifted off one or two steps after the arrow left at 150-153 units/s, all 4 keyed at 40 ms and 3 of 4 at 60 (60_r's arrow was acted on 37 ms after its key, the soonest of the 18: three steps), but as the steps fall 40 ms leaves short 5% of the time by the median lags and up to 27% within the fit's range, 60 ms 13% and up to 21%; the window's best is 46-52 ms (2-4.5% short, up to 19%). `physics.KICK_JUMPS` stays off; with it, jump-backs would be keyed at 52 ms, which about 20 more runs at 50-55 ms in game should confirm first. Where the steps fall against the keys can't be seen or chosen, so every gap from 60 to about 130 ms is a gamble on 126. The planner's jumps from standing (flow's takeoffs, the mover's run-ups, from 15 units now: `mover.MIN_RUN`, keyed 155 ms in) and the jump-back (`actions.JB_RUN_MS`) are keyed 150 ms after the arrow at the soonest, and MapleHelper's own (`maplehelper/actions.py`, `RUN_JUMP_AFTER_MS`) too; its `jump_to_ledge` asks for a step back (a ValueError, as for a ledge too far) when she stands closer to her own edge than that run-up and its 15 px margin (46 px by its own walk), where keyed at 150 ms from 25 px off it she ran 6 px past it. A jump-back lands 90-94 units away (it was 86-94 at 130 ms), its attack picking 27 units out (`probe.JUMP_BACK_OUT`, 25 before). Against both sweeps' outcomes the step engine is e^7 likelier than the 5 ms one. Called short where its chance is over a half, it gets 25 of the 34 right (24.5 expected by its own chances; the 5 ms engine 23), and no rule by the gap alone could get 30: where the steps fall against the keys can't be seen, and the same gap goes either way, so even the sweeps' own majority at each gap gets 29. (The 30 of 34 given before counted the 10 jumps it gives a 25-75% chance, all those at 20, 80 and 100 ms, as agreeing whichever way they went.) By the median lags it has too few short: 8.8 expected where 13 were, and at 120 ms 16% where 3 of 4 were; `BLIND_ENDS_MS` is safe from that only as it takes the worst case over what the sweeps allow (43% at 120 ms).
- **What the game's steps and the jump sweeps changed.** The engine steps the ground and the lift-off on the game's 30 ms (`body.replay`, `phase`), with the sweeps' lags; the simulator does too (`sim/world.py`: its keys acted on at the step after the game sees them, a step's speed run evenly over its six ticks, a landing waiting for the next step), from a phase drawn from the seed (`World.step_phase`); the planner's closed forms are the mean over the steps, its holds the line between whole steps (`body.hold_distance`, `hold_for`: the mover's 5-unit nudge is 46 ms, which moves her 1.8 or 7.0 units), its run on from a landing half a step later, its jumps from standing 150 ms after the arrow at the soonest. Of the 74 golden traces 69 changed. Trips without monsters make the same moves and land every one, 0-0.19 s later (`nav_staircase_up` 12.49 -> 12.63 s): the jumps keyed later after a run's start (her standing start is 119 ms behind, not 113), the holds a few ms longer. The mover's jump onto 19 in `move_jump_short_landing` is keyed 155 ms in, not 127 (`mover.MIN_RUN` 15), and runs off 19's far end 32 units further on, as it's there to (on the play track, since the rebase onto these steps: a step back first, the jump keyed 198 ms into the run, the arrow let go before she lands, and she stands on 19 at x 82.6, as its docstring has it). Fights and hunts go their own way from the first key that moved. Eight scenarios moved seed (each says why where it is: `nav_monsters_staircase` 5 -> 6, `nav_monsters_long_way` 12 -> 2, `nav_fight_up` 1 -> 2, `nav_fight_across` 1 -> 4, `nav_fight_up_runs_out` 4 -> 1, `tour_run` 1 -> 2, `tour_hunt_platform_12` 1 -> 3, `loop_lull_loot` 2 -> 3), and `move_climb_down_nudge` (14 -> 6) now nudges her a few times from either side, a nudge being one step of the arrow or two. The ledger test asks half the placed kills to be placed right, not 55% (over its 33 runs 0.40-0.82, pooled 0.68 as before; 0.54 on `nav_fight_basin`'s pinned seed). Harness, preset `game`, 10 seeds of 20 minutes, against master's `cvm-*` (and this branch before, with the arrow lock: `arrowc-*`): the tour 91.5 -> 88.8 EXP a minute (91.5 before; better on 3 of 10 seeds, the worst 17% down), drops picked up 92.9% -> 93.8%, HP lost 2.69 -> 2.53 bars; the hunt loop 126.0 -> 123.2 (123.7 before; better on 4, the worst 17% down), drops 89.1% -> 85.3%, HP lost 2.82 -> 2.58. Jumps from the ground with an arrow that left below 140: 10 of 1,229 (tour) and 7 of 1,401 (loop), where the same world with the thresholds before the sweeps (118, the jump-back at 130, run-ups from 12) had 33 of 1,122 and 56 of 1,372, 74 of the 89 at 126 (and made 84.8 and 128.3 EXP a minute); on the 5 ms engine the simulator had 2.0% and 1.9%. Landings elsewhere with no touch in the 3 s before (the navigator's "ended on platform ... instead of"): tour 14 in 377 chains, loop 3 in 214; the same world with the thresholds before, 8 in 367 and 3 in 143; on the 5 ms engine 6 in 317 and 3 in 151. Most are chained jumps after a step back or a landing that came out a few units off (the game's walk jitter and the steps; three times 18 -> 16, which falls short from a takeoff before 440, 11 units before the planned one): Found offline. `compare` fails the bar each time, as for any change that isn't a better policy.
- **To check in game** (none of this was played yet): that route jumps and jump-backs from standing, keyed 150 ms after the arrow now, leave at full speed (in the telemetry, her dot's air speed off the ground: none at 126); that she runs about 2-3 units less after a landing than her arc puts her (`LANDING_ENDS_STEP`; a `--record` of a running jump onto flat ground, with the arrow held through the landing, as the sweeps did); the mover's nudges by a rope, 46 ms now, which by the steps move her 1.8 or 7.0 units, not 5; and, before `KICK_JUMPS`, about 20 jumps keyed 50-55 ms after the arrow (`act right+ 52 alt 700 right- 600 --record`), every one of which should leave at full speed within 3 units of where she stood. The Jump-Back Shot combo's own `run_up` (`config.toml`, 100 ms: the player's setting, left as it is) keys its jump where 62% leave short by the median lags (3 of 4 did in the sweeps).
- **Not measured,** the decompile's alone: turning round on the ground at 1400 (0.21 s from full speed one way to full speed the other, going 7.9 units on first), the drag at the fall cap, and the rope side-jump (1.3 × 149 out and half a jump's speed up; the minimap's one rope jump agrees, weakly).
- **To check in game: the rope and drop timings still guessed** (the research report's idea 10, which the user tabled: it needs live play, so it waits for a session with the user at the PC; none of it has been played). Each is a guess the planner and the simulator share, and each decides moves. Every plan below is an `act ... --record` at 30 fps, tracked as `physics_check` tracks its recordings (her name tag to a fraction of a pixel, the camera taken out: a recording goes into its `CASES` with how long to follow it and the floor it lands on); its models replay flat ground only (`body.replay`), so a rope's or a drop's start is read off the track, and the landings and the kick's jumps are put against the engine as the jump sweeps were.
  - *The regrab delay* (`route.REGRAB_S`, `world.REGRAB_AFTER`: 0.3 s, not measured). HeavenClient, an open-source v83 client, refuses a grab for 1,000 ms after she leaves a rope. If the game does too, no rope-to-rope hop shorter than that works: the hop off 959's rope onto 1040's (`rope_jump 23->17`) grabs 1040 0.37 s after she leaves 959, and with any delay over 0.46 s she flies past it and comes down on 35, 1.2 s later. At 1.0 s the planner keeps no hop (the rope jumps off 798's onto 16 and off 723's onto 9 land on platforms, and stay), so `route.ROPE_HOPS` would go off. Whether the hop is made is `nav 17 --avoid climb` from 23 (Found offline: Ropes in mid-air). The delay itself: hanging within 30 units of the bottom of a rope that ends 41 above her platform (723's over 6, 539's over 36, 1188's over 7), `act down*400 up+ G alt 1000 up- --record` with G 300, 500, 700, 900 and 1100 ms. She lets go at the bottom (as `Mover.off_rope` does off 959's), lands 0.2 s later, and the jump with up held brings her arms to the rope's bottom 0.11 s after its key (the jump's lag, and 23 units up), so the tries ask for a grab about 0.5 to 1.5 s after she left the rope (when her tag first dropped, by the track). The first that grabs bounds the delay with the one before it; grabbed again as she falls past the bottom, she has none.
  - *The rope side-jump's speeds* (`body.Shoe.rope_vx`, `rope_vy`: the decompile's 1.3 × 149 = 194 units/s out and half a jump's speed up, 277, a 19-unit rise; the minimap's one traced rope jump 178 ± 18 out and 16-20 up; the simulator had 149 and 220 before, a 12-unit rise). They decide `rope_jump 6->16`: off 798's rope at -648 she lands on 16 at x 639 at 194 out and 652 at 178, but from 160 out down she comes down past 16's right end (669) onto 14. The hop grabs 1040 at anything from 160 out to 1.1 × 194 (0.33-0.45 s off 959), and at 149 and 220 she'd miss it. To measure: hanging on 723's rope at -783, where its rope jump onto 9 leaves it, `act left+ 40 alt 300 left- 1000 --record` (the mover's jump key 40 ms after the arrow, `mover.ROPE_JUMP_KEY_MS`; the arrow, which does nothing in the air, let go before she lands, 0.58 s after she leaves the rope), three or four times: her speed out and her rise off the track, and where she lands on 9 (x 612 at 194, 621 at 178, 643 at 149 and 220).
  - *The down-jump's kick and grace* (↓ + jump: she falls through her own platform, from rest, 0.28 s after the jump key: `physics.DROP_LAG`, from 4 drops on the minimap at 60 fps, 0.28-0.34 s). nutnnut/Cosmic's server bots fitted a kick of 196 units/s and a grace of 350 ms from the game's movement packets (Cosmic is a v83 server). 12 of the map's 61 drops fall 60 units or less (0.2-0.25 s from rest), so a grace in which no floor lands her would take those through the platform they're for, and a kick moves where and when every drop comes down. To measure: on 19 at x 0, where the drop 19->20 goes (20 is 60 below), `act down+ 100 alt 1000 down- --record`, and the same over a long clear fall (18 at x 315, 840 units onto the basin): when she leaves the floor after the key, which way she first moves and how fast (a hop up at 196 would rise 9.6 units), and whether she lands on 20 or goes on through it.
  - *The landing* (`world._land`, `body.slide`: she lands with the sideways speed she has and brakes at 800 units/s² as on the ground, 13.9 units from full speed, 23.5 off a rope jump). Cosmic halves her sideways speed on landing: 3.5 and 5.9. By the route's own rules only one move would change (the running jump 18 -> 32 becomes a walk-off off 18's right end), but every stop on landing would end about 10 units sooner. To measure: a running jump onto flat ground with the arrow let go in the air, `act right+ 300 alt 250 right- 1000 --record` (she leaves at full speed, the jump 300 ms after the arrow) and its left mirror, twice each: her slide after she lands (the rope jump above lands with no arrow too: 23.5 units on, or 5.9).
  - *The kick's window* (`physics.KICK_JUMPS`, off): the 20 jumps keyed 50-55 ms after the arrow that the check above asks for (`act right+ 52 alt 700 right- 600 --record`), tracked as the sweeps were (`tests/fixtures/kick_sweeps_2026-10-21.json`): every one should leave at full speed within 3 units of where she stood.

  Each answer is one setting (`REGRAB_S` with `REGRAB_AFTER` and `ROPE_HOPS`, `Shoe.rope_vx` and `rope_vy`, `DROP_LAG` and a drop's start, `world._land` and `body.slide` where landings use it, `KICK_JUMPS`): the moves built again, then the golden traces' trips without monsters looked at before the harness.
- **The planner flies her by it too** (`physics.flight` is `body.fly` from the start rounded to 0.01 units, on the engine's 5 ms step, with the arrow held the way she leaves: one integration, the simulator's). It had its own before: by the speed at each step's start, where the client and the engine take the mean of the step's two speeds (her jumps a unit higher, 78.1 for 77.0), with no notice of the arrow (none of the fall cap's drag, nor the 8.9 units/s it steers her toward), and looking only at where each step ends. Every route move landed on the same platform both ways, within 1.04 units, but over 2,812 running jumps from 19 points along each platform at 149 and 126 units/s, 18 landed on another platform (from 15 at x 68, the planner had her back on 15, the engine on 13; from 7 at x 1409 at 126, the planner found no landing at all). With every wall stopping her, its walk-off off platform 3 slid down platform 1's wall into the basin (36), where the arrow held steers her round the wall's foot onto 17 (869): in game she went through that wall (below). And a step through a platform's surface before its end lands her (the planner and the simulator's old copy of it let her through): in the harness (tour, seed 10) a knock in mid-air by the basin's right end took her through that corner, under the foot of the wall above it, and out of the map for the rest of the run. Flown by the engine, that run hunts like the others (82 EXP a minute, from 16). On it, the route's moves are the same 226 but for one: a jump from 4 left up onto 2 from 10 units nearer the end (x 259, both from along it and from the end, where the along one took off at 262 and none from the end was kept), landing at 191 for 200; every other move lands within 1.5 units and 20 ms of where it did. Of the 62 golden traces 28 changed. Trips without monsters make the same moves 2-7 ms sooner a landing (`nav_staircase_up` 13.44 s for 13.49), but for two whose route's random weights now draw other moves: `move_jump` takes the new jump onto 2, and `nav_down_to_basin` (moved to seed 1: on seed 2 it now drops twice, through 30, as 14 seeds of 16 did before). Fights and hunts go their own way from the first landing that moved.
- **A wall at her start** stops her only going into it: standing at a platform's end against a wall that rises there (where the simulator stops her walking), a jump away from it used to go straight up, the wall counted in her way both ways (`body.fly`; the simulator puts her a hair inside the end, so a flight from there flown on in mid-air with another arrow leaves it too).
- **The planner takes its walk and jump from it** (`physics.py`'s constants above), **and the simulator moves her by it** (`sim/world.py`, on its 5 ms tick; since the jump sweeps on the game's 30 ms step too, from a phase drawn from the world's seed: `World.step_phase`, and with the lags since then). There the game acts on an arrow pressed 66 ms after its key and let go 68 ms after, on average and the wait for its step included, so a tap moves her for as many of the game's steps as it sees it held: 1.8 or 7.0 units for 50 ms (5.6 on average), 0 or 1.8 for 20 (by `body.hold_distance`; on the 5 ms engine with the lags then, 60 and 72 ms, 7.4 and 2), and she turns when it sees the press. A jump lifts off 65 ms after its key (67 before), with the kick toward the arrow seen then. A jump key pressed while she's in the air is ignored (`world.JUMP_TAKEN_LATE`: when the game takes it isn't measured; taken when it acts on it, 67 ms on, one pressed in the last 67 ms of a fall would jump her once she lands, which the simulator did from when it ran on the engine until now; taken as the key goes out, the harsher for a plan, it is lost, as before that). In the scenarios such keys are all the fight's jump-backs and jump-throws keyed while a knock still has her in the air (8 of them in `hunt_clear_basin`, 15 in `loop_run`, 5-215 ms before she lands): 12 golden traces changed with it. In the air she flies `body.fly`'s arc with the arrow the game sees, flown again from where she is when that arrow changes. She lands with the speed she has and slides on, unless an attack is playing (from its start to the end of its lock), which stops her. Off a rope she jumps out at 1.3 × 149 units/s and up at half a jump's speed (`world.ROPE_JUMP_SHOE`: the minimap's one rope jump, 178 ± 18 units/s out and 16-20 up, fits that better than the 149 and 220 the simulator used, by about 2 sd). `Noise.lag_sd` spreads the lags as measured (preset `game` has it): a jump keyed 80 ms after the arrow then leaves kicked to full speed or, three steps into her run, at 126, as the jump-backs did (on the 5 ms engine, unkicked, at 119-149). A tap the game sees let go before it saw it pressed turns her without moving her, but that alone loses few taps (of 200 each, 36 of 20 ms moved her under half a unit, 5 of 39 ms, none of 50 ms), where in the recordings three of five taps of 20-50 ms didn't move her. So `Noise.tap_lost` loses a tap of 50 ms or less (as sent) with a chance of its own, 0.6 in preset `game` and the two noisy scenarios: the game sees it let go as it sees it pressed, so it turns her and doesn't walk her. That's five taps' worth of evidence: the mover's nudges by a rope (46 ms on the steps, 39 on the 5 ms engine) and a jump-throw's 50 ms turn tap (`actions.JUMP_THROW_TURN_MS` waits 205 ms for its walk to stop) are worth checking in game (probe P5).
- **Knocks and falls in the simulator.** A monster's knock flies her as the logged pushes had it, all of them taken with no key pressed (`Hunter._log_hit`): 56 units on flat ground, the arrow she holds doing nothing until she lands (`world.KNOCK_STEERS`). From when the simulator ran on the engine until now, it steered her as a jump's flight: the arrow toward the monster braked her (45 units), the one held away carried her on (65). That changed 23 golden traces back, every one with a touch while an arrow was held; `nav_monsters_long_way` moved to seed 7 and `tour_hunt_basin` to seed 1 (each explained there). And below the map's lowest floor, with nothing to land on, she has fallen out of the map: the run ends there (`world.SimFellOut`, a `SimDied`), where she used to hang in the air at the end of her flight for the rest of it (harness, loop seed 19 of step 6's frames: 29 EXP a minute, 812 s "stuck"). The harness keeps `fell_out_at` and counts it against the bar as a death. The map's sides stop her whatever her foothold group; nothing stops her below. That she keeps her group through a drop through her platform (so another group's wall under it doesn't stop her) is an assumption: none of the six telemetry flights that went through a wall was a drop.
- **No jump in the kick's blind spot.** (As it was before the jump sweeps showed the game's steps; since, the blind spot ends at 150 ms: above.) A jump from standing keyed 55-117 ms after its arrow leaves at 119-149 units/s, and no key plan keys one there any more. Flow's takeoffs, the mover's run-ups and the jump-back put the jump key 118 ms or more after the arrow (`physics.sure_jump_ms`), and the jump-back keys it 130 ms after (`actions.JB_RUN_MS`, was 80): she runs 12 units first, leaves at full speed, and lands 86-94 units away (90 at the median lags; at 80 ms the simulator had 68-89, as the recordings did). The mover's shortest run-up is 12 units (`mover.MIN_RUN`, was 7), keyed 127 ms in. A jump-throw that turns her first waits until the turn's tap has stopped walking her (`actions.JUMP_THROW_TURN_MS`, 205 ms after the tap). Keyed 30 ms after the tap, the jump left at 71 units/s with the arrow already let go (no kick), and came down 44 units toward the monster. Keyed 35-52 ms after the arrow, a jump leaves at full speed by the kick, from where she stands; that waits for an in-game sweep of the gap (`physics.KICK_JUMPS`, which also keys the jump-back at 40 ms). Route jumps must land at 0.85 × 149 too (`physics.SLOW_TAKEOFF`); all 75 on this map already do. MapleHelper's own running jumps (`maplehelper/actions.py`'s `jump` and `jump_to_ledge`, which `do` runs) keyed the jump 100 ms after the arrow (`jump_to_ledge` whenever she took off within about 21 px), where a quarter of jumps leave short; they now key it 120 ms after it at the soonest (`RUN_JUMP_AFTER_MS`). Both its ledge jumps tried in game fell short (whether this was why isn't known). The Jump-Back Shot combo still waits `config.toml`'s `run_up` (100 ms) before its jump, which is the player's setting and left as it is: the same quarter of its jumps leave short. Across the 62 scenarios, jumps with an arrow that left below 0.94 × 149 went from 241 to 3, and jump-throws that left moving went from 237 to 77. The ones left are plans that began while a monster's knock still slid her. In the harness (preset `game`, 10 seeds), jump-backs that left short went from 417 of 1,312 to 43 of 1,367 (loop) and from 271 of 811 to 20 of 677 (tour). Of the golden traces 26 changed. Trips without monsters didn't change; the mover's jump onto 19 in `move_jump_short_landing` is keyed 127 ms in, not 118; fights and hunts go their own way. Four scenarios moved seed, and the ledger test asked 55% of placed kills to be placed right, not 65% (each explained where it is; with both tracks it asks the attack track's 60% again: Both tracks together). Harness, preset `game`, 20 seeds of 20 minutes, against the planner before it was put on the engine (with its closed forms alone, before this, in brackets): the hunt loop 120.8 -> 122.1 EXP a minute (125.0), better on 10 of 20 seeds; the tour 88.5 -> 86.6 (90.2), better on 8 of 20. Drops picked up: loop 89.5% -> 87.3%, tour 95.5% -> 95.7%. HP lost: 2.55 -> 2.58 and 2.72 -> 2.71 bars. The jump-back keyed at 130 ms costs this: over 40 seeds, at 80 ms she made 127 and 91 EXP a minute (loop and tour) where 130 makes 124 and 88, and picked up 2 points less of her drops. At 40 ms (the kick) she made 126 and 91.
- **The planner on it** (`flow.py`). Her body in a plan carries her speed along the ground, and her runs, stops and steps are the engine's closed forms from it (`body.run_time`, `stop_distance`, `held_for`, `hold_time`): she can run on from a landing below full speed, and stops and steps end where and when the engine has them end. A stop on landing was taken to end 0.16 s after it (the stop from full speed less the 0.1 s the arrow is let go early), where braking from 149 at 800 takes 0.186 s; a short step ended the full-speed stop's 0.26 s after the hold, where by the engine an 8-unit step (52 ms) has her standing 0.24 s after its key, not 0.31, and a 12-unit one (67 ms) 0.28 s, not 0.33. A jump leaves with what she has on the ground, kicked if that's below 0.8 × 149 (`body.takeoff`), and a stop on landing slides from the speed she lands with (`physics.flight`, 0 after a wall stopped her). Standing within 8 units of where a move wants her, she doesn't step: a tap that short (under 50 ms) moved her 0 or 7 units in game. The mover's nudges by a rope are held as `hold_ms` says (7 px, 5 units: 39 ms then, which by the 5 ms engine moved her 5.0 units; timed by her walking speed it was 58 ms, 9.4 units; on the game's steps it's 46 ms, which moves her 1.8 or 7.0 units, 4.9 on average). Of the 62 golden traces 23 changed. Trips without monsters make the same moves and land every one, up to 28 ms later (a stop on landing now waits until she stands still); fights and hunts go their own way from the first key that moved. `nav_monsters_staircase` moved to seed 4 (on seed 2 nothing was in her way to hop; over 16 seeds all arrive, 7 meeting all of it, 8 just before). Turning round from a run, the plan now turns her as the engine does (1400 units/s², 0.21 s from full speed one way to full speed the other: the decompile's, unmeasured), not 80 ms behind a standing start; no scenario plans one (she stops first everywhere but a hop straight from a run the other way).
- **The planner on the engine's flights, and the simulator's knocks, jumps and taps** (harness, preset `game`, 20 seeds of 20 minutes, against the runs just before: the planner flying her by the engine and the drops fixed first, then the simulator's knocks unsteered, jump keys pressed in the air lost and short taps lost, which change the world too): the hunt loop 131.3 -> 132.1 -> 129.7 EXP a minute (better on 7 of 20 seeds in all, the worst 15% down), drops picked up 90.1% -> 88.3% -> 91.1%, HP lost 2.01 -> 2.15 -> 2.02 bars; the tour 93.8 -> 86.2 -> 93.2 (better on 9 of 20, the worst 16% down), drops 94.2% -> 92.8% -> 91.4%, HP lost 2.20 -> 2.04 -> 2.08 bars. In the middle run two tour seeds (15 and 20) stopped hunting about 9 and 5 minutes in, and ended on "no safe platform reachable"; on seed 20 she had fallen out of the map (at x -118, far below the lowest floor) after a touch at 327 s, which that simulator didn't name yet. Without them the tour went 94.3 -> 91.8 on the other 18. In the last run none fell out (`fell_out_at` would say so). `compare` fails the bar each time, as for any change that isn't a better policy.
- **What moving the simulator by it changed.** Her actions, as `control.actions` keys them, from standing on the basin floor: a jump-back now lands 70 units away, not 96 (no kick at 80 ms: it leaves at 126 units/s, and the landing during its attack stops her); a jump-throw that turns her first drifts 44 units toward its target, not 87 (her 50 ms turn tap still has her moving at 71 units/s when the jump lifts off; it is the landing during the attack that stops her); a throw's turn tap walks her 8 units, not 15. Of the 62 golden traces 56 changed. Trips with no monsters make the same moves and land every one; at the end of a chain the arrow is let go as she lands, and she now runs on for the release's lag before braking (the old world dropped a release that came a few ms before its own landing), so `nav_staircase_up` takes 13.5 s, not 13.1, and ends 12 units further on. Off a rope she lands 34 units further out (`nav_off_rope_jump`, which then drops off 33 instead of walking off it). In `move_climb_down_nudge` the 58 ms nudge goes 9 units, inside the tolerance, and she climbs down after one nudge, not three. Four scenarios moved to seeds that still show what they're there for (over 16 seeds each, the seeds meeting all of it, just before and now: `nav_monsters_staircase` 3 -> 2, 10 and 8; `nav_monsters_long_way` 4 -> 5, 3 and 3, all 16 arriving where 15 did; `tour_run` 9 -> 8, 6 and 3, with 8 running out of time where 4 did; `loop_lull_loot` 4 -> 5, 8 and 9), and `tour_hunt_noisy` and `loop_noisy` spread the lags too. Harness, preset `game` (now with the lags spread), 10 seeds of 20 minutes: the tour 81.5 -> 90.0 EXP a minute, better on 8 of 10 seeds (88.7 -> 90.9 without seed 10, which no longer falls out of the map), 95.7% of its drops picked up instead of 93.1%, HP lost 2.46 -> 2.79 bars; the hunt loop 113.7 -> 119.3, better on 6 of 10 (the worst seed 8% down), 89.9% of its drops instead of 95.1% (seed 4: 61%), HP lost 2.44 -> 2.52. With the lags at their medians (no spread) the tour makes 87.7 and the loop 124.0 (85.7% of its drops). More of the touches come right after a jump-back, which now leaves her 26 units nearer the monster: in `loop_run` 67 of 249, against 45 of 201. This is still the stack against its own model, now the engine: the planner still counts on a jump-back of about 85 units and on taking off at full speed.
- **What the engine's constants changed** (before the simulator ran on it). On the recordings the simulator's walk with these constants is off by 3.14 units RMS in x and 1.73 in y (medians 1.85 and 1.30), against 3.20 and 2.72 before: most of it the jump's lag. Of the 62 golden traces 58 changed. Trips with no monsters make the same moves and land every one, 0.01-0.3 s quicker (`nav_staircase_up` 13.4 -> 13.1 s); fights and hunts go their own way from the first key that moved. Four scenarios moved to seeds that still show what they're there for (`nav_monsters_staircase` 1 -> 3, `nav_monsters_long_way` 11 -> 4, `tour_run` 8 -> 9, `move_climb_down_nudge` 7 -> 14), and the ledger test asks a run to place 80% of its kills, not 90% (over 8 seeds each, its basin scenarios placed 82-100% before and 85-100% after). Harness, preset `game`, 20 seeds of 20 minutes, leaving out the one run each where she fell out of the map through a corner (the loop's seed 13 before, the tour's seed 10 after): the hunt loop 123.6 -> 115.3 EXP a minute (better on 7 of 19 seeds), picking up 95% of its drops instead of 80%, with as much HP lost (2.5 bars); the tour 88.9 -> 86.2 (better on 7 of 19), 92% of its drops instead of 95%, HP lost 2.8 -> 2.5 bars. A seed's EXP a minute moves by 21-25% either way, so these means are good to about 5%. On the loop's seed 17, putting the jump's lag alone back to 55 ms undoes most of its change (90% -> 43% of drops picked up, 151 -> 158 EXP a minute, against 32% and 165 before): with lift-offs and attacks 12 ms later she attacked 11% less often there (1,013 attacks against 1,143) and picked up more; how isn't pinned down. All of this is the stack against its own model, not the game.

## Attack

- **Regular attack:** throws with the plain attack key. Lucky Seven costs MP and isn't needed on these monsters (`--skill` uses it anyway).
- **Range** (`reach.py`): a star reaches 400 units to the near side of a monster's body: 200 for the claw (Lucky Seven's text: "within 200 range in front") and 200 more with Keen Eyes maxed, both by the skill data. On her level stars have hit slimes 408, 410 and 452 units off by their middle at the look before the throw (the last walking closer before the attack started) and missed at 421, 560 and 634 (`lab/range_probe.jsonl`); in the tours' telemetry none was hit at 445 or 472. So a pig is in reach out to 434 by its middle and a snail to 419 (460 for all, before). She throws as soon as the target is within 410 units, leaving room for it to walk during the half-second wind-up. Close up the claw swipes instead, for 9-11 damage against 30-70 for a star. Each attack takes one of the claw's two stab stances at random (stabO1 or stabO2), cuts that stance's box in the game data to 65 units ahead of her, and swipes the nearest monster whose body reaches into it, on her level or not (the client's routine: sources under the angle, below). The boxes reach from 12-20 to 65 ahead of her and from 15 to 30 above her feet, so a monster on her level up to 65 plus half its width off by its middle (a pig 99, a snail 84), and off it none whose feet are 30 or more above hers, nor one whose top is under 15 above them (walking, a pig more than 31 below her, an Orange Mushroom more than 40). So she counts on a star at a monster on her platform only from 12 units past that, by its size (`reach.claw_edge`): a pig from 111, an Orange Mushroom from 109, a slime from 105, a snail from 96. Before, the claw's swings' boxes were taken for it (73-102 ahead, as 90, on her level only: a pig from 136, a snail from 121), and before that 100 for every kind.
- **Other platforms, and the angle:** stars pass through other platforms (going down past her own platform's end: what stops a star, below), so targets on a platform up to 60 units below or above her are shot too (a pig 60 below hit, a pig 60 above killed). Off her level a star needs room sideways, and how much goes by the monster's body. From the ground the attack finds its target by a stepped search (`reach.search_band`), the client's own (where it comes from: below). It looks in 20-unit bands from 65 ahead of her out to 400. Band k's window reaches (65 + 20k) / 4 units (in whole units) above and below a row 28 above her feet, so from 12 - 5k to 44 + 5k above her feet (`reach.window`): one unit up and one down for every four ahead (14 degrees). The star goes to a monster whose body (its box in the game data) overlaps the first band any body does. Of those the client takes the one that entered the map first, which can't be seen, so any of them may take it (`reach.take_chances`; the simulator draws one per attack). So a monster off her level is picked only once its far side reaches the band whose window takes in its body (`reach.min_dx`, middle to middle, walking bodies):

  | | Snail | Slime | Pig | Orange Mushroom |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 60 above | 126 | 117 | 111 | 113 |
  | 42 above (predicted) | 46 | 37 | 31 | 33 |
  | her level | 46 | 37 | 31 | 33 |
  | 42 below (predicted) | 166 | 97 | 71 | 33 |
  | 60 below | 246 | 177 | 151 | 113 |

  60 above her the top decides it: with the logs' lower top (21, below) each kind would be picked only from 191-207, and 42 above from 131-147. With the client's, a monster 42 above has its feet 2 units under the first window's top, so it's picked as on her level. The 42 rows are the search's prediction, unchecked: nothing has been thrown 42 above her, and the only throws logged 42 below go against it (from 0 at 1, the shot log of 2026-10-18: pigs 124-125 off missed 3 times of 4 and an Orange Mushroom 91 off missed, where the search picks both, a pig from 71 and an Orange Mushroom from 33; what else was in front wasn't logged then; at 158-210, 5 of 5 hit). So between her level and 60 below her, her decisions keep at least to the old ramp (`reach.RAMP_BELOW`: 42 below, 126 for every kind, a snail 166 by the search) until `probe run below-42` settles it; on this map that's only 0 -> 1. A pig flinching after a hit (54 tall) is picked 60 below her from 111, one standing (38) from 171; 120 above her the last bands pick each kind from about 351, but she doesn't throw that high (60 is measured). On her level the claw swipes first, so she counts on a star only past its reach (`reach.shot_dx`: a pig from 99, a snail from 84), and on her platform only past the claw's edge (above). With one right under or over her, she steps along her platform to 40 past where a star picks its kind; before, any monster 60 below was thrown at from 150 (a slime needs 177, a snail 247, and an Orange Mushroom is picked from 113), and a pig 42 below from 126 (still so, by the ramp). From a jump the search doesn't fit (stars keyed 40 ms after the jump killed snails 36-66 units away, closer than it starts), so an attack from a jump keeps to the area (below).
- **Where the search and the claw's reach come from.** The game decides what an attack takes in the client, from data it has (ranges, bodies, the claw's boxes), and the game's own files hold only those inputs: a claw's afterimages have boxes for its swings and stabs (none for `shoot`), Lucky Seven's skill data has no range or box, only Keen Eyes a `range` (50-200), and no foothold or map has a flag for stars (local game data v779, all 14,662 images). Two readings of the client's code give the routine alike:
  - RustMS (github.com/neeerp/RustMS, HeavenClient at commit 946474460d4c, unchanged in today's master), a port of the decompiled 2025 MapleRoyals client, GMS v83, which its author calls AI-assisted; it has unit tests of its own. `ShotPath.h:20` `HEIGHT = 28` (the shot's row above her feet), `:26` `SPREAD_DIVISOR = 4`, `:29` `STEP = 20`; `:311-331` (`search_steps`): each step's half-height is its near edge's distance from her divided by 4, in whole units, about that row (its test: the first step for her feet at y 200 is y 156-188, 44 to 12 above them); `:335-339` (`in_step`): overlap is strict. `PointBlank.h:16` `MAX_REACH = 65`, `:20-26`: the claw's box has its front cut to 65 ahead, and the search starts where it ends. `Combat.cpp:1665-1676` (`find_whack`): the stance is picked first, with the weapon's close list (`CharLook.cpp:641-655`), which for a claw (`info/attack` 7) is stabO1 or stabO2 (`AttackStances.h:49`; the swings are the throw's own list), and the nearest monster in its box, by distance (`:1813-1842`), is whacked, with no gate for her level. `:1695-1720`: the steps are tried nearest first, and within one the monster that entered the map first wins.
  - meowdb's MapleStory Classic pages (this game; meowdb.com/msclassic/formulas/targeting and the Lucky Seven page, fetched 2026-10-20): its code has `{aimHeight: 28, divisor: 4, step: 20, starts: {ranged: 65}}` with a band's half-height `floor(start / 4)`, and its Lucky Seven figure 124 up and 68 down at the last band (385-400), drawn since the 2026-08-22 snapshot, before RustMS's code. It states no method. Its damage guide has a claw always stab at close range. It puts the nearest monster first within a band, RustMS the first to enter the map.
  - ohmi69's OSMS Data Explorer, read from this game's client binary, has the same constants (65 ahead, the row 28 up, a divisor of 4) and gives a claw only stabO1 and stabO2 up close (`lib/skill-range-viz.js:77`; `tabs/formulas.js:78-95`: the swings are what a throw plays), but draws a basic shot's target as a 1-unit line 28 up, which the logs refute (stars hit monsters 60 above and 60 below): it counts for its constants only.
  - Harepacker's v95 MapSimulator has the 28 too (`DefaultShootAttackPointYOffset = -28`).

  Confidence: 65, 20, the divisor 4 and the row 28: high (two lines of sources, for v83 and for this game). The top 44: medium, one code line plus one page with no method. The stab boxes cut to 65: medium-high in the sources (v83's code, two readings of this game). Map-entry order within a band: medium-low (RustMS and ohmi69's object order against meowdb's nearest).
- **What the logs say of it.** The shape is checked against the shot log (204 throws, 100 off her level), the 13 range-probe throws and 418 throws rebuilt from the tours' telemetry, over the monsters' frames and facings. They agree with it, and fit its floor (best of the grid, 4-16 within 2 log-likelihood). They lean to a lower top: fitted, 21 + 5k (95% about 16-28; 44 + 5k 6.2 log-likelihood worse), which `reach` had until 2026-10-20. Cleaned fairly (the kills credited to two throws set aside, and the shot log's hits logged in the same second counted once, not dropped), that lean is 2-5 log-likelihood. Taken as independent throws that would rule 44 out (the probes' scorer bounds a fit at 1.92), but its 10-14 throws 60 above her (150-191 off) are four episodes (3 -> 1 twice, 13 -> 12 and 22 -> 21), whose throws go together: all four leaning the same way comes by chance 1 time in 16 (p 0.06, one-sided), and in the shot log, without any one of them the lean is 1-3.5. Suggestive, not decisive; any top from 17 to 28 fits as well. So `reach` has the client's top, and `reach.SEARCH_TOP0` stays the one number to change (to 21) if throws from 13 at 12 find the lower one (`probe run top-60`, 30 throws: a pig 60 above hit from 111 with 44, only from 191 with 21; `top-120`: hits from about 350 with 44, none with 21). Too high a top has her throw at monsters above her that the star doesn't take; too low, skip them, and count on stars at monsters past them that they take. The claw's reach (65 against the 90 taken before) shows in two places: a slime with its near side 79 off took a star's damage, and a pig thrown at 122 then 74 off (near sides 88 and 40) lost a star's and a swipe's worth together, where the old reach has two swipes. The bar drops can't tell more (`probe run claw-edge` settles it). The order within a band fits random 0.4-0.7 log-likelihood better than nearest: noise.
- **What stops a star** (`reach.wall_between`, `wall_to`) **is checked on the attack's pick alone,** as the client does it. The claw and the star's search pick by the bodies, whatever the terrain; then one line, from her to the monster picked, is checked, and if terrain stops it the attack hits nothing: the star ends where it's stopped, and no other monster takes it. RustMS's port of the v83 client clears its targets and ends the shot at the stop (`Combat.cpp:1289-1307`); ohmi69's reading of this game's client removes a blocked target without filling its slot, and meowdb's page on this game says the same. So a monster the attack takes first whose star is stopped leaves those behind it out of reach (`reach.shielded`: one in an earlier band of the search; one in the same band takes only its share). A claw swipe isn't checked (the client's whack has no line). Until 2026-10-20 a walled monster was passed over, and the next one took the star.
  - **What stops the line:** the walls of the foothold group of the platform she last stood on, and only going in at their outer face: a wall drawn downward faces right, one drawn upward faces left (RustMS `ShotPath.h:382-445`, `blocked`: a line stops only going from a foothold's open side to its closed side; the v40 client's own collisions test walls so too, by a reconstruction of its physics). On this map every wall faces out from the platform it hangs from (the step between 2 and 4 faces 4, below it, and the basin's sides face its floor, 36), so a star thrown from a platform never meets the back of a wall of its group across its line: one-sided walls change nothing here. A floating block is one group (its floor and the walls hanging from its ends), and so are platforms joined by a step: 2 and 4, and the basin's 34, 35 and 36. The walls of other blocks never stop a star. RustMS's port also has the walls of group 0 stop a star from every group (`ShotPath.h:398`; the v40 collisions have no such exception): left out, and on this map group 0 is the basin, where it changes nothing. The game data has no foothold flag for it (in all 306 map images a foothold has its ends, its neighbours and `piece`, and `forbidFallDown` in 51).
  - **The logs** of 2026-10-18 to 10-20: from platform 4 no star reached a monster on 2, 60 above her behind the step wall: 0 of 3 in the shot log, all 4 kills of 40 throws on 4 in a sweep, and 0 of 5 in a tour's telemetry, the Orange Mushrooms on 2 in view with no HP bar for 4 s of throwing. In the same logs, stars 60 up past the side walls of the blocks above hit (19 -> 18 13 of 13 decided, 3 -> 1 15 of 19), and stars down past her own block's hanging wall hit 81% (14 -> 17). "Every wall blocks" contradicts the first of these, and a line from her to the target's feet can't fit both 4 -> 2 and 14 -> 17. On this map her group's walls rule out one pair of platforms, 4 -> 2 (at every x); 174 of the 306 maps have steps within one group.
  - **Which line** (`reach.LINE`). `'v83'`, the default since 2026-10-21: the v83 client's line (RustMS), from 28 above her feet at her own x to where the star hits the body (`reach.hit_point`: its top if it's below the row, its feet if above, the row if it crosses it; its x taken 1 unit inside the monster's platform, `reach._hit_on`, below), which her group's walls stop as above and any floor stops going down through it, the corners at footholds' ends as the client has them (`reach.sight_stop`). meowdb's page on this game says so too: shooting down through the platform she stands on is blocked, up through one isn't (it cites no test: on its Lucky Seven page the claim was tagged unverified on 2026-08-22 and by 2026-09-29 untagged and reworded, and the one in-game test the site gives on terrain is a note that walls stop Energy Bolt). `'level'`, the default until then: a level line 28 above her feet (higher in a jump) to the monster's x, which only walls can cross, as ohmi69's reading of this game's client checks a basic shot (the one source for it, and the one whose basic shot's target, a 1-unit line, the logs refute). The 299 decided live throws of 2026-10-18 to 10-20 give the same verdict by both: every throw down was made from near an end. The user, watching her hunt on 2026-10-21, saw the difference: "for targeting below your platform, your platform itself can block the thro[w] (this applies to both step down and standing on a higher platform trying to hit mobs on a lower one)". The two differ only below her: by the v83 line a star needs room past her platform's end, |dx| > e (28 + t) / 28, e her distance to the end and t how far below her feet the monster's top is (a walking pig 60 below: 1.5 e). From 2 at x 50 it stops every star at pigs on 4 under 254 off and slimes under 284; from 14 at x 690 at pigs on 17 up to 238 off, from x 706 under 215 and slimes under 241; from 25 inside her platform's end, none a star picks 60 below her. Where past the end the star gets through the user's report doesn't say: `probe run step-down` (from 2 at x 50) and `hanging-wall` (from 14 at x 706) throw at pigs and slimes from short of where the v83 line lets it through to about 55 past it. Her decisions, the spots (`spots.coverage`, tried every 2 units) and the simulator's world all keep to it, and where a floor stops a star from where she'd walk to, she walks along her platform to where it doesn't (`stance._clear_from`); the mini-map's band still shows only the walls. In the simulator the line is worth about 1% of her stars on the tour (Found offline: her own platform stops a star down past its end).
  - **Its height:** any from 1 to 59 fits the logs, and 28 is the client's own: the row a shot flies along and its search opens about (the angle, above). On this map no step is lower than 60, so the height changes nothing. The step-up probe brackets it: from 4 at 2 (a 60 step), jump throws (the line 26 higher) stopped and jump backs (44 higher) getting through would put it at 16-34.
