  - **Her decisions:** she doesn't throw at a monster whose star is stopped, or at one behind one (`reach.can_hit` with the monsters around her), or walk along her platform for one but to where its line is clear and no monster the attack takes first has its own stopped (`stance._clear_from` with the monsters around her: from 14 at x 690 a pig on 17 at 880, in her floor's shadow, takes the stars at one at 1000, and from 729 on it's under 151 off and isn't picked, so she closes in to 736); at one behind a monster the attack takes first whose star isn't stopped she does throw: that one takes the stars until it's dead, then hers comes (it orders the kills, as a monster in her way on her platform is shot first), where a walled one dies to none of them and keeps the stars from those behind it as long as it stands there. With every spot near enough shielded so and she at the nearest, `decide` skips the target (it used to walk 0 units, a tap back and forth on the spot until the kill's patience ran out); spots don't count what's behind a wall or under her floor's edge; and a kill isn't matched to one, nor to one behind one (`fight._in_path`, `ledger.in_path`). A monster's x is taken 1 unit inside its platform's ends, so one at the foot of a wall rising from its own platform's end (the basin's sides) isn't behind it. `reach.WALLS_BLOCK = False` switches the rule off for her decisions: the probes that measure it do so for their run (`probe run step-up`, `step-down`, `hanging-wall`); the shot log and the probe log record its verdict either way.
- **A group** (`stance.py`): a star hits one of the nearest monsters in reach on the side she faces (the search's first band), so she throws toward the side with the most monsters in reach, and keeps working the side she faces while it has any. Turning after every throw spread the damage over both sides.
- **What an attack hits is picked when it starts** (`reach.in_area`), about 67 ms after its key like a jump's lift-off, from where she is then; the star that flies is only a picture. From the ground, the claw's stab comes first, then the star's search (above). From a jump, it's the nearest monster whose body (`localization.monsters`: the boxes of its frames in the game data) reaches into an area fixed to her feet: from 19 below them (fitted, 18-21) to at least 60 above, its near side within 400 ahead. A jump throw's attack key used to come 100 ms after the jump key (the macro's F1 combo), so she was 42-49 units up when it started (60 fps recordings), and the area's floor was about 30 above the platform: over a snail, which is 24-26 tall (33 while it flinches); slimes (40 at least), pigs (38) and Orange Mushrooms (55) reach into it. On 2026-10-19, of 45 jump-throws at snails 3-77 units away on the 168-unit platforms 3, 20 and 22, at most one hurt the snail, and pigs, slimes and Orange Mushrooms on the platform 60 above took the stars and died. Their EXP was taken for the snail's ("snail down"), and the snail, still alive, was then left alone as a dying one. Now the key comes 40 ms after the jump (`physics.JUMP_ATTACK_MS`): the attack starts about 26 units up, and the area's floor about 7 above the platform. In game on 2026-10-20, at a snail 20-60 units away on platform 22: keyed 100 ms after the jump, the star flew over it and did nothing; keyed 40 ms after, a star hit it for 78 and killed it. In a hunt loop afterwards, jump-throws at snails killed them. RustMS's port of the v83 client has no case for the air: from a jump the claw and the search would pick as from the ground, from as high as she is, and 21-26 up (the attack starts up to 11 ms after the jump's lift-off lag) that picks no snail 35-85 off: the stabs' boxes are then 36-56 above the ground, and the search's windows first take a snail 86-106 off. The kills above go against it; `probe run air` weighs the two, and the simulator's world has either (`sim.world.AIR_PICK`).
- **Too close:** she jumps away from it, turning toward it in mid-air to throw. A star leaves about 520 ms after the attack key, in the air or not (recorded at 60 fps), so the key goes in right after lift-off (85 ms after the jump key) and the star leaves just as she lands; its target, though, is picked when the attack starts, about 40 units up and 16 out (`jb_k165` at 60 fps, the jump keyed 80 ms after the arrow; about 27 out by the engine with it keyed 150 ms after, as now), not the whole jump away. It needs 110 units of room behind her and no monster within 160 there; otherwise (cornered) she throws from a jump straight up. Each only if that attack starts low enough to pick the nearest monster's kind (`reach.air_hits`: how tall it is at least, 6 units into the area): a jump back's (floor 25 up) can't pick a snail, and she doesn't jump-throw at one either (`stance.JUMP_THROW_SNAILS`, off since 2026-10-21: in game they mostly missed; Found offline: snails from the ground): she steps back to 3 past the claw's edge (`reach.star_sure`: 99 for a snail; a star from the ground), and as much farther as it walks at her meanwhile, if her platform has the room (the 50 she keeps from its ends with monsters about: not to a short platform's far end, `stance.SNAIL_FAR_END`), else stabs it from the ground. A monster counts as too close by its size, as far as the claw would swipe it (`stance.close_to`; the hunt loop's close quarters too). It all follows from `JUMP_ATTACK_MS`: keyed 100 ms after the jump, a jump throw's attack couldn't pick a snail either, and she shot it from the ground (the claw swipes it, weak but always hitting: 5 swipes kill one). Under a platform within her jump height she doesn't jump to attack (on platform 20, five jump-throws at a snail put her up on 19); she steps away instead, or (cornered) throws from the ground.
- **Spacing:** she steps away from any monster on her own platform within 110 units, or as far as the claw would swipe it (a pig within 111), unless that walks into another one or off her platform. With monsters on her platform she stays 50 units from its ends: a monster touching her pushes her 20-21 units on a wide platform, and 82 and 109 units on a narrow one, which knocked her off.
- **Facing:** she only taps an arrow to turn when she isn't already facing the target. Every tap also walks her a few units, and nine taps in a row once walked her off the end of her platform. A jump-throw that turns her first waits for the tap's walk to stop (`actions.JUMP_THROW_TURN_MS`), so it goes straight up.
- **Where to shoot a platform from** (`spots.py`): a spot on another platform she can stand on, either one no monster can reach or one only monsters not worth hunting reach (snails: she shoots those when they come close), with at least 30% of the hunting platform in reach. Of the spots about as good as the best, she takes the nearest: coming up from the basin she shoots 21, 18 and 12 from the platforms below them, and from above she shoots down, from near her platform's end, where her floor stops the least (17 from 14 at x 756; by the level line 716 had as much). The spots for a platform depend on the map (and on what stops a star, which a probe can switch: `reach.WALLS_BLOCK`), so they're worked out once for each (27 ms a platform by the v83 line, every star's line to every 2 units of the platform from every 10 of the others tried at once, `reach._sight_clear`; 3.6 by the level line): the hunt loop's 16 grounds take 0.67 s at its first look.

## Control

- **Actions** (`actions.py`): every key plan she sends, blocking until the player has run it. Throws come one per attack cooldown. Whenever she walks, she presses pick-up every 0.15 s; on a kill's drops she stops and presses it 4 times (a press takes one item, a pig drops several). No plan's first arrow goes down sooner than 0.8 s after her last attack's key (`ARROW_LOCK_MS`, in `hold_back`, which every plan goes through, the navigator's chains too, as for the jump and attack keys and the attack lock): an arrow pressed sooner (up to 0.62 s after it at least) doesn't move her until about 0.79 s after the key (In game, below; it was 0.6 s until arrows held back that long were seen to set her off 0.2 s late). Her own walks already waited out the attack lock (`wait_lock`); the mover's walks and the chains waited only for a jump in them.
- **One move at a time** (`mover.py`), closed loop, for what a chain can't predict, such as climbing down a rope, which needs her lined up on its top, and a jump off a rope (`rope_jump`: onto it and to about the height it jumps from, a look there by her dot's own height, a climb to it if that's off by more than 15 units, then the arrow and jump, up held for the rope it jumps onto, or the arrow let go just before she lands). Also gets her off a rope she's hanging on (climbing down and off the bottom of one that takes her onto no platform at its top: 959's, under 17's block). A walk-off starts 12 units in from the end at least (`mover.MIN_RUN`; closer, she backs off to 22 first), so she leaves it at full speed as the route has her: from 0.4 units in she left 31's end at 35 units/s, missed 34 and went through the basin's cliff (harness, tour seed 3).
- **A route** (`navigator.py`): one look, then plan, then chain as many moves as are safe and predictable into one key plan. Like a player, she doesn't go the same way every time: each trip weighs every move by a random 1 to 1.35, and moves start from varied spots. From platform 1 down to 34, 14 different routes came up in 40 trips, all within 1-2% of the quickest. Over a long route the weights average out, so ways more than a few percent apart never swap; with `--ways 3` a trip chooses among whole ways, and keeps to the one it took through its replans (Route diversity). Chains are capped at about 3.5 s, and the next one follows on without a stop where it can:
  - About 0.9 s before a chain's last landing she looks around (from where she starts, where she's going can be off screen). With an escort, what would stop her where the chain ends stops her there: the trip's `until`, or in hunt mode a monster in reach.
  - Once she's back on the ground for good, a look places her (standing: ground under her feet, not just her minimap dot, which put her on a platform she was still falling past), the next chain is planned from there (about 5 ms) and queued, and the player starts it the moment the running one ends. The running plan keeps her going for the ~0.3 s that takes: running on with the arrow held, short of the next move, or standing. That look skips the monster search (in game 110 ms, 131 at p90, against 205 with it): with it, none of 15 chains in a hunt loop followed on (seen too late to plan in time); without it, every one with room to run on did. Where there's no room for that (she lands right before her next jump, as all the way up the staircase from 34), she stops there instead; and when nothing follows after all, the rest of that run on is cut, so she stops where the chain was to end.
  - Nor does a pass plan from a look that shows her in the air (no ground under her feet on screen, `navigator.standing`: in the air her minimap dot alone puts her on the platform under her). It waits for her to land (`Mover.settle`) and looks again, and a caller's last look taken in mid-jump doesn't do for a trip's first pass. Planned from a look right after a jump throw, a run to a drop from 33 onto 35 went mostly in the air, and she dropped 30 units short, onto the basin by its cliff (harness, loop seed 30). In a 20-minute hunt loop a caller's look was in mid-jump at 4 of 66 trips.
  - Planned from where the running chain was predicted to end instead, errors built up: a landing 12.5 units off made the next running jump leave 12.5 units early, and landing late (still in the air, or sliding to a stop once her plan was over) the next chain's keys came too early and she fell off.
  - With an escort, chains are planned without the monsters (it fights them instead, at the stops), so one follows on only where none of the monsters seen can get near it, and none stands in the way of its first move; the run on past a chain's end is checked against them too.
  - Where she stops, the look the next pass needs is taken once she has stopped sliding (0.35 s at most): it used to be two looks in a row there, about 0.4 s standing (0.6 s with the slide). A trip also starts from its caller's last look if no key plan was sent since (`go(scene=...)`): the hunt loop's decision goes straight to the first chain.
  - In game on 2026-10-20 (a 3-minute hunt loop): the stops between chains were 0.2-0.5 s, against about 0.4 s the day before, and 0.75-0.8 s with the slow look. Most chains on this map still end where she lands right before her next jump, with no room to look first, and stop there.
  - In the simulator, over 164 paired trips per case: without monsters, trips took 11% less time in transit and 15% less avoiding monsters, with misses from 20 to 9 and from 68 to 10 (the queue that followed on from predicted ends was most of those). With monsters the differences are within the noise: hunt mode 3% faster, avoiding 2% faster, transit 7% slower (one standard error), with as many misses and hits as before.
  - What it records (telemetry, for the mini-map and the coverage report): each route as planned, with where it was planned from (`from`; since 2026-10-21's evening), and the platforms its trip keeps off and the moves it costs extra for a monster in their way when there are any, and at a trip's first plan with `--ways` the ways it chose among (`ways`: each way's move, cost and chance) and which it took (`chose`); each chain as sent; and after each chain, or move made alone, a `moved` record: the moves made, where it was to end, where she was then, and whether she was hit on the way (before, a miss was only in the log, "ended on ... instead of"). Nothing is built without a recorder.

  Meanwhile it glances at her minimap dot and HP about 15 times a second: a hit, or her standing on a platform the running leg doesn't go to, cuts both plans short, and it replans from wherever she is. What the glances show is judged by how long it lasts, not by how many glances show it, so it means the same however often she's glanced at (each glance has its frame's time, `Senses.glanced_at`): she counts as standing where her dot has stood within 0.6 minimap px of where it stood for 0.109 s (`STILL_S`; for 63 ms it can sit still in the air, near the top of a jump or inside one minimap pixel at full speed), off the path for 0.109 s from the first glance off it cuts the plan (`OFF_PATH_S`; 0.046 s once one has her where the leg doesn't go, `OFF_LEG_S`), and after a plan `recover` takes 0.046 s of her dot standing for her stopped (`RECOVER_STILL_S`). Until 2026-10-21 these were 3, 3, 2 and 2 glances 63 ms apart (`STILL_GLANCES`, `OFF_PATH`): n of them are (n − 1) × 63 ms less a game frame, since a glance is the newest frame when it's taken; at that pace nothing changed (Continuous perception: Glances at the feed's rate). She never counts as standing while the plan has her in the air (`Flow.airborne`: from a jump's, hop's, walk-off's or drop's takeoff until a moment after its landing; a climb counts, since below the rope she missed it). Standing still where her run along a platform starts while the plan has her 30 units or more along it (`OFF_SPAN`, by `Span.x_at`) is off the path too: in game a chain that set off 0.2 s late jumped at its planned time from short of its takeoff and fell. A look that shows her still in the air once she has settled after a plan waits for her to land once more (`FALL_LOOKS`): knocked down the 12-unit gap beside platform 3 to the basin, she fell for 2.5 s, and the trip ended "lost: not on any platform" (simulator). Without an escort she avoids monsters: a leg a monster could walk into costs extra so a way around is taken, else she waits for it to move off. With an escort (hunting tours), she fights instead (see Behavior). If her minimap dot disappears, for example under another program's tooltip, it moves the mouse inside the game to clear it.

## Behavior

- **The ledger and the drop store** (`ledger.py`): every kill her EXP gains tell of (Her EXP number) is placed on a monster of the kind its gain named (else left unplaced: `KIND_ANY`) that can have taken the attack of one of her throws in the last 2.5 s (the nearest the claw's stance reaches, else any monster in the search's first band, but none whose star terrain stops: with the first band's only monster up on 2 from 4, none; the likeliest first, and preferring one that has gone; where it was seen last, a throw's path being followed from look to look); with none, in the 2.5 s before the look before, as the gain came after that one: a fight's last star often lands after its last look, and the sweep after it takes no look for up to 7 s (in the simulator's scenarios 16 of 629 kills went unplaced that way). A placed kill's drops become an expected pile there, and looks that searched for loot confirm or forget piles, each keeping what the last look that saw it saw in it and what that's worth by the game data's prices (`looting.worth`); a pile no kill of hers explains is someone else's. Her wounds (`Wounds`): a monster in the path of a throw of hers whose HP bar shows up or changes in a look within 2.5 s of it is hers, followed look to look until a kill of hers is placed on it, it's out of sight 3 s, or 15 s have gone by since it was first hurt (chased too long); the fights and the hunt loop finish these before she moves on.
- **The hunt loop** (`hunt.py`, `tour --loop`): instead of the tour's stops, loot trips and fights on the way, one decision after every look, by the first of these rules that applies: close quarters; a valuable pile (a drop worth 30 mesos or more on its own, 15 s away at most: at once, ahead of shooting); back to the high ground; an urgent pile (about to vanish, close); shoot what's in reach; in passing (while travelling, 15 s per platform; with a step of 150 at most out from under one above her); finish (travelling to another platform: the monsters she has hurt, in reach or a walk of 150 along her platform away, 8 s per objective at most); a pile on her platform; a pile on the way (travelling: a stop at it 2 s at most more than the trip by the planner's times, 2.5 s away at most, 3 a trip); a better spot; lull loot (nothing left on the ground and her kills due back); wait; and, leaving, the piles left then (the most worth per second of travel first) and the next ground. She leaves a ground by the rate rule: her EXP rate there below 80% of the best other ground's (the trip there included), or nothing left and nothing due back (the fast exit; that ground is skipped for 2 minutes). Objectives (a spot, a pile) are suspended by higher rules, not ended, and dropped after 60 s, suspensions included. Visits are recorded with their length in `lab/grounds.json`. The numbers are guesses tuned in the simulator (`docs/hunt-modes.md`). The rate rule and the mini-map's 'hunt' record weigh every other ground's spot and the trip there after most looks: worked out afresh each time, that took 30-60 ms between a look and her next keys; with the spots kept and the trips' times from one search, in the simulator's hunt loop (preset `game`, 5 seeds of 10 minutes, 5,425 looks) the stack's own computing before a plan went out was 2.6 ms at the 99th percentile instead of 53.7 (at most 3.9 instead of 58.6), 5.0 s in all instead of 31.4 (see Where the time goes), and every run the same as before.

- **The hunter** (`hunter.py`) keeps count after every look, whoever took it: a kill counts only when her EXP number goes up (counting a vanished target as dead was wrong about half the time: hit monsters flinch and hide behind damage numbers, and other players kill monsters too): as many kills as its gain's size is, of the kinds it names, and none for a size no kill gives (see Her EXP number). It drinks an HP potion below `--potion-hp` and hands control back below `--low-hp`.
- **A fight** (`fight.py`, `clear`): one throw per attack cooldown, with a look during each. She never walks past a monster. A monster of any kind that comes close on her own platform is in her way and fought, snails included. Reaching a stop's kill count doesn't end a fight while monsters are still in reach. Nor, in any fight, while one she had hurt by then is in reach or a walk of 150 along her platform away (`fight.owed`, `finish_act`), and with nothing else in reach she closes in on it, in the escort's fights too, which don't walk after monsters otherwise. An EXP gain seen by a look the fight didn't take itself (a sweep's, before it ended at its kill count or in the sweep after it) or by the look it ended on (knocked off) is still told apart as a kill when it ends. On a tour she also shoots monsters worth hunting on any platform in reach. A star hits the first monster in its path, whatever its kind, so kills are matched against everything in the path, not just what she was aiming at: from the ground what the claw or the search can pick by each monster's body (`reach.ground_pick`: not a slime 60 below and 150 off, nor a pig 440 off), but not one whose star terrain stops, nor any behind one in the attack's order (the client doesn't refill its pick); from a jump, as high as she is when the attack starts (`reach.in_area`): monsters on the platform above count as far as a star reaches, a snail beside her doesn't. A monster just hit (its HP bar new or lower since it was last seen, or hidden under its damage numbers) counts as tall as it flinches (the game data's `hit1` frames, for 0.6 s): a pig 60 below her is then in the path from 111 units along, against 151 walking. No jump throw, jump back or step (stepping away, closing in) is keyed from a look a touch's knock may still be moving her in (a look within 0.45 s of one that saw her HP drop: `Hunter.knocked`, `fight.knocked_move`; the hunt loop's shooting too): it waits for the next look. Keyed from such a look, the jump key came in the air and was lost, or went in once she had landed and slid on, from where she no longer was (Fixed since); a step walked her on from where the knock had put her, once off the end of 22.
- **Hidden monsters:** a monster she was throwing at is still thrown at for 1.8 s after it drops out of sight (hit, it flinches under its damage numbers, and the detector loses it for 1-3 s). A kill is still seen fading out where it died as long as its death animation runs (the game data's die1 frames: a pig or an Orange Mushroom 0.66 s, a slime 0.84, a snail 1.26), and what's seen there is left alone that long from the look that saw her EXP go up (`fight.fade_s`; 1.2 s for every kind before, and for a kind the data lacks).
- **Waiting:** with nothing in reach she walks just close enough to the nearest monster. With monsters seen but out of reach, she waits up to 2 s from a safe platform (1 s on the hunting platform). With none seen at all, she moves on after 0.4 s.
- **Fighting on the way** (`FightOnTheWay`): before each move, a short burst at whatever a star reaches from where she stands, on any platform: up to 3 kills, never walking after them (but for one she has hurt, a walk of 150 at most: she finishes it), up to 5 bursts per platform. It also shoots any monster in the way of a move along a platform. It's turned off while she goes to rest.
- **Loot** (`looting.py`): each kill's drops are swept when the fight is over, if they fell on her platform (it looks again until she has landed: the fight often ended mid-jump). After the sweep she looks again and goes back for drops still on her platform, standing right on each one, for up to 3 looks. Drops elsewhere are remembered for the tour: her kills', and any seen on other platforms. A remembered spot is forgotten only when two looks 1.5 s apart, 3 s or more after the kill, both see it empty. A drop she has stood on twice that's still there is left alone for 90 s: for mesos that means someone else's drop; for an item it may mean her inventory is full, which the pickup log (OCR) says, or 3 such items in a row. With it full she leaves items and still picks up mesos.
- **A tour** (`tour.py`) sweeps every platform worth hunting. A platform's worth is the EXP a visit is expected to give: at first 30% of its spawns' EXP (game data), counted as 2 visits, then blended with what each visit really gave (`lab/grounds.json`). Monsters giving under a quarter of the map's best EXP aren't hunted, only shot when in her way (snails give 2, against 12-15). Platforms expected to give under 15% of the best one's are skipped: by the data, 34 and 35 spawn slimes, but 11 visits found only snails there. The order: nearest next by route time, judged with a random ±25%, then tidied to cut travel (2-opt); after each stop the rest of the sweep is planned again from wherever she ended up. At a stop she kills all she can reach from her spot; if some are left out of reach, she moves to another spot that reaches them rather than walking down into them, and only then collects the drops (nearest by route first; drops of monsters shot on the way are kept for the next collection, and none starts a trip after 180 s). She moves on once a whole-screen look sees no monster left. When HP is low, she rests on the chair at the nearest safe platform (on the way there she still drinks potions, but low HP doesn't stop the trip), and she ends standing by there.

## In game, on Thicket Around the Beach II

- **Navigation:** 21 moves in a row across every move type without a miss, and a 17-move climb from the basin to the top. Chained, a 13-move route up took 19.0 s in 3 key plans with no misses (33.4 s one move at a time). Over 10 test routes, a move landed elsewhere about once in 15, usually a drop or a straight-up jump timed off; each time she replanned from where she was.
- **Hops on routes:** across 4 routes (45 moves), she hopped 4 monsters in her way, with no hits and no misses.
- **Shooting from other platforms:** from platform 14 at monsters on 17 below, from 3 at monsters on 1 above, from 11 at 12 below and from 15 at 18 below. Most kills took 2 regular throws.
- **Sweeps:** a 6-stop sweep made 7 kills (EXP +92) in 114 s with no rest needed. With long-range shots, all 9 stops in 350 s with a rest: 28 kills (14 at the stops, 14 on the way) and EXP 6,459 → 6,856; far kills' drops were collected 5-50 s later. After the jump-back timing change, a full 9-stop sweep made 28 kills (6 on the way), EXP 6,856 → 7,247 in 309 s, lowest HP 92%. In crowds it took 7-9 throws a kill: each star hits whichever monster is nearest in its path, so the damage spreads.
- **Loot:** recorded at 2 frames a second, the sweep after a fight picked up a slime's Squishy Liquid and two coins within 8 s, and the tour's loot collection picked up drops seen from another platform. With her Etc tab full, a Snail Shell stayed after she stood on it twice; she read the game's message in the pickup log and switched to mesos only.
- **Arrows right after an attack** (2026-10-21; the telemetry of the hunt loops and tours played that night): an arrow pressed within 0.62 s of an attack's key (at least: later ones up to 0.78 s weren't pressed) doesn't move her until about 0.79 s after it. From 00:15 to 00:48, of 13 chains sent within 0.35 s of the look after a throw (their first arrow about 0.3 s after its key), 6 first moved her 0.73-0.92 s after they went out and 2 never did, and 9 missed their move: held on, the arrow set her off about 1.0 s after the key, and let go sooner it did nothing. Twice a walk-off off 3's left end onto 4, the arrow held 0.5 s from about 0.3 s after the key (00:19:04 and 00:19:37), left her standing on 3, and the walk-off sent again went. Arrows pressed 0.575 and 0.66 s after the key were read as moving her at once (but see below: by when she went off the end, the one at 0.575 set off 0.07 s late). From 00:49 every plan's first arrow waits until 0.6 s after the key (`actions.ARROW_LOCK_MS`): in the five runs to 01:17 no chain went sooner than 0.45 s after the look after a throw, all seven walk-offs from 3 onto 4 set off within 0.41 s, and every move that missed was a jump (9 of them: Motion planning), where before it 11 of the 13 were, and 2 were those walk-offs. The 5-minute hunt loop right after it ended 3 chains elsewhere than planned or off their path, the one before it 12. But held back to 0.6 s she still set off late, and those misses were its own: of the walk-offs from 3 onto 4, her dot first moved 0.41 s after the plan went out (the median of 11), against 0.23 s (of 6) when the first arrow came later after the key. Timed better, by when her dot went off the end of its platform (less the fall to her dot's first drop, and her run to the end by the engine), the 17 walk-offs whose first arrow went out 0.59-0.62 s after the key were acted on 0.75-0.88 s after it by her dot (median 0.81: 0.21 s after the plan went out, where an arrow with no attack before it is 0.09 s, the median of 73 walk-offs), so about 0.79 s after the key by the game's clock, 0.12 s late; the 3 whose first arrow went 0.79-0.82 s after the key were acted on at once (0.03-0.09 s). The press read as at once at 0.575 s was the walk-off at 00:19:14 (0.56 s after the key by this reckoning, the throw's key 0.13 s before the look after it): it set off 0.07 s late, and its dot first moved only as she fell. So she moves from about 0.79 s after the key, wherever up to 0.62 s the arrow went down; from 0.63 to 0.78 s no arrow was pressed, so whether one then is swallowed isn't known. **To check in game:** standing on open ground, `act f 650 right+ 500 right- 600 --record`, and the same with 700 and 750 for 650 (twice each, and left): she should start moving 66 ms after the arrow if it isn't swallowed, else about 0.79 s after the key; if 650 isn't, `actions.ARROW_LOCK_MS` can come down to it. Of the 10 chains that set off right after a throw for the jump from 16 up onto 14 (for the rope to 6), 8 came down short of 14 and fell to 25 (taking off 15-30 units short where tracked: the jump key went at its planned time) and one overshot onto 17 off a step back gone late; the 18 sent with no throw in the 1.5 s before all landed. (In the logs these read as rope grabs toward 6 that dropped her onto 25: 00:34:11, 00:46:42, 01:05:40, 01:05:58, 01:24:47, 01:44:06, 01:44:27, 01:48:40.) The first arrow now waits until 0.8 s after the key (Control), and the simulator acts on a swallowed one at about 0.79 s (`world.ARROW_HELD` 0.77 and the wait for the step; 0.835 at first, fitted to the dot's first move: with it, held back to 0.6 s the chains onto 14 were mostly cut short on 16 rather than falling to 25 as in game).
- **Jumps from standing** (2026-10-21, 34 jumps from platform 34 keyed 0-160 ms after the arrow): the game moves her in 30 ms steps, and a jump three steps into a run leaves at 126 units/s and lands 13 units short; from 150 ms after the arrow a jump leaves at full speed but for at most 5% (none of the 10 keyed 130 ms or later left short; of the 8 keyed 100 and 120 ms after it, 6 did). The kick: all 4 at 40 ms and 3 of 4 at 60 left at full speed, not reliable enough to plan on. Motion planning has the tables.
- **Respawns, and how full the map is** (the telemetry of 2026-10-19 to 21, offline: Respawns and drop life): a platform she emptied was mostly still empty 12.56 s later (of 77, watched without a gap over 2 s, 80% weren't back, 23 watched that long; past 30 s too few were watched to say; in the simulator about half were back within 5 s), and the platforms in view had a monster at 26% of their spawn points (the basin 12%, and none 45% of the time). Drops lay over a minute (95% at 60 s); how much longer, no hunt has shown.
- **Not yet checked on its own:** placing her from the minimap dot and the camera when her name tag is hidden.

## Probes to run

What a star hits still has open questions that only the game can settle (see Attack): the search window's top and floor, the search between her level and 60 below her, how an attack from a jump picks its target, what stops a star (that her own platform stops a star down past its end, and the step wall one up to 2, the user saw on 2026-10-21; where it gets through, and how high its line runs, are left), and how far the claw swipes. Each is now between reach's answer (the client's routine, or the logs' where the sources don't reach) and one other. `python -m gamelab probe run NAME` puts one of them to the game; none has been run in game yet (built and tested in the simulator only). The player must be running, as for `hunt`. `probe score` weighs both answers on the log (below), and once one is decided, what the second column names goes its way.

| Command (her place -> theirs, what it throws at) | Decides: reach's answer / the other | Expected by reach's | By the other |
|---|---|---|---|
| `probe run top-60` (13 at x -14 -> 12, 60 above her: pigs and Orange Mushrooms 110-200 off) | `SEARCH_TOP0`: 44, the client's / 21, the logs' fit | Hits from 111 (Orange Mushrooms 113) | Hits only from 191 (193) |
| `probe run top-120` (15 at x 76 -> 12, 120 above: 340-420 off; whole-screen looks) | The same | Hits from 351 | None |
| `probe run claw-edge` (21 at x -225, her level: pigs and slimes 95-160 off; with up to 2 others in front, the snails on 20 and 22) | `CLAW_FAR`: the stabs cut to 65 ahead / one box to 90 ahead, her level only (reach's until 2026-10-20) | Swipes (small bar drops) out to 99 off a pig, 93 off a slime; stars beyond | Swipes out to 124 and 118 |
| `probe run claw-down` (3 at x 716 -> 4, 60 below: pigs within 95 at the pick, Orange Mushrooms 60-125; 80 throws) | `claw_takes`: the stabs, 15-30 above her feet / the throw's swings, 17 below to 62 above | No hit at a pig; stars at Orange Mushrooms from 113 | A third of the throws within 99 swiped |
| `probe run below-42` (0 at x 1222 -> 1, 42 below: pigs 60-160, Orange Mushrooms 100-160, snails 130-220) | `RAMP_BELOW`: the search alone / the old ramp; and `claw_takes` | Pigs hit from 71, Orange Mushrooms at any distance, snails from 166; no swipe | The ramp: every kind from 126. The swings: a third of the throws at pigs within 99 swiped |
| `probe run step-down` (2 at x 50 -> 4, 60 below: pigs 200-310, slimes 230-340; 40 throws) | `LINE`: the v83 line / a level line | None under 254 (pigs) or 284 (slimes): 2's own floor stops the line; hits beyond | Hits |
| `probe run hanging-wall` (14 at x 706 -> 17, 60 below: pigs 180-250, slimes 190-290; 40 throws) | The same | None under 215 (pigs; on 17 from 189) or 241 (slimes; from 183); hits beyond | Hits |
| `probe run step-up`, with `--style jump_throw` and `jump_back`, each with `--x 260` too (4 at x 500 -> 2, 60 above, behind the step wall: 200-400) | `SHOT_H`, fitted; her own group's walls stop a star / no wall does (the logs had it, and the user saw it on 2026-10-21) | No hits from the ground (the line 28 up) or jump throws (54 up), hits from jump backs (72 up), by either line | Hits from all three |
| `probe run other-wall` (19 at x 104 -> 18, 60 above: 200-260) | Only her own group's walls stop a star / every wall does | Hits, as on open ground | None |
| `probe run air` (22 at x 30: jump throws at a lone snail 35-85 off) | `in_area`: the area fixed to her feet / the v83 client's, as from the ground from as high as she is | Hits at every distance | None (21-26 up, the stabs reach 36-56 above the ground, and the search first takes a snail 86-106 off) |
| `probe run floor` (14 at x 824 -> 17, 60 below: Orange Mushrooms 90-150, pigs 120-190, slimes 130-200) | `SEARCH_FLOOR0`, fitted (no other answer) | Orange Mushrooms hit from 113, pigs from 151 (171 standing), slimes from 177 | |

Where the probes stand is chosen so their throws tell the answers apart under the client's pick:
- **step-down and hanging-wall** throw where the two lines differ and about as far past it, 40 times: that her own platform stops a star down past its end the user saw in game, and what's left is where it lets one through, which the hits by distance in `probe score` show (and about half their throws are where the lines differ, as all 20 were before 2026-10-21). By the v83 line a star needs room past her platform's end, so from 2 at x 50 (169 from its end) the lines differ at pigs on 4 out to 253 and slimes to 283; an Orange Mushroom is clear from 199, and all of 4's are 201 or more off, so none is thrown at. hanging-wall stands at x 706 (25 inside 14's left end; from x 730 the lines differed only at pigs 166-178): pigs differ at 189-214, slimes at 183-240, and no Orange Mushroom can be close enough.
- **claw-down** throws at a pig only where it will be within 95 when the attack picks (`Probe.at_pick`, `probe.x_after`: one seen walking goes on at its kind's speed the way it faces for the time from the look to the pick, turning back at 4's end). A pig on 4 is never closer than 81 to her place (4 ends at 669), and the swings reach one to 99, so the throws that tell the answers apart are at a pig in the last 18 units of 4, where it turns, and walking 125 units/s (the game data's) it goes 20-26 units from the look to the pick. Where she stands moves that: with the game's errors (the harness's preset `game`) she stood 704-720 on seeds 1-8, within the 20 she keeps to, where a pig comes within 69-85 of her. Thrown at by where the look had it (88-100 off), it was within 97 at the pick on 13-37 of 40 throws there, and 40 throws decided the stabs on 1 of the 8 seeds, the swings on 2; by where it will be, 63-74 of 80. Orange Mushrooms out to 125, where their stars from 113 show how often a hit is seen. It needs 80 throws: with the stabs nothing at all is hit within 99, and a run of no hits only slowly rules out a swipe a third of the time (with the game's errors, 40 decide the stabs on all 8 seeds and the swings on 4, 80 both on all 8).
- **below-42** throws at Orange Mushrooms only from 100. One 42 below reaches into the stabs' boxes in its taller frames (58-67 units: half its standing and a quarter of its walking), which the scorer, by its walking body (55), leaves out; past the boxes' reach (97) it tells the ramp from the search and nothing else. Pigs (54 tall at most) never reach the stabs from 42 below.
- **step-up** is left with the line's height (the user saw the step stop her stars on 2026-10-21, as the logs had it), and can't tell the lines apart: the v83 line to a walking body on 2 ends at its feet, 60 up, so it meets the step wall below its top corner whatever the distance, as the level line does from the ground and from jump throws, and clears it with it from jump backs. Only a body lifted off its feet right behind the step's edge (a pig's third walking frame lifts 4; 281-315 off from x 500) would take the v83 line over the corner: a few hits there from the ground would point to it. Nor, since the star's hit point is taken on the monster's platform (`reach._hit_on`), does a body hanging over the step's edge at the far end of a star's reach (from 4's right end, 368-410 off) take it past the wall. Nor can any probe here tell one-sided walls from walls that stop a star either way: every wall on this map faces out from the platform it hangs from.

**How a probe throws:**
- The navigator takes her to her place, keeping clear of monsters, and she keeps to it, facing the way she throws: knocked off, or carried off by a jump back, she walks or goes back, her last walk the way she throws (from 12 behind her place at least), so no throw turns her. A turn's tap walks her 7 units on: from x 30 on 22 (`air`) her throws after the first came from 38, where the snail came back within reach of her. Nor does she walk or throw from a look a touch's knock may still be moving her in (`Hunter.knocked`).
- She throws the regular attack only when exactly one monster is in front of her (within 420 units by its middle, from 140 above her feet to 140 below; behind a wall or not, since a walled one the attack takes first takes it and the star stops at the wall), and it's one the probe throws at, in range sideways (for claw-down, where it will be when the attack picks). `--crowd N` also throws with N others in front, for a busy ground (the scorer weighs every monster in front); claw-edge does with 2 of its own (`Probe.crowd`), for the snails on 20 and 22, 60 above and below her: in game it threw none alone (below). A jump back's range ends 27 units nearer (`probe.JUMP_BACK_OUT`, the engine's): she's that far out when its attack picks, and from 4 at 2 Orange Mushrooms 387-394 off at the look were past a star's reach by then.
- After each throw she waits for its star's evidence before the next: every look until 0.9 s after the star was due, or up to 2.5 s while the target is hidden under its damage numbers. So a hit is never another throw's, and a monster hit by one throw has stopped flinching when the next attack picks.
- Her own platform's monsters (`probe._keep_clear`): one of a kind it doesn't throw at that comes within 150 of her (`fight.IN_HER_WAY`) is fought off first (`fight.clear` on her platform, up to one kill). One it throws at, on her platform (claw-edge, air), closer than its range starts, is stepped away from (`probe.GIVE_WAY`), up to 60 from her place and never within 90 of her platform's end, and where she can't step, waited out: fighting those off cost claw-edge nearly all its throws in the simulator (below). Either way no probe throws while one of her platform's monsters is in her way (`probe.in_her_way`, `choose`), whatever its crowd: one that close in front takes the attack itself, and such throws told nothing (below). A probe that stands where monsters spawn and throws at another platform's (`probe.clears_first`: step-down from 2, step-up from 4, claw-down from 3) clears her platform first (every monster on it in reach, 8 kills or 30 s at most), and again whenever one of its monsters is in front of her or in her way, and waits for a throw between clears. A run that fights 120 s without a throw (`probe.FIGHT_MAX_S`) stops and says so: her platform's monsters come back faster than she clears them, and she'd fight its whole time (as step-down did in game, below). The report counts the fights and their time.
- `--x`, `--dx LO-HI`, `--style` and `--throws` change a probe. `--walls on|off` sets whether her decisions keep to the wall rule during the run (whether a target behind a wall, or behind a monster the attack takes first that is, is thrown at, and the fights); the probes that measure it set it off.
- The player, the Lie Detector watchdog and the alarm work as for `hunt kill`: `probe run` is refused while the alarm is up, and every key goes through the player. Waiting for a lone monster she can go minutes without a key, the moment the alarm is otherwise noticed, so the probe also checks it before every look (a look brings the game to the front). `probe list` and `probe score` send nothing.

**The log** (`lab/probe_geometry.jsonl`, one line per throw) keeps where she and every monster in front of her were at the throw, and at the first look after it (about when the attack picked its target), each monster with the way it faced and its pose as the look read them. For each of those monsters it keeps its HP bar before the star could land, the bar in every look after, the looks it wasn't seen in, and whether it was gone at the end. It also keeps her EXP gains and the wall rule's verdict.

**`probe score`** (or `probe score FILE`) reads the log with the map's game data. A monster showed a hit when its HP bar came up or dropped, or when it died (her EXP up, and it the one gone): the claw's if it took under 20 HP's worth of its bar (a swipe does 9-11, a star 30-70: 16% of a pig's bar, 14% of an Orange Mushroom's, 17% of a slime's, 44% of a snail's, by their HP in the game data), else a star's. It showed none when it was seen with its bar as before 0.3 s or more after the star was due. While it was hidden, nothing can be told. Each answer is reach's model of the attack with one piece changed (`probe.Model`), and gives every monster in front at the pick its chance of the claw's swipe and of a star that gets to it, as the simulator's world picks: the claw's stance, one per attack, the nearest in its box; else any monster in the search's first band as likely; from a jump the area, or the search from as high as she is; then the line to the star's pick alone, the star lost if it's stopped (`probe.chances`). A monster the look after the key saw walking is as likely anywhere it walks from 40 ms before that look to 40 ms after it (`probe.placings`, `PICK_SLACK_S`): that look isn't taken at the pick, and its frame is older than its time, by how much isn't measured in game. Its likelihood is that of every monster's hit or none, with the two hit rates fitted (a monster taken shows a hit at least 30% of the time, one not taken at most 20%: without those bounds a run of hits fits "not taken" as well as "taken"; and neither rate within 5% of 0 or 1, since a hit or none is read wrong now and then too), and of every hit's size, read wrong 5% of the time. So a single "none" where every other throw hit weighs at most 2.9 (log 0.95/0.05), under what decides; with the rates allowed to 2% of 0 and 1 it weighed up to 3.9, and below-42 was decided in the simulator on one or two throws. Then:
- **Each question**, over the throws of the probes that ask it (the second column): both answers' log-likelihood, on how many throws they have the attack take something else, and the answer decided once it fits 3 better than the other (about 20 to 1) on 5 or more of those throws (`probe.DECIDE_THROWS`). Where they have it take the same on every throw, it says it can't tell.
- `SEARCH_TOP0` and `SEARCH_FLOOR0` fitted to every throw from the ground (the values within 1.92 of the best, and how reach's value and the logs' 21 do), and `SHOT_H` to every throw (a jump raises the line by `reach.air_rise`), each by the line in force (`reach.LINE`, or `probe score --line level|v83`, the questions but `LINE` and the score's header going by it too): a throw whose star the line stops took nothing, so its miss says nothing of the window. Until 2026-10-21 `SHOT_H` was fitted by the level line whatever reach had: where only a floor stops the line, nothing bounded it.
- The claw probes: the target's hits by how far off it was, each the claw's or a star's.

**Checked in the simulator** (`tests/test_probe.py`; scenarios `probe_top`, `probe_step_up` and `probe_air`). Every probe was run in a world with reach's answer and in one with the other (the simulator's world goes by `reach`, so the other claw is `reach.CLAW`, the other line `reach.LINE`, the other air pick `sim.world.AIR_PICK`), 20 throws each (claw-edge, step-down and hanging-wall 40, claw-down 80, as in game), at a lone monster walking its platform. From each log the scorer decides on the world's answer, on seed 1 and on each of seeds 1-8 but one run (below), and never on the other (monsters walking the game data's speeds, a pig 125 units/s):

| Probe | Question | World with reach's answer: lead (throws they differ on) on seed 1; the least of seeds 1-8 | With the other answer |
|---|---|---|---|
| top-60 | `SEARCH_TOP0` | 29.5 (15); 26.8 (15) | 8.6 (17); 6.2 (15) |
| top-120 | `SEARCH_TOP0` | 29.1 (17); 20.2 (15) | 3.4 (15); 2.6 (10): seed 5 undecided |
| claw-edge (40 throws) | `CLAW_FAR` | 8.3 (13); 8.3 (13) | 16.7 (14); 5.3 (20) |
| claw-down (80 throws) | `claw_takes` | 7.1 (78); 6.8 (77) | 23.8 (80); 10.1 (80) |
| below-42 | `RAMP_BELOW` | 22.9 (14); 18.8 (11) | 7.1 (17); 5.4 (18) |
| step-down (40 throws) | `LINE` | 5.6 (9); 5.0 (10) | 35.7 (18); 20.0 (10) |
| hanging-wall (40 throws) | `LINE` | 16.7 (29); 16.7 (29) | 32.4 (26); 25.0 (20) |
| step-up | her group's walls | 6.1 (20); 6.1 (20) | 40.1 (20); 40.1 (20) |
| other-wall | other groups' walls | 40.1 (20); 40.1 (20) | 6.1 (20); 6.1 (20) |
| air | `in_area` | 31.2 (20); 31.2 (20) | 6.1 (20); 6.1 (20) |

The leads are smaller where the world's answer is "not taken" (the logs' top, the ramp, the v83 line, every wall, the v83 air pick: a run of no hits only slowly rules out "taken"), and largest where it hits what the other answer can't. The one run left undecided: top-120 in the logs' top's world on seed 5 (2.6, on 10 throws). A monster walking 125 units/s moves 5 units in 40 ms, and at an answer's edge that's enough: taking the positions of the look after the key as the pick's, claw-edge in the old claw's world on seed 2 leaned 2.9 the stabs' way, where pigs walking away from her seen 120-125 off (inside the old box's 124, so a swipe by it) showed a star's damage: at the pick they were 125-128 off, the simulator's look coming 20 ms before it (its monsters are shown as they were 40 ms before its time). Moved back by the looks' times instead (4 units), it leaned 14.7 that way; taken as anywhere from 40 ms before the look to 40 ms after it, the old box is decided by 5.3. With claw-edge's 20 throws seed 1 was decided in both worlds on 5 throws that differ, the least that decides; it makes 40, as in game. Before monsters walked the game data's speeds (pigs 70 units/s), three runs were left undecided, on 1-4 throws; at the rebase, five, and one leaned the wrong way. below-42 also asks the claw off her level, but its few throws at pigs within 99 lean the stabs' way by 0.8-2.6 in reach's world (decided on none), and decide the swings in a world with them on 2 of the 8 seeds (seeds 5 and 6; seeds 3 and 7 lean 0.2-0.8 the stabs' way), on none the other way: claw-down's 80 throws decide it on all 8. With the game's errors (the harness's preset `game`: stars that miss, walks that stop early or late, lags spread, lost taps, her dot to whole pixels, hit monsters hidden 1-3 s, sightings missed) 140 of the 160 runs are decided (145 before step-down and hanging-wall threw past where the v83 line lets a star through, 2026-10-21: their 32 runs decide 25, the level line's world on step-down only 4 of 8; 129 before the slack, claw-edge's 40 throws and claw-down's throws by where a pig will be), none the other way, and four lean it, by up to 2.0 (below-42 in reach's world on seed 4; step-down on three, by 0.2-1.5; four by up to 2.9 before): in game a probe may take more throws than these, and `probe score` reads every run's in the log. `tests/test_probe.py` runs claw-down with them in both worlds. From a jump the scorer takes either height the attack may start at as likely (21 or 26 up for a jump throw): taking only the later, as `reach.air_rise` does, two throws in the v83 world at a snail that had walked 87-92 off by the pick, which the world's attack (21 up) takes, cost seed 2 its decision. Also:
- 30 throws from 13 at a lone pig on 12 (`probe_top`, seed 1): 27 hit, 114-220 off at the pick; the other three were at the pig walked to 90-110 off by the pick, under the 111 a star picks it from (of seeds 1-16, misses come only at 90-110 off and hits only from 112). The top fits 41-45; the logs' 21 is 38.4 worse (39.6 before the game's 30 ms step). In a world with the logs' top: 7 hit (193-222 off), the top fits 21-25, and 44 is 14.8 worse.
- 20 throws from 14 at an Orange Mushroom on 17: the floor fits 10-14 (reach has 12); in a world whose floor is 20, 15-40.
- From 4 at an Orange Mushroom on 2 (`probe_step_up`): 6 throws from the ground hit nothing and 6 jump backs all hit. Her group's walls fit 7.7 better than none, and `SHOT_H` fits 16-60.
- 12 jump throws from x 25 at a snail 44-47 off (`probe_air`, the first 58; from x 30 before the game's 30 ms step, whole steps of which her step to her place now is): the area fits 18.7 better than the v83 client's pick.
- With hit monsters hidden for 1-3 s, as in the game: of 30 throws at the pig on 12 (`probe_top`'s seed 1), 20 are read as hits, 9 can't be told, and 1 is read as a miss, at the pig 109 off by the look at the pick, at the window's edge, which the world's star missed (by the looks' times the scorer can't be sure it was out: 2 of its 5 moments put the pig inside); none the world's star hit is read as a miss. Before the game's 30 ms step 19, 9 and 2, both misses 105 off. 44 still fits 33.5 better than 21 (35.5 before).
- 20 throws from 21 at a lone pig walking at her: the claw's hits in the 80-100 bins, a star's from 100 (the world swipes a pig out to 99.5); at a slime, the claw's in the 70-100 bins and a star's from 100 (93.5). From 3 at a pig on 4 (within 95 at the pick): no swipe in 80 throws; in a world with the swings, 29 of the 80 swiped, at 70-100 off.

## Found offline, not fixed yet

None left from the refactor (each fixed below). Found since (2026-10-20, in the harness's runs):
- **The simulator can knock her out of the map at the basin's right corner.** A touch's knockback from the air near the right end of 36 (x 951, where the wall up to 35 meets the floor) carried her past the wall's bottom end, which `physics.fly` lets her slip under at the corner: her arc crossed x 951 just below y 576, past 36's end, with no floor below. She then fell forever at x 1479 (hunt loop, seed 10, 128 s into the run: 13.8 EXP a minute instead of 124-126). The simulator now flies her by the engine (`body.fly`), which lands a step that goes through a platform's surface before its end, so that corner holds (Motion planning: its arcs and the planner's).
- **Then the navigator never gave up getting off a "rope".** Placed off every platform, it logged "on a rope at x 1477: getting off" every 5 s for the rest of the run, and the hunt loop's own backstops (`STUCK_S`) never ran, since the trip never returned. The simulator now ends the run when she falls out of the map (`SimFellOut`, which the harness counts as a death), so it no longer gets there; in game a misread position could still do the same.
- **In the simulator the basin's cliffs let her through, and nothing is under them.** In the air from another block (27, 31, 33 and the others over the basin), the walls rising from 36's ends to 34 and 35 are another foothold group's and don't stop her (`body.OWN_GROUP_WALLS`), and past them nothing is under her: she falls below the map's lowest floor (`SimFellOut`, which the harness counts as a death). Whether the game lets her through them is unmeasured: the six telemetry flights through another group's wall all went through the hanging walls of floating blocks, none through a cliff with nothing under it (and through one, players would fall out of this map). So is what the game does with a fall out of the map. At the merge it ended 3 of 60 harness runs over seeds 1-30 (tour seeds 5 and 25, loop 19). The stack keeps clear of it now (Fixed since: the drops, the chains, the knocked jumps, the walk-offs), and none of the 60 falls out, nor any of 1,056 scenario runs (seeds 1-16). Taking a wall with nothing under its far side as stopping her whatever her group would close it in the simulator, but would rest on nothing measured either.
- **Knock-offs rose with the claw's edge by size**: fights ending with her knocked off her platform, tour 93 -> 113 in 10 runs of 20 minutes, hits pushing her off one 15 -> 20 (loop 8 -> 14), with jump backs +23% and jump throws +25%. At the branch's head it's 103 (93 at master; a run has 1-30, so about 10 either way is noise). `nav_fight_up` arrives on 30 of seeds 1-48, 39 at master: all 18 failures run out of time after a touch knocks her off 3 or 4 (or off 18, 20 or 21 on the way back up), in jump throws, jump backs and throws at pigs, slimes and snails within about 90 of her. Not mitigated: whether she's knocked off more in game is to be counted there (on 3, 20 and 22, with pigs and slimes 100-136 off). With both tracks (her body by the engine, monsters pushed back by hits), `nav_fight_up` arrives on 43 of seeds 1-48; the 5 others run out of time. With the review's fixes, 36 (38 without the waits for her to land and for a knock to end): the 12 others run out of time at 180-194 s, 10 of them knocked off 3 or 4 and climbing back from 32 or the basin, which took 164-180 s on the seeds that went that way and arrived at the merge.
- **Looks in mid-jump forget drop spots with the drops still there.** A look's loot strips are put just above the ground of the platform her position is put on, and in mid-jump that's the one under her (`pose.where_dot` puts her y on it), so they sit as high above its ground as she is and find nothing; two such looks 1.5 s apart forget a pile (`DropStore.on_look`, `Looter.remember`). It's what most of the hunt loop's lost drops came to: in the basin (seed 2), 357 of the 359 piles forgotten with drops on them were forgotten by looks in mid-jump, and 152 of the 204 drops that lay their whole life on 36 had such a pile. It got worse on this branch as jump backs and jump throws grew: the loop's drops share 84% at master, 72% now, nearly all of it on 36 (seeds 2, 4, 6, 8). `looting.STANDING_ONLY` (off) lets only a look that shows ground under her feet see a spot empty. On, the loop picks up 92% of its drops but makes 7% less EXP a minute (8 seeds; two runs fell out of the map), as it then goes for piles in the basin that monsters always guard (seed 4: 7 of its 10 pile trips there ran out their 60 s); the tour is about the same (87.4 EXP a minute, 94.6%). The hunt loop's pile rules should weigh that before it's switched on.
- **The simulator's attack picks by each kind's walking height, not by the frame a monster shows** (`World.top`: a pig 46 tall, as the decisions have it). By its frames a pig standing 60 below her (38-40 tall) would be picked from the ground only from 171 units sideways, not 151, and from a jump not at all, where in game pigs 60 below were hit; and a slime mid-hop on a platform 60 above (its feet lifted 16) only from 177, not 117.
- **A drop planned a few units from another platform's end below.** A drop goes from the middle of where her platform overlaps the one she lands on, but another may lie under part of that overlap: 11 -> 20 drops from -45, 6 units from the end of 12, which is between them. Flow then can't chain it (its spot lands on 12), and the mover drops within 20 units of the move's spot: in `nav_drops` (seed 1, where the route's weights come out otherwise since the drops moved) she landed on 12 and replanned. The middle of the stretch that lands on the platform (a drop falls straight down: `platform_below`) would hold.
- **On a rope, the escort threw at monsters "in reach" for the rest of a tour** (harness, tour seed 28, with her decisions on the v83 line, `reach.LINE = 'v83'`, in a world with either line). A chain capped at its length mid-climb (from 17 by 14 and 6 up to 3, `chain ended: long`) left her on the rope by 6, and the escort (`FightOnTheWay`, `navigator._shoot_on_the_way`), from a look that put her on a platform, threw again and again: the last 260 s went by without a kill, since the world takes no attack on a rope. It's the escort's, not the line's: with the level line that seed never got there. With her decisions on the v83 line, the default since 2026-10-21, that seed makes 121 EXP a minute, its longest stretch without a kill or a pick-up 24 s, and none of seeds 1-30 has one over 60 s (the escort's bursts are capped per platform, `ON_WAY_TRIES`).

- **Chained jumps miss more on the game's steps** (harness, preset `game`, 10 seeds: What the game's steps changed, in Motion planning). Landings elsewhere with no touch near: tour 14 in 377 chains, against 6 in 317 on the 5 ms engine. A chain is planned open loop from one look: on tour seed 9 a step back on 19 held 189 ms went 41.5 units, not the 35.8 planned (the release seen 60 ms late: walk jitter and the lags' spread), the jump off 19 landed 7 units short on 18, and the run on to 18's jump up onto 16 took off at 439, where from 440 on she'd have landed on 16 (planned 451): she fell to 32. On the steps a takeoff is also up to half a step's run (2.2 units) either side of the mean, and a run on from a landing waits up to a step. Route jumps keep 12 units of slop either side of their takeoff, which errors that add up over a chain's legs can use up. Since step 2 of the live fixes (Chains on the night's live misses, below) a chained jump takes off only where it lands with 8 units of slop at its real takeoff: 7 such landings in 384 chains of the tour's 10 seeds, 3 in 175 of the loop's.

Found since, not fixed (the simulator's physics):
- **She can slip out of the map at a wall's foot.** In a 20-minute tour with the feed (harness seed 10), knocked back mid-jump at the basin's right end, she passed x 951, where the wall up to platform 35 ends at the floor's own height, fell past the floor and out of the map, and the run ended at 3 minutes ("no safe platform reachable"). The same seed with blocking looks never got there. In game a map's sides are walls of their own. It happens at both ends of the basin, and which runs meet it shifts with any change to what she does: in the hunt loop with the feed, seeds 8 and 12 once the tracker gated its poses, seed 6 with the looks' newer costs; there she hangs "on a rope" outside the map (x -492 or 1477), "getting off" every 5 s for the rest of the run (264-673 s without a kill), which the harness counts as `stuck_s`. Comparisons in this README leave such seeds out, on both sides, and say so.

Fixed since, each a deliberate change to the scenarios that pinned it (re-recorded, and their `expects` changed to pin the fix):
- **Falling out of the map by the basin's cliffs** (harness, both tracks; the simulator's cliffs: Found offline). At the merge 3 of 60 runs over seeds 1-30 fell out (tour seeds 5 and 25, loop 19), each in the air above 36 from another block, by one of its ends. Fixed one cause at a time (each fix changed the runs, and others fell out until all were in), none of the 60 falls out now:
  - A drop goes from the middle of the overlap with the platform she lands on (`route._drop_moves`), not with whichever platform came first: 27 -> 36 dropped 6 units from 36's left cliff (from 27's overlap with 2, above it) and 33 -> 36 6 from the right one, and a touch's knock as she came down took her through (tour 25, loop 19). 27 -> 36 is gone (only 12 units by the cliff land her on 36).
  - No drop comes down within 70 units of a cliff (`route.KNOCK_CARRY`): knocks carried her 58 and 62 units off drops from 31 at 185 and from 33 at 907 (loop seeds 11 and 12). 33 -> 36 drops from 881.
  - No chain is planned from a look of her in mid-jump (Control: a route): loop seed 30.
  - No jump is keyed from a look a knock may still be moving her in (Behavior: a fight). On tour seed 5 a jump throw so keyed went in once she had landed on 34 and slid on (the turn's 205 ms wait let it: such a jump key isn't always lost in the air), took her up onto 31 0.4 units from its end (that nothing was overhead was checked 55 units back, where she was at the look), and the next throw's turn tap walked her off it. In `loop_run` the jump keys lost in the air went from 21 to 9: those left come from a touch after the look, in the plan's lead-in, which no look shows.
  - A walk-off has its run-up (Control: one move at a time): knocked onto 31's end, she was sent back down to 34 from 0.4 units in and left at 35 units/s (tour seed 3).
- **Shooting down at a snail 60 below** (both tracks). The decisions counted a snail on a platform 60 units below, 150-270 units off, in reach, where the simulator's attack area couldn't pick it: in `nav_fight_basin` on seed 6 she threw 72 stars from platform 30 at a snail on 33 and none hit, and on the physics track 6 of the 7 seeds of 16 that ran out of time did that. From the ground both now go by the star's search (Attack: other platforms), which picks a snail 60 below from 246 units off, and `nav_fight_basin` meets all its checks on all of seeds 1-16. Which is right in game is unmeasured (a pig 60 below was hit in game).
- **The probes keep their geometry** (`probe_air`, `probe_step_up`, both tracks). `probe_air` failed "every one kills the snail" on 6 of seeds 1-16 and `probe_step_up` "jumping back, every star does" on 3, none on the attack track. A run starts with her facing unknown, so the first throw turned her, and the turn's tap walked her 8 units on toward the snail's spawn, where it came back within reach of her (261 touches over the 16 seeds, 77 on the attack track); a knock then had the next jump throw keyed mid-knock, its jump lost. And a jump back carries her 25 units out by the pick, past a star's reach of targets at the probe's far end. Now she faces the way she throws at her place, doesn't throw from a look of a knock, and a jump back's range ends 25 nearer (Probes to run): all 48 runs of the three probe scenarios on seeds 1-16 meet their checks.
- **Resting at very low HP now reaches a safe platform** (`tour_rest_hp_low`). After a stop ended on "HP low" (below `--low-hp`), `Navigator.go`'s first health check ended the trip to rest at once, so she sat down where she was, among the monsters, and in the simulator died there. The trip to rest now checks only for potions (`Hunter.drink_if_low`): she drinks on the way, shoots what's in her way, and rests on the safe platform.
- **Drops noted during a loot trip are kept** (new scenario `tour_collect_loot_on_the_way`; `tour_run`, `tour_hunt_from_safe_spot`). `Tour.collect_loot` set the remembered drops to the rest of its list after each trip, so the drops of monsters shot on the way were forgotten (only a later whole-screen look found them again). They're now remembered for the next collection (or a fight's sweep) rather than gone after in the same one: going after them at once, every kill on a trip made another trip, and in the simulator three tour scenarios ran out of time shooting respawns. Collections are also capped (`LOOT_SECONDS`, 180 s: no trip starts later than that into one), because where respawns guard the drops, as on 18 below platform 16, going back for them never ended. In `tour_run` one trip now goes to a kept drop first; in `tour_hunt_from_safe_spot` the last collection goes after the kept drops on 18 until the cap (115 s more, 6 more items picked up, 15 more kills).
- **Kills made shooting a monster in the way of a move count in the trip's kills** (`nav_fight_basin`: 16 -> 18, all of the world's 18; `tour_run`: 74 -> 76 on the way). `FightOnTheWay.clear_way` now returns its kills, as `shoot` does.
- **`tour --seed` seeds the navigator's route choices too**, not just the sweep order, as the simulator does (no scenario goes through the command; a test pins it).

Fixed on 2026-10-19 as step 1 of `docs/hunt-modes.md`, one change each, before the new hunt loop, so it isn't credited with them:
- **The report adds up HP lost** (`tour_hunt_basin`, `tour_hunt_noisy`, `tour_run`): `Report.add` left `damage_taken` out, so every stop said "HP lost: 0%".
- **Every kill counts**: the trips to rest and to stand by, and the fights guarding drops, now add their kills to the tour's `on_the_way` (`tour_rest`: 0 -> 7 on the way; `tour_run` now reports all 114 of the world's kills).
- **No jump or attack inside the attack lock**: every plan that presses jump or attack is held back just enough that the key comes after her last attack's 730 ms lock (`Actions.hold_back`, used by `Actions.play`, so the mover's single moves too, and by the navigator's chains). Pressed inside the lock, a jump is ignored. This happened in 4 scenarios, by 2 to 494 ms (`nav_fight_up`, `nav_fight_across`, `tour_hunt_from_safe_spot`, `tour_hunt_platform_12`). In `nav_fight_across` the timing then differs enough that she isn't touched on her way to 18.
- **The mover's walks press pick-up** (the walk to an x after a trip, the walk before a jump up or a drop), as `Actions.walk` does, every 150 ms and through the slide after. Walks last about 0.2 s longer. 17 scenarios changed; `tour_run` picks up 189 items instead of 152, and the basin ends `tour_hunt_basin` with no drops left. `nav_monsters_staircase` moved to seed 1, because with seed 3 no monster was in her way to hop any more.
- **A trip to drops gets there** (`tour_hunt_basin`, now pinned to have no "couldn't get there (timeout)"). On the platform she's going to, the navigator no longer fires bursts at whatever is in reach (a loot trip to the basin's own drops used to spend its whole 60 s on that). It only shoots a monster between her and the x she's sent to (`clear_way`, at most 3 fights in a row), then walks there. 8 scenarios changed. `tour_hunt_from_safe_spot` now needs just over 400 s, so its limit is 600 s.

In the simulator's game-like world (`python -m gamelab.sim.harness`, preset `game`, 10 seeds of 20 minutes), these fixes took today's tour from 90 to 85 EXP a minute. It now picks up 95% of its drops instead of 94%, and loses less HP (2.6 instead of 2.8 bars). Its loot trips run out their 60 s 31 times instead of 64, but clearing the way to a drop now hits its 3-in-a-row cap 52 times instead of 11.

**The hunt loop in the simulator** (`tour --loop`, step 5 of `docs/hunt-modes.md`; not tried in game yet). Against the fixed tour above, preset `game`, 20 simulated minutes a seed:

| | tour | loop |
|---|---|---|
| EXP a minute, 10 seeds (20 seeds) | 84.8 (85.7) | 114.2 (117.7) |
| better on | | 9 of 10 (19 of 20) seeds |
| worst seed | | -35% (seed 7, both) |
| drops picked up a run | 185 (195) | 250 (266) |
| share of her drops picked up | 95.3% (95.6%) | 95.2% (95.8%) |
| HP lost (bars of 1.4%) | 2.6 (2.7) | 2.5 (2.5) |
| deaths | 0 | 0 |

With `--think 1` (the stack's real computing time charged): 80.8 -> 116.8 EXP a minute, better on all 10 seeds, 95.8% of drops: it passes the whole bar. With the knobs on, it misses one point: seed 7 (start on platform 24), 48 EXP a minute against the tour's 74. There it hunts 17 from the spot on 16, which pays best by the rate rule, but is knocked off it again and again, and the climbs back and the trips for piles on the way keep it from judging the ground (leaving is decided only when no other rule fires). Seed 7 swung between 44 and 85 EXP a minute across variants of the loop that differ in nothing it should care about; the share of drops picked up also moves by about 3 points between such variants (one started 0.2 s later). In the crowded `plain` world: 133 -> 208 EXP a minute and 336 drops a run against 299, but only 69% of its drops: with monsters always in reach, shooting comes first and piles are gone for only when about to vanish or when the ground empties.

**`--rest-avoid`** (step 7; `python -m gamelab.sim.harness rests`): 40 trips to rest at 28% HP from the hunting grounds, preset `game`. Keeping clear of monsters, she lost 1.6% HP a trip instead of 6.2%, and got there in 6.1 s instead of 28.5 s, with no deaths either way. The trips that shoot their way through made 120 kills on the way; the avoiding ones made none. It stays an option until it's checked in game.

**Jump-throws at snails** (found in game on 2026-10-19, fixed on 2026-10-20; see Attack). On the 168-unit platforms 3, 20 and 22 a snail cornered her and she jump-threw at it: of 45 throws at most one hurt it, and the stars hit pigs, slimes and Orange Mushrooms on the platform 60 above. The simulator hid it: it resolved an attack when its star left (she had landed by then), from wherever she was then, hitting anything on her level at any distance. Now:
- `stance` decides close quarters by the nearest monster's kind: a jump back or a jump throw only if its attack starts low enough to pick that kind (`reach.air_hits`, from `physics.JUMP_ATTACK_MS` and `JUMP_BACK_ATTACK_MS`). A snail is shot from the ground: from 125 away if she can step back that far (a star), else from where she is (the claw swipes it). Checked in game on 2026-10-20: an attack keyed 40 ms after the jump hits snails (a star for 78, the snail dead), so `JUMP_ATTACK_MS` is 40, and cornered by a snail she jump-throws again.
- A fight's targets in the path of a jump throw (what a kill is matched against) come from her height when the attack starts: the platform above as far as a star reaches, and not a snail beside her. EXP from up there was taken for the snail's: on 2026-10-19 "snail down" was logged 3 times while the snail lived on, and the snail, then left alone as a dying one, twice ended the fight that way.
- The simulator picks an attack's target when it starts (`World._aim`), by the monsters' bodies: on the ground the claw's swing first, then the attack's area, from where she is then, in the air or not; hit monsters flinch taller for 0.6 s. New scenario `fight_snail_short_platform`: with the old decisions in this simulator it passes on none of 30 seeds (on 22 the snail is still alive after 15 s, on 11 "snail down" comes while it lives), with the new ones on 18 (on the others the snail walks into her first, and a touch knocks her off the end of 22).
- In the simulator's game-like world (preset `game`, 10 seeds of 20 minutes): the tour 84.8 -> 83.9 EXP a minute (152.9 -> 142.0 kills a run), the hunt loop 114.2 -> 115.4 (190.0 -> 187.0), its worst seed (7) 48.5 -> 80.7. Far fewer snails die (in the 10 runs, tour 211 -> 117, loop 78 -> 29), and stars that went over them hit the monsters above instead, which give more EXP. The claw swipes more often (tour 966 -> 1,642 swipes): by the game data its swing reaches into bodies up to 110-125 off by their middle, where `stance` and `decide` count on a star from 90-100 units. HP lost a run 2.6 -> 2.8 bars (tour) and 2.5 -> 2.6 (loop); drops picked up 95.3% -> 93.7% (tour), 95.2% -> 94.9% (loop).
- Left as it was: the ledger (`behavior/ledger.py`, `in_path`) still matches kills against a throw's path from the ground, as it isn't told which throws were jumps; and `stance`'s and `decide`'s distances for a star (90-110) are still inside the claw's reach for slimes, pigs and Orange Mushrooms.

**Stars from platform 4 at monsters on 2** (found in the live logs of 2026-10-18 to 10-20, fixed on 2026-10-20; see Attack). On 4 she threw at Orange Mushrooms on 2, 60 above her behind the step wall joining the two, and no star got there: 0 of 3 in the shot log, 0 of 5 in a tour's telemetry, all 4 kills of 40 throws on 4 in a sweep. About 1.2-1.5% of her live throws went that way (5 of 419 in the telemetry, 3 of 204 in the shot log). The simulator hid it: its stars went past every wall. Now:
- `reach` knows what stops a star, and every decision goes by it: `stance` and `decide` (and their walks along her platform), `pick_target`, the spots (`coverage`: a spot on 4 at x 244 had 40% of 2 in reach by distance, now none), the fight's far targets, the hunt loop's rules (shoot, in passing, back to spot, better spot, a trip's interruptions), the tour's stop on the way, the escort, and kill matching (`fight._in_path`, `ledger.in_path`). `hunt look` says "behind a wall", and the mini-map's reach band stops at the wall.
- The simulator's world stops the star there too (`World.nearest_in`, by the group of the platform she last stood on), the claw's swing as well. New scenario `fight_step_wall`: on 4 at x 520 with Orange Mushrooms on 2 and nothing on 4. With the old decisions in this world she threw 23 stars in 20 s and hit nothing; now she throws none and moves on after 1.7 s.
- The shot log keeps her x, the target's x, her group and the rule's verdict (`clear`), so the probes can be scored from it. A monster seen past its platform's end is placed at the end (`Scene.place`).
- In the simulator's game-like world (preset `game`, 10 seeds of 20 minutes), against before: the world's rule alone took the tour from 89.5 to 88.4 EXP a minute and the hunt loop from 122.4 to 120.8; with the decisions too, 87.5 and 122.1. In each, 9 of 10 seeds are the same throughout. The one that differs is a run that met 4 -> 2, diverging from there (tour seed 8: 104.3, then 92.8, then 83.5, where she went up onto 2 instead and was knocked back down 10 times; loop seed 7: 71.9, 55.4, 68.5). On this map the effect is within noise; on maps with steps it should be larger.
- Not measured in game yet: the line's height (anything from 1 to 59 fits), its height in a jump (a jump throw's attack starts 26 up, its line under the 60 step's top; a jump back's 44 up, over it), whether the claw's swing is stopped too, and whether it's a level line at all and not one from her to the target's feet (they differ shooting down from 2 at 4 from far left of the step, and down past her own block's hanging wall).

**The star's search, and the angle off her level** (from the game data, meowdb and the live logs, 2026-10-20; see Attack). Off her level she judged a star's reach by one ramp for every monster (150 units sideways 60 below her, 200 above), and the simulator by a box. The logs fit the client's search by bands better, and by it the room a star needs goes by the monster's body (60 below her, an Orange Mushroom from 113 units, a slime from 177). Now:
- `reach` has the search (`search_band`, `min_dx` and `max_dx` by body, `body_of`), and her decisions go by it with each monster's kind (`can_shoot`, `can_hit`, `shot_dx`): `stance` and `decide` (with their steps away from one right under her, and a walk to one band past where a star picks it), `pick_target`, the fight's far targets, the hunt loop's rules, the tour's stop on the way, the escort and `hunt look`. Kill matching too (`fight._in_path`, `ledger.in_path`, `reach.ground_pick`): from the ground what the claw (on her level) or the search can pick, in the order it takes them; from a jump the area to 400 by a body's near side. Spots go by a pig's body (12 from 13: 60% -> 63% in reach).
- The simulator's world picks by it (`World.nearest_star`), the claw only on her level, and its stars reach 400 to a body's near side from the ground or a jump (434 for a pig by its middle; 494 before).
- Step 1's walls fit with it. Of her throws from 4 at monsters on 2 (60 above, 286-392 units off), the search alone picks one on 2 every time; with the walls, none. On the research's fits rerun against `reach`, the search scores -55.52 (shot log, off her level), -42.49 (telemetry, the nearest monster in front), -82.22 (telemetry, the whole pick) and -64.06 (her level); with the walls, -54.09 on the shot log (its 3 misses from 4 at 2) and the same on the rest, since the walls change no other pair. On the terrain research's scoring (did a throw from the ground hurt anything, 195 decided throws): the old rule -116.70, with the walls -116.40; the search -116.44, with the walls -116.24.
- In the simulator's game-like world (preset `game`, 10 seeds of 20 minutes), against before: the world alone (her old decisions) took the tour from 87.5 to 85.9 EXP a minute and the hunt loop from 122.1 to 111.3; with the decisions too, 85.2 and 121.4 (better on 3 and 5 of the 10 seeds; in the new world against her old decisions, on 4 and 5, with 3 of the loop's the same). HP lost 2.64 -> 2.62 bars (tour), 2.51 -> 2.42 (loop); drops picked up 95.9% either way (tour), 83.9% -> 79.8% (loop, nearly all of it seeds 2 and 4, where monsters are always in reach). Attacks that hit nothing: tour 666 -> 869 -> 786, loop 917 -> 1,114 -> 1,072. Most of the rise is stars at targets 400-470 off by their near side, aimed at within 410 by their middle and walked out of reach by the time the attack started: the shorter range makes the world harder, and the decisions win back part of it. Aiming within 400 instead would cut those by another 4-5% (tour 87.7, loop 121.2 EXP a minute); it's left at 410, where the range probe hit slimes at 408 and 410, until it's measured in game.
- `tour_run` moved from seed 8 to 6, and its limit to 1200 s: the basin's respawns make it chaotic (470-1190 s on seeds 1-16, before and after), and on seed 8 one EXP gain came in as the stop's fight ended, unplaced.
