**Her own platform stops a star down past its end** (2026-10-21, from the user watching her hunt live: "the step up for sure blocks the star. And for targeting below your platform, your platform itself can block the thro[w] (this applies to both step down and standing on a higher platform trying to hit mobs on a lower one)"). The step's the logs had already; the floor's is the v83 line's, so `reach.LINE = 'v83'` is the default (Attack: which line):
- What it stops on this map: from 2 at x 50 a star at a pig on 4 under 254 off, at a slime under 284; from 14 at x 690 at a pig on 17 up to 238 off; from 25 inside her platform's end, none at a monster a star picks 60 below her. Her decisions (`stance`, `decide`, `pick_target`), the spots, the hunt loop's rules, the tour's stop on the way, the escort (`FightOnTheWay`) and kill matching (`fight._in_path`, `ledger.in_path`) go by it through `reach.can_hit` and `wall_to`, and the simulator's world stops such a star (its log says at which floor).
- Where a monster the attack takes first has its star stopped, she walks along her platform to the nearest place 10 apart from where a star gets to her target past it too (`stance._clear_from` with the monsters around her: in `stance`'s step from under a monster and its closing in, and in `decide` when each of its own goals is so shielded). From 14 at x 690 a pig on 17 at 880, in her floor's shadow, takes the stars at one at 1000; from 729 on it's under 151 off and isn't picked, and she closes in to 736, where by the v83 line alone she waited.
- `decide` skips a target "a floor in the way from all along her platform" where that's what stops it (from 14, a pig on 17 at 900 is in reach only from x 747 to 749, between her floor and the search's 151), and `hunt look` says "a floor in the way", and "behind one whose star is stopped" for one behind such a monster.
- The star's hit point is taken 1 unit inside the monster's platform, as the monster is (`reach._hit_on`). The client's is past its platform's end where the shot's row ends inside a body hanging over it: from 4's right end at a pig on 2 within 22 of its end, 368-410 off, it was over the step's edge, short of the step wall (none of the logs' throws from 4 got to 2, and the user saw the step stop them); and from a jump at a slime right beside her by the basin's right side, its far edge (the row starts 65 ahead), at the wall's foot, where the world stopped the star (`loop_rest_hp_low`).
- The spots: to shoot down, the place on her platform with the most in reach is near its end (17 from 14 at x 756, was 716). `reach.clear_length` tries every star's line at once (`reach._sight_clear`, `_hits_on`, `_ys_at`, in the same arithmetic as one at a time: 12,232 coverages on the real map and the test maps came out the same), 27 ms a platform against 42 one at a time (3.6 by the level line); the hunt loop's 16 grounds take 0.67 s, once, at its first look.
- The probes: step-down and hanging-wall throw past where the v83 line lets a star through as well as short of it, 40 times; step-up is left with the line's height (Probes to run).
- Golden: 11 of the 73 traces changed, each still showing what it's there for. `probe_step_up`: its question's wording, in its log's first line. `tour_hunt_other_spot` and `tour_hunt_onto_platform`: the second pig on 17 starts at 1240, not 1320 (the spot on 14 is at 756 now, reaching 17 out to 1166, and from 1320 none of seeds 1-32 showed the first; both show on seed 6 from 1240). In the other eight her decisions first differ where her floor stops a star: `nav_fight_up` and `nav_fight_up_runs_out` (from 14 at a slime on 17 under it: no step to where the level line would have it in reach, as from all of 14 the v83 line doesn't), `nav_fight_basin` (from 30 a snail on 33 in her floor's shadow isn't shot on the way), `tour_hunt_from_safe_spot` (from 16 at a pig on 18 under it she steps 19 units, to where the line clears, not 59), `tour_run` (from 31 a slime on 34 in the shadow isn't "in reach on the way"), `tour_hunt_platform_12` (from 20 the world's floor stopped a star at a slime on 21 that had walked into the shadow since the look; it shows what it's there for, then runs out its 400 s collecting drops, where it ended at 303 s on a trip's time-out), `loop_spot_off_ground` (a kill put on another monster in the path, then a star at a pig that walked into the shadow; later an urgent pile on 17 drew her down, and a touch knocked her to 25: 683 -> 548 EXP in its 181 s), `loop_leaves_empty_ground` (going for a pile on 17 from 14 at x 691, its guard, a slime 213 off, is in her floor's shadow from all of 14, so she didn't stop to shoot it there and walked off among them: 449 -> 118 EXP in 180 s, knocked down to 25 four times).
- Harness, preset `game`, 10 seeds of 20 minutes (`line-v83a-*`), against master's (`cvm-*`): the hunt loop 126.0 -> 127.0 EXP a minute (8 seeds the same, 2 better, by 10.6% and 0.5%), drops picked up 89.1% -> 89.7%, HP lost 2.82 -> 2.87 bars; the tour 91.5 -> 90.9 (better on 5, worse on 5, +0.3% on average, standard error 4.9%, the worst -30.1%, the best +30.2%), drops 92.9% -> 94.1%, HP lost 2.69 -> 2.59. Neither passes the bar: the line is the game's, not a policy. Over 30 seeds, this branch with either line in her decisions and the world (`line30-*`): the loop 126.9 -> 127.3 (25 seeds the same), the tour 92.6 -> 90.9 (-1.3% on average, standard error 2.1%; better on 12, worse on 17). In a world whose floors stop a star, her decisions on the level line (master's) lose 198 of 18,748 stars to a floor on the tour (1.1%) and 16 of 24,083 on the loop, for 90.6 and 126.2 EXP a minute; on the v83 line 93 of 18,681 (0.5%) and 6 of 24,247, for 90.9 and 127.3. Those left are monsters that walked into the floor's shadow between the look and the pick, sightings missed (the preset's), and a kill between a throw's key and its pick that left one in the shadow to take the star.
- Found, not fixed: the hunt loop shoots a ground from wherever on its spot's platform the trip stopped with a target in reach (`_on_spot`), not from the spot's x: from 14, she landed at 705-713 and shot 17 from there, where her floor stops a star at a pig under 204 off, against 170 from the spot (736); with nothing in reach from there, she waits, where a few steps would bring some in reach. And a guard of a pile in her floor's shadow is shot from nowhere on her platform (above).
- Not measured in game: where past her platform's end the star gets through (`probe run step-down`, `hanging-wall`: by the v83 line a pig from 254 and 215 off); the hit point taken on the monster's platform.

**Monsters left half hunted** (2026-10-21, from the user watching her hunt live: "it can leave a monster half hunted and then just suddenly move on, not killing the monster. An experienced play[er] won't do this"). In the 12 hunt loops of that night (about 70 minutes of telemetry) she set off 44 times with a monster's HP bar down and it in reach, or a step of 18-99 units along her platform away: 24 travelling on, 7 for a pile on her platform, 3 each for lull loot, high ground and the next ground, 2 each for loot elsewhere and an urgent pile; 23 of those monsters weren't killed in the next 20 s. At 00:49:51, from 3 at x 714, she had hurt a pig on 4 (its bar at 27%) that stood at 584, 130 off, short of the 151 a star needs 60 below her, and she left for drops on 7. Now:
- Her wounds are kept whoever looks (`ledger.Wounds`, Behavior): a monster in a throw's path whose bar shows up or changes within 2.5 s is hers until a kill of hers is placed on it, it's out of sight 3 s, or 15 s after it was first hurt.
- The hunt loop, travelling to another platform and done shooting in passing, finishes the ones a throw from where she is or a walk of 150 along her platform gets to (rule 4b, `Hunt._owed`; `fight.owed`, `fight.finish_act`), 8 s per objective at most (`FINISH_S`); a trip stops for them before each chain (`_interrupt`). Not on her way along her own platform, nor at the ground, where shooting goes on as before: unbounded, in the simulator's basin, where respawns never stop, there was always one she had hurt in reach, and piles and spots ran out their 60 s (52 times in 30 runs of 20 minutes, against 26), 2.9 points fewer drops picked up.
- A fight (`clear`, the tour's stops and the escort's bursts) doesn't end at its kill count while one she had hurt by then is in reach or a walk of 150 away, and closes in on one with nothing else in reach, approach or not; the escort's target (`FightOnTheWay.target`) is such a one too.
- New scenario `loop_finishes_what_she_hurt`, the 00:49:51 case: one star from 3 at x 760 hurts the pig, it stands at 584, she walks to 714, and the hunt loop picks its next ground. Before, she set off 2.2 s in with the pig at a third of its HP, and killed it 43-50 s later, when she came back (seeds 1-8); now she steps back along 3, kills it at 4.6 s and then sets off, on all 8.
- In the simulator's hunt loop the rule itself seldom fires (6 s in 30 runs of 20 minutes, 10 trips stopped for it in 16): shooting in passing already takes what she's hurt in reach. In the tour, of the monsters she had hurt in the 10 s before she changed platforms of her own accord (not knocked, not on her way to rest), those she didn't kill in the 10 s after went from 645 to 408 (30 runs); in the hunt loop, from 70 to 118, with her platform changes from 445 to 628 (with the hunt loop's rule alone, 75; with the fights' alone, 103: the runs part ways at the first change, and these counts go with them).
- Golden: 24 of the 73 traces changed, each still showing what it's there for (and the new one). In the other 21 the first difference is a fight going on where it ended (a throw or a jump throw instead of the walk to its drops) or closing in on one she'd hurt instead of "nothing in reach"; three scenarios moved seed, as she now gets there: `nav_fight_up_runs_out` (4 -> 17: of seeds 1-48, 46 arrive, 41 before), `nav_fight_across_runs_out` (2 -> 15: of 1-16, 14 arrive, 12 before) and `loop_lull_loot` (1 -> 4: no lull came on 1; of 1-16, 8 meet all of it, 10 before). The ledger test's bar for kills placed right is 0.5 (its 33 runs: pooled 0.70, 0.69 before, three below 0.55).
- Harness, preset `game`, 20 minutes: the hunt loop over 60 seeds 128.3 -> 128.6 EXP a minute (better on 25), drops picked up 90.6% -> 89.9%, HP lost 3.03 -> 3.06 bars, objectives run out 58 -> 74; the tour over 30 seeds 90.9 -> 89.8 (better on 10; standard error about 2%), drops 94.2% -> 93.5%, HP lost 2.73 -> 2.78.

**Snails from the ground** (2026-10-21, from the user watching her hunt live: "snail has too small hitbox and jump throw mostly missed them. Especially on short platform, maybe just directly hit the snail or go to the opposite edge of the platform to do a no jump throw against the snail would solve the issue much quicker"). Keyed 40 ms after the jump (`physics.JUMP_ATTACK_MS`) a jump throw's attack takes a snail in by its area, and one such star killed one in game on 2026-10-20 (Attack: what an attack hits), so cornered by a snail, or too close to one for a star from the ground, she had gone back to jump throws. That night's telemetry has 78 of them at a snail on her platform: under 46 units off (in a stab's reach from the ground, short of the star's search) 5 of 26 hurt or killed it, at 46-84 off 18 of 33, at 85-130 6 of 10. RustMS's port of the v83 client, as from the ground from as high as she is (21-26 up), picks no snail 35-85 off. Now (`stance.JUMP_THROW_SNAILS = False`):
- A snail too close for a star from the ground (`close_to`: within 96) is never jump-thrown at. She steps back to where a throw from the ground is a star (`reach.star_sure`, 99), and as much farther as a snail walks at her meanwhile (43.75 units/s over the step and `SMALL_STEP_REACT_S`, 0.45 s: 30-40 units), to 40 from her platform's end at most rather than the 50 she keeps with monsters about (`SMALL_STEP_END`): on the 168-unit platforms 3, 20 and 22 that's their far end. Not to 25 (`EDGE_MARGIN`): the step's walk is planned before her last attack's lock has run out, and a touch meanwhile knocked her 40 units toward 3's end and the step of 110 took her off it (`nav_fight_up`, seed 1); of that scenario's seeds 1-48, 36 arrived with 25, 38 with 35, 41 with 40 and with 50. With no room for that, she stabs it from the ground (9-11 damage, every one a hit, each pushing it back 18 units) until it's dead or out where a star picks it. A jump back's attack picks no snail anyway.
- No step either (stepping away, closing in) from a look a touch's knock may still be moving her in (`fight.knocked_move`, which kept only jumps from such looks before): on 22, a snail's touch knocked her 40 units toward its end, and a step back of 106 from where she had been took her off it.
- Scenarios: `fight_snail_short_platform` (22, a snail 40 off, her 29 units from 22's end: no room) meets all it expects on 24 of seeds 1-32, against 11 with the jump throws, in a world whose jump throws pick the snail by their area; on most of the others the snail walks into her first and a touch knocks her off 22. New `fight_snail_far_end` (20, a snail 35 off near its left end: she steps back to 40 from the right end, and a star kills it within 6 s; 15 of seeds 1-16) and `fight_snail_stab` (3, a snail 15 off in the middle: stabs until it's pushed out where a star picks it, dead within 8 s; 16 of 16).
- Golden: 6 of the 74 traces changed, each still showing what it's there for, and the two new ones. In `nav_fight_short`, `fight_snail_short_platform`, `nav_fight_up` and `tour_hunt_platform_12` the first difference is at a snail too close: a stab (a throw from the ground) or a step back where she jump-threw. `nav_fight_up_runs_out` (17 -> 18) and `nav_fight_across_runs_out` (15 -> 7) moved seed, as she now gets there: of seeds 1-48 and 1-16, 41 and 12 arrive (46 and 14 with the jump throws, in this world, whose jump throws' area picks a snail: its 3 has one where `nav_fight_up` passes).
- Harness, preset `game`, 20 minutes, against finishing what she hurt (the entry above): the hunt loop over 60 seeds 128.6 -> 128.9 EXP a minute (better on 22), drops 89.9% -> 90.1%, HP lost 3.06 -> 3.01 bars; the tour over 30 seeds 89.8 -> 90.4 (better on 14), drops 93.5% -> 94.0%, HP lost 2.78 -> 2.81, and it fell out of the map once (seed 17, knocked past 36's left end at x 128 by a slime's touch: the basin's cliffs, Found offline). Snails give 2 EXP, and the world's jump throws pick them, so it can't show what the stabs and steps back save in the game, where jump throws at a snail close by mostly missed.
- Not measured in game: whether jump throws at snails pick them at all (`probe run air`), and how long the stabs and steps back take her on 3, 20 and 22.

**Shooting from 22 at 21** (2026-10-21, from the user watching her hunt live: "I also find the attack seldomly attack mobs on platform 21 from platform 22"). 21 (-399..-51) is 60 above 22 (-39..129), and its right end 12 units left of 22's left end. What there is to it:
- The spots aren't it: 22 is 21's spot (x 16, 86% of 21 in reach by the client's window, which takes a pig 60 above her from 111 units sideways, a slime from 117, a snail from 126), one she can stand on (only snails spawn there), and no wall of 22's block crosses a star's line up to 21.
- Nor her decisions on 22 when she stands there: in that night's telemetry she was on 22 with monsters on 21 in view in 65 looks, and threw at those in reach in all but a few. The rest: one under the search (77-117 units sideways: 94, 77, 103 and 113), or a snail on 22 close to her that she was jump-throwing at (Snails from the ground, above). Of the 3 times she went on from 22 with one on 21 in view, 2 had it under the search, and she jumped up onto 21 beside it: the way on from 22 jumps onto 21 at its right end, from 22's left part, where those near that end are 37-67 units along.
- Mostly she seldom stood there. Of the 49 grounds the hunt loop chose that night, 21 was none (4 18 times, 36 13, 17 14, 1 4): by the value map (`lab/grounds.json`) its 12 visits, all the fixed tour's stops (none timed), gave 15 EXP each, and with no visit timed its rate is taken over 60 s: 0.24 EXP a second, against the basin's 1.17 and 4's 0.91. That's the value map's to learn (it will as it's visited), not changed here.
- Now in passing (rule 4) a wanted monster above her that a step of 150 at most along her platform brings out from under is a reason to stop a trip and shoot (`Hunt._pass_step`, stance's step from under): from 22, a step right of 40-76. Only above her: with those below her too, in the simulator's hunt loop she picked up 88.5% of her drops against 89.0% (60 runs of 20 minutes), shooting in passing longer. New scenario `loop_shoots_21_from_22`: on 22 at x -10 on her way up to 18, a pig and an Orange Mushroom on 21 near its right end, 76-80 along, under the search. Before, she jumped up 0.4 s in on all of seeds 1-8 (the first kill 11-42 s in, or none); now she steps out, the first kill comes 3.5-5.5 s in, and she goes on at 9.1-9.4 s, on all 8.
- Golden: 5 of the 76 traces changed, each still showing what it's there for, and the new one: in `loop_lull_loot` a step out from under 12's monsters from 13; in `loop_finishes_what_she_hurt` one more throw at the pig in passing; in `loop_rival` a throw comes where she picked up, in `feed_loop_rival` a jump back where she threw, and in `loop_leaves_empty_ground` the log names one more monster: the in-passing rule weighs every wanted monster in view, not just its targets.
- Harness, preset `game`, 20 minutes, against snails from the ground: the hunt loop over 60 seeds 128.9 -> 128.2 EXP a minute (better on 32), drops 90.1% -> 89.0%, HP lost 3.01 -> 2.95 bars, and a fall out of the map at the basin's right corner on seed 16 (x 952: the basin's cliffs); the tour as before (it has no such rule).
- Not measured in game: how often she now stands on 22 with 21's monsters in reach (the value map sends her there seldom).

**A kill's pile where its monster was seen last** (simulator, 2026-10-21). The ledger put a kill on the monster as the look before the throw had it, and its pile there: a slime shot from 16 walked 90 units along 17 before it died, its drops fell past the pile's 60 units (`LOOT_REACH`), and the look that saw them took them for someone else's (not gone for for 30 s, `hunt.OTHERS_AFTER`) while the pile was soon seen empty. Now what can have taken a throw's attack is followed from look to look, as her wounds are (`Ledger.on_look`), and a kill goes where its monster was seen last.
- In the simulator's hunt loop (preset `game`, 16 seeds of 20 minutes), the kills placed 47 units from where their monster died at the median (p75 102, 39% over 60), now 43 (p75 91, 34%). What's left is mostly a monster she hit hiding under its damage numbers (the preset's 1-3 s): its last sighting is that old.
- Golden: 17 of the 77 traces changed, each still showing what it's there for: a kill placed on another monster or elsewhere, then the piles and the wounds it closes go otherwise (from `nav_fight_basin`'s closing in at 136.9 s to `loop_lull_loot`'s 27 -> 46 drops picked up). Two scenarios moved seed: `nav_fight_up` (1 -> 3: on 1 her bursts from 14 at 17's monsters went on otherwise from its third kill, and the trip ran out of time; 40 of seeds 1-48 arrive, 41 before) and `tour_run` (1 -> 2: one of its 225 kills went uncounted; over seeds 1-16 12 meet all of it, 15 before, the 4 others missing 1 of 185-227 kills in the count).
- Harness, preset `game`, 20 minutes, against shooting from 22 at 21 (the entry above): the hunt loop over 60 seeds 128.2 -> 128.6 EXP a minute (better on 28, worse on 31), drops 89.0% -> 89.4%, HP lost 2.95 -> 3.00 bars, objectives run out 87 -> 68, no fall out of the map (1 before); the tour over 30 seeds 90.4 -> 90.5 (18 the same, 6 better, 6 worse), drops 94.0% -> 93.7%, HP lost 2.81 -> 2.74.

**Valuable drops at once** (2026-10-21, from the user watching her hunt live: "Expensive items should attempt to pick immediately"). Every pile was worth 1 to the hunt loop. Now each drop is worth what a shop pays for it, the game data's own price (`info/price` in Item/Etc and Item/Consume, read by item id; `looting.PRICES`, checked against the data by a test): Squishy Liquid 6 mesos, Slime Bubble 50, Leather 10, Pig's Head 7, Pig's Ribbon 10, Orange Mushroom Cap 8, Snail Shell 1, the Red, Orange, White and Blue Potions 5, 15, 35 and 22 (they restore 100, 250 and 500 HP and 200 MP, which doesn't add to them: her HP key is bound to Meat, and the regular attack takes no MP). Mesos by their coin: this map's monsters drop 10-20, a bronze coin, 15; a gold coin 75. The drop store keeps what the last look that saw a pile saw in it, what that's worth all told and its dearest drop (`Pile.items`, `worth`, `dearest`; one not seen yet, a pile's coins). Of the drops seen where her kills died that night (2-12 s after, within 60 units), a Slime Bubble lay in 5 of the 52 slimes' piles seen, Squishy Liquid in 34, coins in 39; nothing seen was worth more.
- A pile with a drop worth 30 mesos or more on its own (`hunt.VALUABLE`: a Slime Bubble, a White Potion, a gold coin), 15 s of travel away at most (`VALUABLE_TRAVEL`), is gone for at once (rule 6a), right after close quarters: ahead of the high ground, shooting, shooting in passing and finishing what she hurt, and on the way there only close quarters and her HP stop her. By its dearest drop, not its worth all told: two pigs' coins and leather lying together in the simulator's basin (50) took her off shooting there 8 times in 3 minutes (`loop_basin`). Twice per pile at most (`VALUABLE_TRIES`): a monster by it leaves it to later (`Looter.collect`), and from where it lies she may not shoot that one.
- Once a kill's drops of hers on another platform have landed, her next look is at the whole screen and every drop in view (`hunt.FAR_DROPS_LOOK`, once for each such kill): from a spot her band's drops are her own platform's, and one on the ground she shoots was seen only once she stopped shooting. About 120 ms more than the band's look in game (What a look costs).
- The piles she goes for when leaving and in a lull, the most worth per second of travel first (`Hunt._best_pile`), where they were the nearest.
- The simulator can drop what was seen in game (`Realism.drops`, `world.GAME_DROPS`: a slime's coins 75% of kills, Squishy Liquid 65%, a Slime Bubble 10%, a Red Potion 4%; a pig's coins 80%, Pig's Head 7%, Leather 2%; an Orange Mushroom's coins and cap 70% each; a snail's coins 63%, shell 47%, a Red Potion 11%; drawn from a generator of their own, the default world's mesos and a pig's leather unchanged), the harness's preset `game-drops`; and the harness weighs the drops picked up by their worth (`value_share`, `value_per_min`).
- New scenario `loop_valuable_drop_at_once`: on 17's spot on 16, a slime and two Orange Mushrooms on 17, the slime dropping a Slime Bubble. On seeds 1-16 she sets off for it before the mushrooms are dead on all 16, and picks it up 11.3 s after the slime's kill at the median (7.5-33.7); without the rule, on none, 17.2 s (9.6-35.1), shooting the mushrooms from 16 first; without the whole-screen look, on 12.
- Golden: 6 of the 77 traces changed, each still showing what it's there for, and the new one. In all six the first difference is the whole-screen look once a far kill's drops landed (the simulator's takes 0.25 s more than the band's), which puts what follows 0.15-0.65 s off (earlier where it stood in for one the loop would have taken anyway): `loop_spot_off_ground`, `loop_leaves_empty_ground`, `loop_lull_loot`, `loop_finishes_what_she_hurt`, `loop_shoots_21_from_22`, `loop_rest_hp_low`. In the default world no drop is worth 30.
- Harness, preset `game`, 20 minutes, against a kill's pile where its monster was seen last (the entry above): the hunt loop over 60 seeds 128.6 -> 128.6 EXP a minute (+0.3% paired, standard error 0.9%; better on 26), drops 89.4% -> 88.9%, HP lost 3.00 -> 3.04 bars, objectives run out 68 -> 61. In `game-drops`, 60 seeds, the rule off and on: 130.1 -> 129.8 EXP a minute (-0.1% paired, standard error 0.6%), drops 90.5% -> 92.4%, their worth 90.4% -> 92.9%, 149.2 -> 153.7 mesos' worth a minute; without the whole-screen look, 127.7, 92.3% and 150.9.
- Not measured in game: what other players pay (a market's prices aren't a shop's); what drops these monsters have that the detector can't see (equipment, scrolls: Limits); how long the whole-screen look after each far kill holds her up.

**Piles on the way** (2026-10-21, from the user watching her hunt live: "A lot of times the player can make a small detour to pick up the loot when moving to another place, but right now it just move away without picking up the loot, then when approaching time to expire it goes a long detour across the map to pick up the about-to-expire loot"). In the 12 hunt loops of that night (about 70 minutes of telemetry) she made 44 trips for a pile about to vanish (rule 6), 668 s in all, 27 of them of 10 s or more (585 s: from 16 to 1 for 94 s, from 3 to 21 for 51 s, from 3 to 34 for 43 s); 23 of the 44 (280 s) were for piles a trip of hers had passed before with a stop at them costing 2 s or less on top of the trip (the planner's times, below), 15 of them 0.7 s or less, the stop alone: they lay on her route. At 01:13:51 she shot a pig and a slime on 21 on her way to 3 and set off before their drops had landed; 16 s later her route took her back through 21, 43-99 units from them, and on; 48 s later she went back for them from 14, 19 s. A pile on her platform within 150 she took in passing (rule 5), but a chain of moves doesn't stop where it passes one.
- The planner says how long to stand at an x (`Planner.arrive`: the quickest of the routes landing anywhere on the platform, with the walk along it from where each lands; `travel` ends wherever she lands).
- Rule 5b (`Hunt._detour`): travelling, with no target, wound or pile here first, she goes for the pile whose stop costs the least for its worth, if it costs 2 s at most on top of the trip (`DETOUR_S`: her way to it, the wait for its drops to land if they haven't, 0.6 s on it and her way on from it to the objective's place, against her way there from here), it's 2.5 s away at most (`DETOUR_REACH`) and no monster seen stands by it (or on its platform, where she can't stand). Asked before every chain too (`_interrupt`, with 0.5 s less, so she doesn't stop for one just over). Not from a pile on the way, a valuable one or an urgent one, and 3 a trip at most (`DETOURS`); a stop shoots in passing out of the trip's own budget and leaves finishing to it.
- Bounded so: with no bound on how far the pile is, one near the end of a long trip made the whole trip its own while the trip's clock ran; in the simulator's hunt loop (preset `game`, 60 runs of 20 minutes) she picked up 89.1% of her drops for 127.8 EXP a minute, and objectives ran out their 60 s 111 times; 2.5 s away at most, 90.1%, 128.3 and 99 (4 s: 89.8%, 128.0, 99; 6 s: 89.4%, 127.4, 99); with the trip's budgets shared, 90.0%, 128.7 and 87.
- New scenario `loop_pile_on_the_way`: on 13, on her way to 17's spot on 16, coins of a kill of hers 20 s old on 19, off her route (13 -> 15 -> 18 -> 16; by the planner 1.3 s more). On seeds 1-16 she picks them up 2.4 s in and is at the spot at 7.4-8.6 s (the stop took 3 s in all, with the pick-up's look for what's left, `Looter.mop_up`); without the rule she's there at 4.4-5.7 s, and picks them up on 11, 11.8-27.0 s in, on a trip back.
- In the simulator's hunt loop (60 runs of 20 minutes, preset `game`), her trips for piles about to vanish to another platform than hers went from 3.2 a run (91 s) to 1.9 (58 s), those of 10 s or more from 2.4 (88 s) to 1.5 (56 s); all such trips from 21.2 a run (518 s) to 19.2 (482 s), most of them on the basin's own floor, among its monsters (the basin is hunted on itself). The stops on the way: 5.1 a run, 58 s.
- Golden: 4 of the 78 traces changed, each still showing what it's there for, and the new one; in each the first difference is a stop on the way: `loop_lull_loot` (piles on 12 on her way back up to 13: 31 -> 49 drops picked up, 23 -> 5 left), `loop_finishes_what_she_hurt` (the pig's drops on 4, then on: 2 -> 4), `loop_shoots_21_from_22` (the drops on 21 on her way up: 6 left -> 0) and `loop_rest_hp_low` (coins on 36 on her way down from 32).
- Harness, 20 minutes, against valuable drops at once (the entry above): preset `game`, the hunt loop over 60 seeds 128.6 -> 128.7 EXP a minute (+0.4% paired, standard error 0.8%; better on 25), drops 88.9% -> 90.0%, HP lost 3.04 -> 3.03 bars, objectives run out 61 -> 87; preset `game-drops`, 129.8 -> 127.1 (-2.3%, standard error 1.5%; one run fell out of the map off 33 into the basin's corner at 330 s, -75%: without it -1.1%, the median -0.3%), drops 92.4% -> 93.1%, their worth 92.9% -> 93.4%, 153.7 -> 151.5 mesos' worth a minute. All three steps against shooting from 22 at 21, preset `game`: 128.2 -> 128.7 EXP a minute (better on 27 of 60), drops 89.0% -> 90.0%, HP lost 2.95 -> 3.03.
- Not measured in game: how much a stop on the way costs her there (the planner's 0.6-2 s, 3 s in the simulator with the look after it), and how often she still makes a long trip for a pile left elsewhere: count the 'urgent pile' trips in the telemetry again (`hunt` records).

**Jumps up a step taking off too early** (2026-10-21, from the user watching her hunt live: "I found the motion plan seems to jump too early from the edge of a platform, especially the short ones, causing not fulfilled plan"). In that night's telemetry, each chained running jump matched against where her dot was next seen standing, 40 of 425 didn't end where planned. Her dot put 10 of those on 25, 420 units below 14, whose x the stacked platforms 3, 6, 14, 25 and 30 share (localization); 26 of the other 30 were up a step of the map's staircases, 60 up onto the next platform, whose end is 12 units across from hers (32 -> 29 and 29 -> 28 seven times each, 19 -> 18 six, 3 -> 1 and 22 -> 21 twice, 31 -> 29 and 18 -> 16 once), and every one came down short of it. A running jump took off 33 units from the end by the route (`JUMP_FROM`), and flow took off up to 12 further back (`flow.JUMP_FROM_FAR`; its spread is drawn squared, mostly 2-4): up a step that lands her 16 units onto the platform, 4 from 45 back. Of the 293 jumps up a step, those taking off 33-36 back missed 4 times in 117, 36-39 back 5 in 75, 39-42 back 9 in 47 and 42-45 back 8 in 36; by the landing planned, those to land under 12 in from an end missed 16 times in 102, 12-20 in 9 in 130, 20 or more in 1 in 61.
- Each running jump takes off where it lands with the most room near where it was found (`route._placed`: within 30 units, and 15 in from the end she jumps off at least, `TAKEOFF_EDGE`): 20 units in or more (`TAKEOFF_ROOM_WANTED`) whether she leaves the ground on time, 12 late, or 10 (flow's spread, `TAKEOFF_SPREAD`) or 22 early; else the most under the worst of those; else under 12 early or late alone (`TAKEOFF_SLOP`, as `sure_landing` has it). Up a step: 15 back, landing 34 in, 24 taking off flow's 10 earlier and 12 with 12 more. That's 19 pairs of platforms (3 -> 1, 32 -> 29, 13 -> 12...). The others: jumps up onto an overlapping platform from along it, landing by its near end, from 97 back, not 125 (11 -> 10, 12 -> 11, 20 -> 19, 21 -> 20, 27 -> 26: 42 in, not 14), the three down across a gap onto 15, 20 and 24 from 69 back, not 65 (22 in, not 18), 4 -> 2 from 35, not 33, and the jumps from 1 up onto 0; those landing 20 in whatever the spread keep their takeoff.
- A jump off an end and one found along the platform that now take off at the same spot are one move: 226 moves, 81 of them jumps, are 200 and 55. Each starts its run-up 20 units before it (`RUN_UP`); it was 7 for the jumps from along a platform, and the mover, keying its least run of 12 from there, took off 5 units late. Building the moves takes 0.18 s, not 0.07 (5,726 landings flown, not 3,128).
- Flow's own spread is unchanged (`Flow._jump` takes off up to 10 units before a move's takeoff; `JUMP_FROM_FAR` is for a jump without one, which the route no longer makes). Flow's landing margins are the other track's (live/fixes). (Since the rebase onto it: `Flow._takeoffs` and `sure_takeoff`, and the two agree: The play track on the live fixes, below.)
- The mover's jump (`Mover._run_and_jump`) lets go of the arrow just before she lands (`flow.ARROW_LAG`), and she slides 14 units on. Held through a level jump's air time and 150 ms more, it ran her 59 units past a landing 60 up: from 20 up onto 19 she ran off 19's far end onto 24 (`move_jump_short_landing` pinned that; now she stands at 81, having landed at 67). It made no jump that night: its 14 moves were 4 climbs, 9 drops and a climb down.
- In the simulator, which only has the spread of her lags to make her take off elsewhere than planned (preset `game`, 20 minutes): moves not ending where planned, the tour (30 seeds) 53 -> 25 in 976 -> 869 chains, the hunt loop (60 seeds) 27 -> 14 in 734 -> 812; plans cut short off their path 10 -> 5 and 4 -> 1. The hunt loop over 120 seeds 129.9 -> 129.6 EXP a minute (-0.1% paired, standard error 0.7%; better on 41, worse on 42, the same on 37), drops 89.2% -> 88.6% (seeds 1-60 90.0% -> 88.0%, 61-120 88.4% -> 89.3%), HP lost 3.00 -> 3.00 bars; one run fell out of the map at the basin's right corner (seed 65 at 671 s, x 952), none before. The tour over 30 seeds 90.5 -> 91.8 (+2.1%, standard error 1.9%; better on 17), drops 93.7% -> 94.5%, HP lost 2.74 -> 2.96 bars.
- Golden: 29 of the 79 traces changed, each still showing what it's there for. The first difference is a jump taking off nearer the end (the jump key later in a run: `tour_hunt_platform_12`, `loop_shoots_21_from_22`, `nav_fight_up`), a miss that is gone (`loop_finishes_what_she_hurt`: up 29 -> 28 she fell into the basin at 34 s, and now goes on up), the mover's jump (`move_jump`, `move_jump_short_landing`), or the route's random weights drawn in another order, with fewer moves (`nav_drops` drops 12 -> 21 at once, not through 20). Four scenarios moved seed for that: `nav_down_to_basin` 1 -> 62, `nav_walk_offs` 1 -> 36, `nav_monsters_staircase` 5 -> 10 and `nav_monsters_long_way` 12 -> 3 (each says why).
- To check in game: the jumps up a step, on new telemetry (`D:/MapleHelper-scratch/play/p4/jump_margins.py` matches each chained jump to where her dot next stands): a few short in 100, against 9 that night; and none walking off the end before the jump (15 back, she has 3 units to spare taking off 12 late).

**Ropes in mid-air: 17 from 23, and jumps off a rope** (2026-10-21, from the user watching her hunt live: "The motion plan also feels very fixed, e.g. it never tried to go thru platform 12. It also never went to platform 17 via two rope hops from platform 23"). Of the map's 8 ropes, two had nothing to do with 23. The one under 17's block at x 959 (-240 to -83, its bottom 58 above 23, nothing at its top) had no move at all. The one from 17 at 1040 (down to -95, 70 above 23) had only its climb down: it hangs 11 units past 23's right end, and the route took a rope only from a platform under it. So nothing went up the map's right side from 23, 25, 30 or the basin but the long way round by its left side: 23 -> 17 was 15 moves, 18.6 s in the simulator, and 36 -> 17 14 moves, 18.7 s.
- A rope hanging just past a platform's end (`route.GRAB_PAST`, 30 units) is climbed from it by a running jump at it, as any climb is (`_past_end`; flow chains it and the mover does it as before): she takes off `GRAB_LEAD` (41) before the rope, to be at the top of her jump over it, if she grabs it with her feet at its bottom or above (`SURE_GRAB_REACH`, as a client that wants her feet on the rope would have it) taking off on time, and by the simulator's reach taking off 12 early or late, 10% slower or as slow as `SLOW_TAKEOFF`. Off 23 the takeoffs that catch 1040's rope are 966-1026 by the simulator's reach and 986-1014 with her feet on it; she takes off at 999. 23 -> 17 is 3.6 s, one chain, and the basin's right side goes up: 36 -> 17 in 10.5 s, 36 -> 3 (the spot for 4, the hunt loop's most made trip that night, 30 times, each 21-26 s live) 17.6 s not 21.3, 36 -> 1 19.1 not 23.6.
- Jumps off a rope sideways (`rope_jump`): onto the rope (from under it, past an end, or down from its top), up or down it to a height (`hang`), and off it with the arrow and jump at the decompile's rope jump (`side_jump`: 1.3 x 149 out, half a jump's speed up, in no foothold group), holding up for another rope she passes within `GRAB_X` of with her feet in reach and up it (`ROPE_HOPS`), or landing with room to slide to a stop (23.5 units from 194 units/s; `ROPE_JUMPS`). Each is tried from every 5 units up the rope (not within 25 of the top of one she'd climb onto a platform from) and kept from the middle of the heights from which it ends the same 15 higher or lower, and at 0.9 and 1.1 of the speed out and 0.9 of the speed up. Kept on this map: 23 -> 17 by the user's two hops (959, then 1040, jumping off 959 at -195, the middle of -240 to -150: from lower she comes down under 1040's bottom), 6 -> 16 by 798's rope and 3 -> 9 by 723's. Of the others, 20 went where a drop or a walk-off gets her quicker, and 6 -> 4 off 723's rope only from within 25 of its top: not kept. The hops are 0.8 s slower than the climb past 23's end (4.4 s in the simulator from 23 at 900), so the planner takes them when that's in a monster's way, or with `nav 17 --avoid climb`; the other two are slower than the ways round them. Their cost: getting onto the rope, the climb to the height, a look there (`ROPE_LOOK_S`), the flight and the climb up the next rope, and a risk of 0.5 s (a guess). Both flags are on by the user's word; the rope jump's speeds and the 0.3 s before she can grab a rope again (`REGRAB_S`, the simulator's) aren't measured, and the hop grabs 1040 0.37 s after leaving 959.
- The mover does a rope jump closed loop (`Mover._rope_jump`): onto the rope and up it for as long as it takes to the height, a look there by her dot's own height (snapped, it would put her on a platform within 25 of her), a climb up or down to it if that's off by more than 15, then the arrow, jump 40 ms after it, and up from just after she leaves the rope until she's over the next one's top; onto a platform, the arrow let go just before she lands. Not on the rope at the look, it jumps nothing and the navigator replans. In the simulator, with the game's errors (preset `game`'s noise), the hops took her onto 17 on 16 seeds of 16.
- Off a rope that takes her onto no platform at its top (959's) she climbs down and lets go at its bottom (`Mover.off_rope`): holding up there did nothing, and the trip ended "lost: not on any platform" (`nav_off_rope_no_top`).
- A trip to a spot to shoot a hunting ground from keeps off the ground itself (`keep_off`: `route.KEEP_OFF_S`, 8 s more a move onto it, in the navigator's routes and in the route times the hunt loop and the tour choose spots by). Up the right side the quickest way from the basin to 17's spot on 14 climbs into the middle of 17's monsters: in `loop_spot_off_ground` the transit's fights there and their knocks (onto 35, twice) kept her from the spot until its 60 s ran out. Now she goes to the spot on 16 by the left side, as before.
- Route variety, checked (the navigator's weights, each move's cost times a random 1 to 1.35 a trip, kept through its replans: the planner's choice over 200 trips each). Downhill near-equal routes take turns: 3 -> 36 6 ways, 4 -> 21 14, 12 -> 17 6, 2 -> 21 2. Uphill there was one way each (36 -> 3, 36 -> 17, 23 -> 17, 21 -> 4: the same on 200 of 200), since nothing went up the right side; now 36 -> 3, 36 -> 17 and 36 -> 1 go up it two ways (by either of the basin's ropes, about half each). Weights a move average out over a long route, so ways 12% or more apart (the map's left side up through 12, against 18 and 16) never swap; nothing else was changed there.
- Platform 12 (-399..-51, y -445): nothing missing. Into it, drops from 2 and 11, walking off 11 and the running jump up from 13; out of it, up onto 11 (straight up and running), walking off onto 13, drops to 20 and 21. It's on the quickest ways between the map's upper and lower left (2 -> 21 through it on 38% of trips by the weights, 4 -> 21 on 17%, 13 up to 11, 10, 8), and was on none of that night's trips: the hunt loop's grounds were 4, 36, 17 and 1 (12, as 21, has 9 old visits and 123 EXP in the value map, none timed), and up from the basin through it is slower than by 18 and 16 (19 -> 3: 13.9 s in the simulator against 10.1; the planner's 16.2 against 13.0). Its jump up from 13 was one of the steps taking off 33-45 back (the entry above).

**After the review of the nine entries above** (2026-10-21, a correctness review and a fidelity review, each finding checked before it was acted on):
- Her decisions took a monster's platform only when it was on hers, so for one elsewhere the v83 line's hit point wasn't taken on its platform (`reach._hit_on`), as the simulator's world and the spots take it: from 4 at 600 she threw at a pig on 2 at 207, 393 off, where the step wall stops the star. Over the whole map (118,739 places in reach, every kind) the two lines differed at 115, all from 4 at monsters within 22 of 2's end. Now `reach.wall_to` finds the monster's platform by its id (`mapdata.Platform.peers`, attached as its walls and floors are), and they differ nowhere.
- The fights' rule to finish what she has hurt (Monsters left half hunted) ran in a probe's fight off a monster close to her too (`probe._fight_off`), and finished the probe's own targets: in the simulator, `probe step-down` with an Orange Mushroom on 2 walking at her threw 5-7 stars at the probe's pigs on 4 after the fight's one kill on seeds 1-4, none in the probe's log (the probe has `reach.WALLS_BLOCK` off, so every one counted as in reach). That fight doesn't finish wounds now (`clear(finish_wounds=False)`): 0 on all four. The escort's and the tour's fights still do. (Since the rebase onto the live fixes the probe's fights are `probe._fight`, its fights off one close and its clears of her platform first, `clears_first`: neither finishes wounds.)
- Travelling, shooting in passing put back among its monsters every wanted one in view (for the step out from under one above her, from 22 at 21's), and with them this look's kill: `visible` is taken before the kill is counted, so it was still in it. She threw at it, and then at its "hidden" ghost for `fight.HIDDEN_FOR`: in `loop_finishes_what_she_hurt` 13 of her 26 stars hit nothing, 3 in a row after each kill (now 4 of 16). Now those others are only stepped out from under (`Hunt._pass_step`), never thrown at, and not one seen fading out where a kill died. Over seeds 1-8: `loop_finishes_what_she_hurt` 131 -> 100 stars, 64 -> 25 hitting nothing, 22 -> 25 kills; `loop_shoots_21_from_22` 406 -> 358 stars, 132 -> 101 hitting nothing, 79 -> 71 kills (a kill on 21 comes before she leaves 22 on all 8 either way).
- `loop_finishes_what_she_hurt` is the finish rule's (4b) again, as its expects say once more: the step back along 3 is rule 4b's (shooting in passing steps out only from under a monster above her, and the pig is below), the stars shooting in passing's. With `FINISH_S` 0 she leaves 2.2 s in on all of seeds 1-8 and the pig dies 34-51 s later; with it, at 4.6 s, before she leaves at 5.4 s.
- Golden: 7 of the 83 traces changed, each still showing what it's there for; in each the first difference is a throw at a kill or its ghost that's gone (she goes for its drops, or throws at a live one): `loop_finishes_what_she_hurt`, `loop_lull_loot`, `loop_rival` (three stars at a ghost), `loop_leaves_empty_ground` (a jump throw at a pig dead 91 to her left; 644 -> 752 EXP in the world), `feed_loop_rival` (a jump back at a dead slime), `loop_spot_off_ground` (667 -> 756), and in `loop_shoots_21_from_22` her stance's log line, "nothing in reach", before the step out.
- Piles on the way (rule 5b) were weighed by the planner's plain routes, but since trips to a spot off its ground keep off the ground (Ropes in mid-air, `keep_off`) those aren't always her way: from the basin to 17's spot on 16 the plain route goes up the right side, past 17, and the trip up the left. From 36 at x 449 a pile at 779 was 1.3 s more by the right side (taken) and is 3.8 s by the left; from 2, going to 4's spot on 3, a pile on 10 at x 51 was 5.6 s more by the plain route through 4 (skipped) and is 1.2 s on the trip's way, which keeps off 4. `Planner.arrive` keeps off what it's given as `travel` does (its searches kept per what they keep off), and `Hunt._detour` gives it the trip's (`_keep_off`) for her ways to the objective's place, from here and from the pile; her way to the pile is the stop's own trip's, which keeps off nothing. Over every start and every pile within `DETOUR_REACH` on the map (3,285-3,331 pairs a spot), against the seconds of the routes the trips take, the verdicts at 2.5 s differed at 146 pairs for the spot on 16 (74 piles taken that cost more, 72 skipped that cost less), 139 for 17's on 14 and 42 for 4's on 3 (all skipped); now at 7, 35 and 0: a route that has to go onto the ground anyway, at its 8 s, and landings just either side of the limit. No golden trace changed.
- A snail too close for a star from the ground (Snails from the ground): the step back to where a throw is a star came first wherever there was room for it, to a 168-unit platform's far end if need be, and the stab only without, where step 2 had asked for the stab first within its reach (the user: "maybe just directly hit the snail or go to the opposite edge of the platform"); nothing had compared the two. Every close snail is within the stab's reach (`close_to`, 96), so stab first means never stepping back. Measured in the simulator (preset `game`'s noise, one snail too close on her platform, a fight to its kill, the snail 20-80 units off either side; a kill not made in 20 s counted as 20): on the long platforms 1 and 34 (432 starts, seeds 1-3), stepping back first killed it in 3.0 s on average against 3.3 for stabbing first, with 183 touches against 331, so that stays. On the five short platforms (3, 20, 22, 31, 33; 1,120 starts a run), where only the far end has the room: of seeds 1-8's, in the 187 starts where it took her there, she was knocked off her platform 27 times against 9 stabbing, the kill coming in 6.5 s against 5.7 (within 4 s 25% against 16%, within 10 s 94% against 97%); seeds 9-16's were even (155 such starts: 7.2 s each, knocked off 33 against 37); in the world without the game's errors the far end was ahead by 0.07 s on average and level on knocks (58 against 57). Weak, then, and leaning to the stab: `stance.SNAIL_FAR_END = False`, she steps back within the 50 she keeps from the ends and on a short platform stabs it where she stands. In the scenarios: `nav_fight_up` arrives on 85 of seeds 1-96 against 80, `fight_snail_short_platform` meets all it expects on 68 of 96 against 71, `fight_snail_stab` kills at 4.1 s not 5.1. `fight_snail_far_end` (the step to 20's far end) is now `fight_snail_step_back`, on 34 (15 of seeds 1-16). Not measured in game: how long her snail fights on 3, 20 and 22 take and how often a touch knocks her off.

- The simulator's jump throws took in every snail their area picks (`world.AIR_PICK = 'area'`), its comment still saying the 40 ms jump throws' kills fit that and that it wasn't measured in game, where that night's hunts say otherwise: of 78 jump throws at a snail on her platform, 6 of 29 under 46 units off hurt or killed it, 20 of 36 at 46-84 and 8 of 12 at 85-130 (and 6 of 8 throws from the ground at 85-130), so the harness couldn't show what `stance.JUMP_THROW_SNAILS` saves. Neither passes the bar (EXP better on too few seeds; worst seeds -27% and -21%).
- Left as it was: the escort's fights in her way (`FightOnTheWay.clear_way`, `shoot`) still finish what she has hurt, as the user asked of her (she'd leave a monster half hunted), bounded to those hurt by the kill count's look, which was to keep the trips from running out and didn't (The play track on the live fixes, below: the hunting trips to drops ran out on the basin, and the rest trips' fights ran to their 15 s twice as often); only the probe's fight opts out, and since the rebase the fights in transit.

**The play track on the live fixes** (2026-10-21, `live/play` rebased onto master's live fixes of that night, every conflict resolved so that both sides hold; see Motion planning, Attack, Control):
- Where the two met: this README, the scenarios' seeds and docstrings (both tracks had moved the same ones, each for its reasons, kept; the seeds re-chosen at the end, below), `stance.py` (the jump back's span and the snail constants), the presets (`game-drops` and `game-air` are `game` with the arrows swallowed too, as `game` is now: `Realism.lock_arrows`), `Realism` (the drops and the arrow lock), the ledger test's bar (both had moved it to 0.5, each for its own reason), and the probe's fights: the live fixes replaced `_fight_off` by `_keep_clear` (clearing her platform first, never throwing with one of hers in her way, `GIVE_WAY`), and the play track had that fight stop finishing wounds; both of `probe._fight`'s fights, off one close and the clears, now pass `finish_wounds=False` (`tests/test_fight.py`). The probes keep the play track's definitions (step-down and hanging-wall throwing past where the v83 line lets a star down, 40 throws; step-up's question) with the live fixes' crowds and clearing, and `probe score` fits `SHOT_H` by the line in force, which is the v83 line now by default.
- **The route's takeoffs and the chains' agree.** The play track places each route jump's takeoff for room (`route._placed`); the live fixes take a chained jump off only where it lands with room from where she really leaves the ground (`Flow.sure_takeoff`). Flow takes every one of the 55 route jumps off from its own takeoff, standing at its spot or running in, and from the 10 units before it that its rng picks on all but 4: jumps down from along a platform (10 -> 15, 13 -> 20, 18 -> 32, 19 -> 24), where a slower takeoff from further back comes down on her own platform's end, so no takeoff lands with room from all of the spread and `_placed` took the best under the slop alone; there it keeps to the route's. The live fixes' margins check (the takeoff a chain really uses lands 9 in, and from 8 units early or 6% slower): all 55 pass, the least room 16.5 units (9.2 over master's 81). `tests/test_flow.py` pins both. The mover's shortest run-up, 15 units since the jump sweeps (`MIN_RUN`), is inside the route's 20 (`RUN_UP`).
- **Two of the night's fixes no longer show at the placed takeoffs**, which absorb what they fixed there: after the climb 36 -> 32 the jump onto 29 takes off 15 units from 32's left end, so there's no step back to press too early, and the jump 16 -> 14 from 15 in lands after a start 0.12 s late (the first arrow held back only to 0.6 s after a throw's key: all 7 of seeds 1-8 that kill the pig). Their tests run at the takeoffs they were found with now (`tests/conftest.py`'s `old_route_takeoffs`, master's 81 jumps exactly), where without the fix she falls short as in game; the hold-back and the step's timing stay for every other plan. A step after a climb is pinned where the placed route has one: the climb 7 -> 1 and the jump off 1 onto 0 (a step back left, held 234 ms, its arrow at the planned standing time). But the run that replaces the step is pressed before the top with only 15 units of room before the end (`route.TAKEOFF_EDGE`), so the climb's predicted top has to be right, and from under a rope it wasn't (the review's fixes, below).
- **The ropes on the game's steps:** `nav_rope_past_end`, `nav_basin_up_right_side`, `move_rope_hops` (both hops, onto 17) and `nav_off_rope_no_top` show what they're there for. A rope jump's jump key, 40 ms after its arrow, comes a step of the game after it or later (`mover.ROPE_JUMP_KEY_MS`), and no plan presses an arrow inside an attack's lock (her rope jumps come after a climb, and `hold_back` holds every plan's first arrow), so the simulator swallows none. The four have no monsters, so no attack's lock comes into them (their world has `lock_arrows` off); with `lock_arrows` on in every one of the 86 scenarios, seeds 1-4 (344 runs), it swallows none either.
- Seeds re-chosen where the merged code no longer showed what a scenario is there for, each docstring saying why: `nav_fight_up` 2 -> 1 (38 of seeds 1-48 arrive; 37 since the review's fixes), `nav_fight_up_runs_out` 1 -> 2, `nav_monsters_staircase` 6 -> 24, `nav_monsters_long_way` 5 -> 15, `tour_hunt_basin` 1 -> 3.
- Golden: 86 traces, master's 76 and the play track's 10, all meeting their expects. Against master's, 32 the same and 44 changed, each first at one of the play track's changes (the placed takeoffs and the rope moves in the trips without monsters; finishing, the v83 line, snails from the ground, piles on the way and the whole-screen look after far drops in the fights, tours and hunt loops; step-up's wording); against the play track's 83, 5 the same and 78 changed (the steps change every move). ## Where the time goes

Profiled offline on 2026-10-20 (no game, no keys): perception with `bench-look` on the 17 saved frames, the continuous feed's step on them, and the hunting stack in the simulator's hunt loop and tour (`python -m gamelab.sim.latency`, with cProfile on top): planning routes and chains, `stance` and `decide`, placing monsters, the ledger and the drop store, telemetry. What stood out, and what was done about it, each giving the same answers as the code before (the checks are named with each) and timed back to back with it (the stack's rows re-measured after the review, the planner's two speedups undone and the C off for "before": the first table had 3.6 s for all of the hunt loop's computing, the sleeps' and searches' share alone, and a different run's percentiles):

| | before | now |
|---|---|---|
| A look placing her (`Senses.look` without monsters; offline, the grab comes on top) | 17.0 ms | 12.9 |
| ... with the monsters in her band (a fight's look) | 48.4 | 31.8 |
| ... and the drops on her platform (the hunt loop's) | 62.4 | 43.0 |
| ... the whole screen and every drop in view | 237.1 | 162.9 |
| The feed's step: her place, the monsters followed re-found | 17.5 | 14.8 |
| ... with her band searched; with the whole screen | 50.0; 147.9 | 33.9; 91.8 |
| The feed's computing for a second of frames at 30 a second, all threads (`--feed`; `watch`, which also looks for drops) | 1.33; 1.50 cores | 1.02; 1.21 |
| The hunt loop's computing before a key plan went out (simulator, 5 seeds of 10 minutes, 5,425 looks): 99th percentile; longest | 53.7; 58.6 ms | 2.6; 3.9 |
| ... before a sleep or a search: 99th percentile; longest | 30.2; 86.4 | 1.9; 6.1 |
| ... all of it, between every two calls to the game (of which before the sleeps and searches) | 31.4 s (20.5) | 5.0 s (3.5) |
| The tour's before a key plan (3,360 looks): 99th percentile; longest | 1.4; 50.9 ms | 1.3; 4.0 |
| ... before a look, longest (planning its sweep) | 312 | 36 |
| Building the map's moves (every hunting command does, as it starts; the engine's flights, since the merge below) | 3.29 s | 0.071 (0.18 since its jumps' takeoffs are placed for room: 5,726 landings; 0.25 with the ropes in mid-air: 7,973) |
| One flight (`physics.flight`, the engine's, not cached) | 0.85-0.95 ms | 0.019 |

With the physics and attack tracks (the perception track rebased onto them, 2026-10-20: her flight by the engine, `body.fly`, and the monsters at the game data's speeds), the stack's rows again (`sim.latency`, preset `game`, 5 seeds of 10 minutes, the C on and off, the same hunts either way): the hunt loop (6,094 looks) before a key plan p99 2.7 ms, at most 4.6 (5.3 with the C off), before a sleep or a search 2.3 and 3.5 (4.3), all of it 6.0 s (of which the sleeps' and searches' 4.4); the tour (3,621 looks) before a key plan p99 2.2, at most 6.1, where with the C off it was 5.5 and 164: chains planned on the move fly landings the planner hasn't, 1 ms each in Python. Before a look at most 43 ms either way (planning its sweep).

What each was (medians in ms; bench-look's 3 rounds of 3 looks at each frame, alternating with the code before):
- **The hunt loop's choices.** After most looks, its rate rule and the mini-map's 'hunt' record worked out where she'd stand to shoot every other hunting ground (3.6 ms a ground) and a route search to each (0.7 ms each): 30-60 ms between a look and her next keys. The spots depend on the map (and on the wall switch probes use) and are kept; the route times come from one search to every platform (1.6 ms), kept for the last 256 places (Motion planning, Behavior).
- **Ropes** (8.6 -> 4.3): each column was split viewed in place, and the shapes made over the whole column (Perception).
- **Monsters** (her band 34.1 -> 22.5, the whole screen 127.9 -> 78.1): on 6 threads the silhouette matches alone took 12 ms of the band; numpy's work after each match held Python's lock, so the threads took turns at it, 18 ms more. It's in C now (below). Every band of the whole screen's matches on the pool together, instead of a band a thread, was tried and not kept: no better (101 against 105 ms with the CPU busy).
- **Drops** (her platform 8.5 -> 6.0, every platform in view 88.9 -> 53.8, on the 30 saved screenshots that place her): strip by strip, each strip waited for its slowest template; now every strip's templates go to 8 threads together.
- **Her flight** (0.85-0.95 -> 0.019 ms): the engine's (`body.fly`), stepped 5 ms at a time in Python; the planner flies thousands (3,128 different landings) to build the map's moves, and a chain planned on the move could try dozens it hadn't before. In C now (below).
- What's left of the stack's own computing is small: in the simulator (with cProfile's overhead) planning a route 0.5 ms (median; at most 11), a chain 0.4 (at most 4), placing a monster seen 0.09, a look's placing 0.1, `stance` 0.04, the ledger's and drop store's bookkeeping after a look 0.01-0.02 (at most 0.9), a telemetry record 0.007.

**Python or C.** Two pieces are in C, `gamelab/native.c`, called through ctypes (which lets go of Python's lock for the call); `gamelab/native.py` builds it with MSVC 2022's Build Tools the first time it's needed, into `gamelab/build/` (git-ignored, named for the source's hash; `python -m gamelab.native` builds it now), and without the compiler, or with `native.USE` off, they're done in Python as before: the same answers, slower. The two are the monster search's step-1 bookkeeping and her flight: loops that numpy and OpenCV can't do without Python's own work, the first between OpenCV's calls, the second a simulation step by step. Each does the Python's arithmetic in the Python's order (IEEE double and float, `/fp:precise`, no fused multiply-adds), so the answers are the same to the bit: on all 31 saved 1920×1080 screenshots and 2,000 random inputs for the search, on 172,000 random flights (platform, x, time and speed at landing), and in tests that compare them with and without the C. The golden traces and the harness's runs (the loop and the tour, 20 seeds each, with blocking looks and with the feed) came out exactly as before; since the merge, the flight in C is the engine's (`body.fly`), and all 73 golden traces come out the same with the C off, as do `sim.latency`'s hunts. The elevated key player never loads it. Left as they are, with the numbers why:
- the silhouette matches (OpenCV, 0.48 ms each on one core, 79 for a band, on the threads): a direct loop in C wouldn't be faster than OpenCV's;
- the masked colour checks (30 a band, 0.39 ms each, OpenCV letting go of the lock: about 5 ms of the band's 22): in C at best 4 ms faster, and not OpenCV's scores to the bit (its masked normalised match goes through Fourier transforms), so a monster scoring near the 0.6 threshold could come out differently;
- her name tag (3.5 ms near where she was; the whole play area every 30th look, 36 ms): OpenCV's match takes as long on 1 thread as on 12, and matched in pieces its scores differ by up to 1e-4;
- the Lie Detector check (the player's, 67 ms 4 times a second): the elevated player loads no DLL, and as little as it can;
- the GPU (onnxruntime with CUDA is installed, the onnx package isn't): one batched convolution could take the silhouette matches off the CPU, about 0.4 of the feed's 1.0 cores. Not built: its scores would differ from OpenCV's by about 1e-5, so some spots could change places; the model would have to be written by hand; and nothing shows yet that the CPU the feed takes costs the game frames (it runs at below-normal priority). If it does (`check` on a recording made with `watch` running), the cheaper knobs are the feed's schedule (`worldstate`): her band 5 times a second, the whole screen once a second, and for `watch` the drops on her platform every 0.5 s and in view every 2 s.

## Memory and disk

A hunt runs for hours, and perception is to run all the time, so what the stack keeps has to stay bounded.

- **Monster speeds:** `Tracker.speeds` (what `nav` sums up as the speeds seen) keeps the latest 400 of each kind (`SPEED_SAMPLES` in `localization/monsters.py`). Kept all, one a monster a look, a look every frame would add about 110,000 an hour.
- **The drop store** (`behavior/ledger.py`) drops a pile 5 minutes after looks have seen it gone (`GONE_KEEP_S`); kept for ever, every kill added one, and every look went through all of them. The hunt loop's count of the piles she went for that were seen gone (its progress, for the stuck guard) is kept apart (`DropStore.gone_tried`), so it decides exactly as before. In the simulator's hunt loop (preset `game`, seeds 0-2, 20 minutes each) the store ended with 77, 82 and 112 piles instead of 280, 257 and 338 (at most 90-123 at a time), with the same EXP and the same counts after every look. Piles never seen gone, on platforms she didn't look at again, stay: 6-20 at the end of those runs.
- **Telemetry** (`gamelab/telemetry.py`): live hunting wrote 72 KB a minute (4.2 MB an hour; the looks' `scene` records the most of it, 21 KB a minute at 257 bytes each), and a feed of 10 looks a second would write 10-15 MB an hour, in one file per run that was never deleted. A run's file now goes on in a new part past 64 MB (`MAX_BYTES`): a file of its own, named for the time the part starts and always later than the part before, so it sorts after it; its first record is the run's session record with `continues` (the part before's file name) and `part` (2, 3, ...). The part being written is the file written to last, so the mini-map window and the web page (`telemetry.newest`, by last write) go on to it, the rest of the part before read first, without starting over (`ui.sources.LiveSource`: a file whose first record `continues` the one followed isn't a new run). They had started over at each part, as at a new run, and lost what a run sends once: the tour's and the hunt loop's safe platforms and grounds, coloured on the page, with her trail and the log. On NTFS the last write os.scandir reads is the directory's copy, brought up to date only when a file is opened or closed: a part still being written that nothing else opens shows as written when it was made, 0 bytes long. A tour that finished after `watch` began so stayed the newest until `watch`'s part closed, hours later, the page frozen on it. `newest` asks os.stat of the files the directory says were written in the last 7 days (`RECENT_S`; a part is open for 15 hours at most, hunting), and `prune` of every file, which had counted such a part as empty. When a run starts, and again each time a run goes on in a new part, the oldest telemetry files are deleted until the rest take at most 2 GB (`KEEP_BYTES`; `prune`, never the part being written); it says so when it deletes any. None is deleted for its age (`KEEP_DAYS` is None; a number of days would delete files last written longer ago too): live telemetry is evidence that later fits go back to (the terrain and geometry fits of 2026-10-20 were made from the live runs of 2026-10-19 and 20), and small old files cost next to no disk (the first two days of live runs were 38 files and 2.9 MB). The size cap alone bounds the disk. Pruning only at the start had let a run left going for days, such as `watch`, grow the folder without bound. A file another run is still writing can't be deleted on Windows and is left.
- **The player's log** (`lab/player.log`): about 40 bytes a plan, some 150 KB an hour of hunting, never cut. When the player starts, a log past 5 MB is moved to `player.log.1` (the one before goes) and a new one begins (`LOG_MAX_BYTES` in `control/player.py`; the standard library only, so the elevated player loads nothing more).
- **Cancel files** (`lab/inbox/<plan id>.cancel`): a plan cut short as it ended left its cancel file for good (25 had piled up, the player deleting them only when it starts). Now no cancel file is made for a plan that's over (the player renames a plan to `.running` when it takes it and deletes that only once its keys are let go of), and reading a plan's result deletes its cancel file (`control/client.py`).
- **What's there:** `python -m gamelab lab-usage` sizes each kind. On 2026-10-20 the main `gamelab/lab` held 1.5 GB: recordings 1.2 GB (64, made only on request: `--record`, `combo`, `calibrate`), screenshots 277 MB (255), golden traces 2.2 MB, telemetry 2.8 MB (38 files), harness runs 0.5 MB, the rest 0.4 MB. Recordings and screenshots are measurement evidence and are never deleted for you.
- **Soak runs** (`python -m gamelab.sim.soak frames|sim [--minutes 10] [--no-trace]`; minutes long, so not in pytest): the stack kept going offline while Python's allocations (tracemalloc) and the process's private bytes are sampled every 10 s; after a warm-up (the first fifth) it gives the growth an hour on the samples' line and what grew, by line and by part of the code. Telemetry goes to a temp folder in parts of 1 MB, so rotation runs too. Measured on 2026-10-20, other work sharing the CPU:
  - `frames`, 10 minutes: 5,900 real looks at the 17 saved frames (each frame 30 looks in a row; every 5th look with the drops on her platform, every 30th the whole screen; after each the hunter's listeners, the monster tracker, a glance and telemetry). Python's memory was 152.8 MB after the warm-up and 153.0 at the end, +0.4 MB an hour on the line. Of the 198 KB the stack gained, 145 KB was one line of the monster search's spot lists, the same after 1 minute as after 10 (freed tuples Python keeps for reuse), and 14 KB the ledger's 151 kills (about 100 bytes each, kept for the whole hunt). The private bytes moved between 230 and 262 MB as the big looks came and went.
  - `frames --no-trace`, 30 minutes: 29,184 looks (16 a second). Private bytes 197-237 MB, the first third's mean 219.0 and the last's 221.9: +8 MB an hour on the line, inside that swing. A run of hours would tell a slow creep in OpenCV or numpy from noise.
  - `sim`, 10 minutes: 5.6 simulated hours of the hunt loop (seed 0, preset `game`; 1,197 kills of hers). The stack grew 100 KB in the last 4.5 hours, the ledger's kills; the simulator's own lists of every plan and log line grew 8.9 MB, which a real run doesn't keep. At the end the drop store held 112 piles (7 not gone), the landing cache 2,721 entries (2,718 once the planner is built), and telemetry had gone through 30 parts.
- **Still kept for the whole run:** the ledger's kills (about 100 bytes each, 200-400 an hour; `hunt.py` counts them by their index), and piles never seen gone.
- **The continuous feed** (see Continuous perception) holds a fixed amount: 4 frames in shared memory, the last 8 states, at most 10,000 events not drained, only monsters seen in the last 10 s. `soak feed` (the real child on the saved frames, the stack's side asking as a fight does, 15 minutes): the stack's side's Python memory 17.7 MB after the warm-up and 17.9 at the end, its private bytes 66.5 and 67.2; the child's private bytes swung between 156 and 197 MB; 6 minutes more after her band's search went by time, 66.5 and 67.3 MB on the stack's side, and the child's last third's mean 175.3 MB against its first's 181.4 (162-196). The step itself under tracemalloc for 8 minutes in one process: Python's memory 2.0 MB throughout, the private bytes' last third 183.9 MB against the first's 193.1 (161-199 as the big searches came and went). `watch` writes about 16 MB of telemetry an hour (see Continuous perception). With the searches on a thread of the child's own (2026-10-21), 15 minutes of the same with the CPU busy with other work: the stack's side's Python memory 11.8 MB after the warm-up and 12.9 at the end (0.9 MB of it the soak's own record of each state's times, kept for its report), its private bytes 58.4 and 63.4; the child's private bytes 180.5 and 182.0 (165.9-186.3; the first third's mean 179.9, the last's 182.1).

## Limits

- **Recalibrate** after moving the game window, changing maps or changing resolution. Her name-tag template is kept between calibrations; `calibrate --fresh` rebuilds it, and should be run where no rope crosses her name.
- **`hunt` needs a 1920×1080 game window,** the size its templates were made at.
- **Each new monster type needs a colour gate** (a body-colour test in `PALETTES` in `perception/mobs.py`) before its game sprites are used. Its templates come from the game data automatically.
- **Her name tag can be hidden** behind screen widgets at the map's edge, like the party HP box. She's then placed on screen from the minimap dot and the camera, which is only good to about 11 map units.
- **Camera correction for recordings needs ground with a pattern** under her, like a girder or tiles. Plain ground can't be tracked.
- **Swing detection is tuned to the claw's white/lavender swing effect.** Other weapons or skills may need `SWING_MIN_PIXELS` in `analysis/track.py` adjusted.
- **Lucky Seven costs about 7 MP per cast.** A 32-shot test uses about 220 MP. Use `--regular-attack` for long runs. Hunting uses the regular attack unless `--skill` is given.
- **How far above or below a star reaches** beyond 60 units isn't measured. `hunt shots` sums up the shot log.
- **Other players** hunt this map too. Platforms are often empty, and stops then end with no kills. Their minimap dots are seen (Other players), but only inside `MINIMAP_BOX` (300 px wide: this map's minimap; Kerning City's runs past it), and two within 4 game px (46 map units) of each other read as one. Nothing yet knows how much of a ground they take, so the hunt loop doesn't steer clear of them (`hunt.PLAYER_DISCOUNT` off), nor leave the piles by them (`hunt.PLAYER_PILES` off). Their dots are followed from look to look; which marker the game draws over which (hers or theirs) isn't known.
- **EXP from anything else** isn't a kill when no kill of the map's monsters gives that much (Her EXP number), but a share of 2, 4, 6 or 8 is taken for snails, and one that adds up to kills for those kills. Kinds that give the same EXP can't be told apart by it. The EXP table (meowdb.com's) is checked at levels 19 and 20 only. At another EXP rate (an event, a coupon) the sizes go off after 8 kills and each change is one kill, kinds not named, until `gains.EXP_RATE` is set to it. A kill in the very look the cursor comes over the number or leaves it is taken with it (its EXP counts, the kill not).
- **Loot templates cover this map's drops only** (`ITEMS` in `perception/loot.py`); another map needs its drops' names added. Equipment, scrolls, and bills and sacks of mesos aren't looked for; she still picks them up when she walks over them. Nor do they have a price in the hunt loop's table (`looting.PRICES`): a kind the detector named that isn't there would count as worth the dearest drop here.
- **The full-inventory message** is "You can't get anymore items." It's in the client, not the data files, so `loot.py` matches that and the usual other wordings. Without the OCR packages, only the 3-in-a-row rule tells.
- **The simulator is a model:** its timings and physics are fitted to gamelab's own measurements, its monsters are pushed back by a hit as far as one recording measured and never leave their platform, and its perception is exact unless a scenario adds noise. Use it to check that a change does what was meant, then confirm in game.
